# Firmware feature notes

Design, measurements and user decisions per feature, in build order. Hardware facts and toolchain: [`llm.md`](llm.md). How to build and flash: [`workflow.md`](workflow.md).

Issue numbers written as #N or cinder#N refer to the original private repository; Ember#N refers to the public [Ember](https://github.com/tarakanof/Ember) repository.

## Bot face (step 2) and rendering performance (measured 2026-10-03)

Step 2 done: `components/bot/` (pure C: `bot_behavior` = port of Ember's BotBehavior.swift, `bot_shape` = body ring + eye strokes, `bot_raster` = AA stroke rasteriser), host tests in `firmware/test/host/run.sh` (blink rates, eye swap behind blink, sleepy at 300 s, triangle geometry, rasteriser == per-pixel reference on 1500 cases). View: `main/bot_view.c`. Demo inputs: turn = look, push/touch = hop, long hold = next mood.

What made it smooth (refresh ms, before → after): 3-layer glow lines 250 → eyes-only 8-10; hop 250 → ~40; happy eyes draw 46 → 13.
- **Build `-O2`** (`CONFIG_COMPILER_OPTIMIZATION_PERF`); IDF's default `-Og` made loops ~2x slower.
- **Local BSP copy** `components/bsp_knob` (Apache-2.0): 2 draw buffers in internal DMA RAM (2×472×60×2 B) instead of 1 in PSRAM.
- **LVGL refresh 16 ms** (`CONFIG_LV_DEF_REFR_PERIOD`), frame timer 16 ms.
- **Own rasteriser**, not lv_line/borders: LVGL's wide AA round lines are very slow; a full-face circle-border object added 4-8 ms to every eye refresh. Eyes = RGB565A8 canvases (alpha so the outline shows through), span-based (exact capsule interval per row, distance only on the AA fringe).
- **Outline = one 448×448 opaque RGB565 image**, rendered on mood change only (on the core-1 `rim` task and swapped behind the blink since 0.9.74, #115; before, ~120 ms on the LVGL task), moved as a whole for the hop. Dead ends: tiles (48 px or 16 px). Each small area is a separate QSPI transfer (160 tiles: 500 ms/frame), and LVGL falls back to full-screen redraw beyond 32 invalid areas (`LV_INV_BUF_SIZE`).
- **Never log from the LVGL task**: USB-Serial/JTAG console writes can stall without a reader; stats log runs in its own task.
- LVGL task stack is 8 KB (BSP default): keep big scratch arrays static (one overflow crash seen). `tri_build`'s 5.8 KB table scratch was the last such local until 0.9.32 ("Task stacks and internal RAM").
- `idf.py -DCMAKE_C_FLAGS=...` is cached in `build/toolchain/cflags` and silently persists; use source defines or `fullclean`.
- **Outline squash**: 11 pre-rendered variants (5 squash, base, 5 stretch, ~4.8 MB PSRAM) drawn by a core-1 task after each mood change (~1.3 s); the LVGL task is pinned to core 0 (BSP copy). Hop frames ~40-60 ms. LVGL image scale transform was ~160 ms/frame. Redrawing only the ring as 20 slices or 4 bands was no faster (~3 ms fixed cost per area) and **broke the ring at the seams** (areas reach the panel at different times), so the outline is invalidated as one area.
- **Hop tuned for the knob** (user): 1.0 s instead of 0.62 s, squash × 0.75 (`BOT_HOP_DURATION_S`, `BOT_HOP_SQUASH`).
- **No tear sync possible (re-checked 2026-10-05, #42)**: with TEON now sent with its parameter byte (the init table sent 0 bytes; RDDSM 0x0E reads 0x80 = TE on), no free GPIO (7, 15, 16, 18, 21, 38-42, 45-48) toggles: all follow their pull-up/pull-down, so TE is not routed to the ESP. Panel reads work over a 3-wire half-duplex SPI device on SIO0 (GPIO13) with CS (GPIO12) driven by hand at 5 MHz (0x0A → 0x9C, 0x0C → 0x55, 0xDA → 0x53, 0x52 → brightness), but GSL 0x45 always reads 0, so no scanline either. Earlier note: the schematic's "TE" on GPIO38 floats on this unit (TE waits always time out, idle level random across boots), and the adapter's TE mode needs a 440 KB full-frame buffer that only PSRAM can hold, which QSPI DMA can't send (ESP_ERR_NO_MEM). Some tearing during the hop remains; smaller per-frame motion reduces it.
- Free memory at runtime (bot face): internal ~125 KB (largest block 54 KB), PSRAM ~3 MB before the 11 variants. Wi-Fi will need internal RAM: don't grow the DMA draw buffers.
- Eye raster history: per-pixel distance cost up to 46 ms per frame for the happy eyes; the span raster solves each segment's capsule x-interval per row (outer radius = any coverage, inner = full) and computes distance only on the 1-2 px fringe. libm `atan2f` dominated the glint raster on the S3 (hence the polynomial, max error ~0.06 deg).
- **Happy eyes are a 16-point arc** (0.9.64, #103, user: "made from straight lines"): 7 points left 6 visible straight capsules (up to 1.0 px off the curve at rest); 16 points keep it under 0.25 px (host test). Raster cost on host (-O2, 128×128 box) +29-42 % (19.7 → 25.4 µs per eye); S3 refresh log: to do.

### Micro-saccades, outline off glance leans, badge and error fixes (#111, review P1, P5, P8, 0.9.68)

The first batch of [`bot-review.md`](bot-review.md) (user decision: P5 + P1 + P8 first, as the review's suggested order), matched to the BotNext simulation (`design/faces/canvas/BotNext.dc.html`).

- **P1, micro-saccades** (`bot_behavior.c` `start_micro`, `start_saccade_to(..., SAC_MICRO, ...)`): every saccade records its target (`fix_x`, `fix_y`) and schedules the first micro-saccade at the end of the move + max(0.6 s, gap). The gap is lognormal(0.8 s, σ 0.4, 0.3-2.5 s), sleepy lognormal(2 s, σ 0.4, 0.8-5 s). Amplitude 0.012-0.025 gaze (sleepy half), direction toward the fixation target ± 1 rad when the eyes are more than 0.015 off it, else random, so the gaze stays near it (under 0.06 in the test). 25 ms `EASE_IN_OUT`, no lean, no blink coupling, `next_saccade_at` and the fixation target unchanged. One due within 50 ms of the next saccade is pushed past it. None while acting (weather moments, whose cues own the gaze), while tracking (full chase, half chase following) or while `bot_hold_gaze` holds (half chase between follows, which now holds micro-saccades too); `bot_track_end` makes the release point the target. No new pose field: they move `gaze_x/y`, which `bot_pose_same` already quantises.
- **P5, outline dy from the hop only**: the pose gains `hop_dy` (the hop's lift, also in `offset_y`, joins `bot_pose_same`), and `bot_rim_dy(p, r, hop_scale)` turns it into the outline's offset: the nearest even pixel, symmetric about 0 (`2·floor(|x|/2 + 0.5)` with the sign of x). Before, `lround(...) & ~1` took the lean too and rounded −0.6 px to −2 and +0.6 to 0, so upward glances moved the whole 448 px outline by 2 px. The eyes keep the lean (`to_screen` still uses `offset_y`). The badge and the label follow the outline offset as before. Since then a weather act's kept lean (see Bot weather moments) only matters for the eyes.
- **P8, badge**: `s_badge_gap` and `s_badge_dot` are created before the eye canvases, so the eyes draw over the black gap instead of being bitten by it; sizes unchanged (gap 0.3 r, dot 0.22 r), as in BotNext. Done's rest gaze moves from (0.67, 0.77) to (0.5, 0.6) (± 0.08, 0.06). Waiting has no rest target (viewer or the sides), in BotNext too, so it is unchanged.
- **P8, error**: the label canvas leaves out the tool icon in error (`bot_label_mark`: the Clawd and Codex marks and T3's pixel icon); the text stays below the triangle as a name plate. The label now re-renders on a mood change (`s_label_mood`), not only on a colour change.
- **Tests** (`test_bot.c`): `test_micro_saccades` (4 idle seeds over 280 s: count, amplitude, 25 ms, none in a fixation's first 0.6 s or within 50 ms of the next saccade, no lean or blink, gaps 0.3-2.5 s with a median near 0.8 s, mean cosine toward the target > 0.6, drift < 0.06; sleepy half amplitude and 2 s median; every other mood; none while tracking, held by a half chase or acting), `test_outline_dy` (rounding table, mirror symmetry and evenness from −14 to 14 px, no outline move off a hop in any mood with knob glances while the lean still reaches ≥ 1.5 px), `test_badged_rest` (done targets only the new rest or the viewer; the label mark except in error), and `hop_dy` in `test_pose_same`. Against the 0.9.67 behaviour (the old `bot_behavior.c` with the two new functions stubbed the old way) all of them fail. `test_moment.c` models the outline offset with `bot_rim_dy`.
- **Cost** (`idf.py size`, origin/main 0.9.67 at 3c39425 → this branch, same machine, IDF 5.5.5): image 1,702,191 → 1,703,687 B (+1,496, flash `.text`); DIRAM 102,303 → 102,327 B (`.bss` +24, three doubles in `bot_t`); PSRAM `.bss` unchanged. `stack_walk.py` `lvgl` 8,608 (margin 1,888) unchanged. Simulated (BotNext, review table): +2-4 eye redraws/s, eye areas only, steps ≤ 3 px; outline moves off hops 10.8 → 0/min in idle, 10.4 → 0 in done, 36.6 → 0 in error.
- **On the knob**: 0.9.67 baseline from Ember's full diagnostics, a 1 h window on 2026-10-12 (58 one-minute points, mixed pages and moods, so an aggregate, not per mood). 0.9.68 the same way, 2026-10-12 22:39-23:35 UTC (56 points after the first 5 min of uptime). The two windows mix pages differently (0.9.67: 47 of 58 points at 11-17 fps; 0.9.68: 27 of 56, plus two steady ~26 fps / ~28 % core 0 stretches and a few spikes, likely the maintainer's Devices › Demo checks), so the aggregate shift is mostly page mix. At matched 11-17 fps: core 0 18.3 → 19.2 %, frame avg 5.98 → 6.00 ms, frame max median 12 → 14 ms, consistent with the predicted +2-4 eye redraws/s. Internal free rose ~0.7 KB (different heap state after the update reboot), not a saving. Confirmed by the maintainer on the knob with the demo mood scenes (idle, done, waiting, error): micro-saccades in holds, no ring jump on glances, the badge gap no longer biting the right eye, and error's label without the mark. Invalidated areas are not measured yet: the 30 s stats log needs USB and has no per-second area count (#118).

  | | 0.9.67 (aggregate, 1 h) | 0.9.68 (aggregate, 1 h) |
  |---|---|---|
  | Core 0 load | median 18 % (14–27) | median 19.2 % (13.7–50.8) |
  | Core 1 load | median 8 % (6.8–13.3) | median 8.3 % (6.9–35.5) |
  | Render fps | median 12.9 (6.6–26) | median 13.4 (4.2–34.7) |
  | Frame avg | 6.0 ms (5.75–8.0) | 6.3 ms (5.74–11.3) |
  | Frame max | median 12.5 ms (11–52) | median 16 ms (12–85) |
  | Internal free | 84.6 KB (84.2–84.8) | 85.3 KB (84.2–86.2) |

### Breathing eyes, heavy sleepy blinks and an asleep state (#112, review P2, 0.9.69)

The second batch of [`bot-review.md`](bot-review.md), matched to BotNext (`design/faces/canvas/BotNext.dc.html`, its P2 behaviour and constants).

- **Breath** (`bot_behavior.c` `breathe`): the pose gains `breath` in [−1, 1], the sine of a phase that advances by dt / period on every `bot_pose`. Each cycle draws its period at the wrap: idle 4.2 s, working 3.2, waiting 2.6, done 3.6, sleepy 5.5, error 3.0 (BotNext's value), asleep 6.0, each × 0.85-1.15. A mood change keeps the phase; the new period starts at the next cycle, as in BotNext. `bot_eye_strokes` multiplies every eye's height by 1 + 0.025·breath and moves its centre by `BOT_BREATH_REACH`·breath (0.006 r, about 1.2 px); never through `scale_x/y`, so no outline variant and no ring move.
- **Sleepy**: the lid is 0.80 + 0.12·breath (times the slump while it eases in). The 2.8 s blinks at 2.2× length are gone: sleepy blinks are heavy (`blink_heavy`), every lognormal(7 s, σ 0.4, 3-15 s), closed for 0.4-1.2 s (`blink_hold`), a 0.25 s close and a 0.3 s `EASE_OUT_BACK` reopen that opens briefly past the breathing lid; no double blinks. A mood change during a heavy blink starts the usual 1.6× mood blink, already shut in both eyes when the heavy blink was at least half closed (no open frame). A weather act's costume blink in sleepy is not heavy: a 2.2× blink, as in 0.9.68. Micro-saccades stay as 0.9.68 has them (half amplitude, 2 s median), as in BotNext. Until 0.9.77 the device morphed the capsule for the lid, so a sleepy eye at lid 0.68-0.92 was a short horizontal capsule about 50 × 19-36 px; since then it is BotNext's clipped dash (#116, P10, below).
- **Asleep** (`asleep`, `asleep_at`, `sleep_lid`): `BOT_ASLEEP_AFTER_S` (600 s) of awake sleepy time, the bot falls asleep: the lid eases to 1 over 1.2 s (`EASE_IN_OUT`, from the current breathing lid instead of BotNext's fixed 0.80, so there is no jump), the gaze glides to (0, −0.35), and saccades, micro-saccades and blinks stop. Breath becomes a bob: centre ± `BOT_BREATH_BOB` (0.0077 r, 1.5 px), height unchanged, a 6 s period. The pose carries `asleep`; `bot_rim_tone(mood, asleep)` keys the outline, so the view re-renders it once, at `bot_rim_alpha` 25 % (55 % idle and sleepy, 100 % otherwise), and once more on waking. A push that wakes it hops at once while the squash variants are still re-rendering on core 1 (about 1 s), so that hop shows the base outline unsquashed, as a mood change's pop already does. Leaving sleepy without a heavy blink past half closed showed the lid at 0.55 for a frame before the 1.6× mood blink closed it (0.78-0.92 → 0.55; 0.9.68 had 0.55 → 0.55, BotNext the same drop). Since 0.9.76 (#121) the mood blink starts part way closed, at the more closed eye's lid (the heavy lid and any heavy blink, `heavy_lid`), backdated through the right eye's lag as the shut case is, so no lid drops before it closes.
- **Waking**: any mood change except idle (as for sleepy) wakes it; the mood blink then starts shut in both eyes (backdated through the right eye's lag), so no open frame flashes first. Input wakes it to awake sleepy (`rouse`, the maintainer's brief for #112: it wakes on input as sleepy does): a knob turn (`bot_look`), a push (`bot_push`), a chase (`bot_track`, `bot_look_still`) and a demo scene's act (`bot_act_begin`, since closed eyes cannot act). The lid eases from 1 back to the breathing lid over 0.6 s, saccades resume after it, and the bot falls asleep again 10 min later. Input and a chase (every `bot_track` frame) also restart an awake sleepy bot's full 10 min. A weather moment's act pauses the countdown instead (maintainer decision on review): `bot_act_begin` keeps the time left (`sleep_left`) and `bot_act_end`/`bot_act_drop` resume it, so a sleepy bot with frequent moments still falls asleep after 10 min of awake time between them. A knob turn or push during an act still neither looks nor hops, but since 0.9.76 (#121) it sets the paused time left back to the full 10 min, so input mid-act counts as input; before, a bot with 1 s left fell asleep 1 s after the act. With the deadline at infinity during an act and pushed by every chase frame, `bot_pose` needs no acting or tracking guard. BotNext has no input, so this part is ours.
- **Weather moments while asleep** (maintainer decision on review): automatic moments do not wake the bot. `moment_tick` passes `.shown = bot_page && !s_bot.asleep`, so the scheduler treats asleep like a page that is not shown: no moment fires, and the periodic slot keeps moving forward. After waking, the first periodic moment waits its full interval instead of firing on the waking frame; `.blocked` alone would have fired the overdue glance at once. A weather change seen in the last 30 min of sleep (`WXM_WAIT_S`) is still pending, though: it fires `WXM_SETTLE_S` (3 s) after waking, since the `shown` flip resets `steady_at`. Demo moment scenes still play.
- **`bot_pose_same`**: `breath` joins, quantised by the eye centre's movement (`step / BOT_BREATH_REACH`, asleep `/ BOT_BREATH_BOB`), and `asleep` must match. With the ×1.025 height the eye edges move about twice the centre, so a breath redraw moves an edge about 1 px (review probe: dash 1.12, round 1.25, angry 1.02, happy 0.69 px); a 0.5 px edge step would need 0.012 and about +2 redraws/s, against the ~2 redraws/s the review budgets for breathing. The lids are now quantised by their eye movement too (`step / BOT_LID_REACH`, 0.2 r per lid unit, an eye tip's travel; 0.4 r, the lid line's travel, since 0.9.77): in lid units the breathing sleepy lid crossed the half-pixel step about every frame (0.137 lid/s against 0.0026 per step). Blinks still redraw on every frame they move.
- **Demo**: act `asleep` (Ember's scene `bot.asleep`: the idle view + act `asleep`) parses to `DEMO_ACT_ASLEEP`; like `sleepy` it has no natural end, so the scene plays 8 s. `page_tick` calls `bot_sleep` (sleepy and asleep at once, the lid closing from open) while it plays. Every scene pauses a sleepy bot's countdown: `scene_start` records `bot_asleep_in` (0 asleep, else the time left, a paused act's included), then `bot_sleep_in(INFINITY)` for an awake sleepy bot, which also stirs an asleep one for a `sleepy` scene, so nothing falls asleep mid-scene. `scene_end` puts the state back with `bot_sleep_in`: asleep again, or awake with the time left, also after a moment or chase scene that woke it. A `sleepy` or `asleep` scene on a bot that was not sleepy ends with `bot_wake`.
- **Tests** (`test_bot.c`): `test_breath` (each mood over 580 s: period range ± 15 %, mean within 3 %, jitter present, range ±1; asleep 6 s; scale stays 1), `test_breath_eyes` (height × 1.025 and centre ± 0.006 for dash, round and angry, happy breathes; asleep 1.5 px bob with the height unchanged), `test_breath_redraws` (breath adds 1-3 redraws/s in idle, working and done; sleepy under 16/s; asleep 1-2.5/s), `test_sleepy_lid`, `test_heavy_blinks` (heavy, 0.4-1.2 s shut, rate and 7 s median, the overshoot), `test_asleep` (10 min, lids shut and gaze still for an hour, one outline re-render to 25 %, the alpha table), `test_wake_from_asleep` (mood with both eyes shut at once, idle, `bot_wake`, look, push, act, chase, lids easing open, asleep again 10 min later, `bot_sleep`, `bot_sleep_in` including the infinite pause, `bot_asleep_in`), `test_asleep_guards` (an act pauses and resumes the countdown, also with a 20 s moment every minute, a second end resumes nothing; a chase restarts the full 10 min; only sleepy falls asleep, other moods 20 min; costume blinks not heavy, 2.2×; since 0.9.76 a push or turn mid-act gives the full 10 min after the act, none outside sleepy, a nested `bot_act_begin` keeps the first time left, and `enter` and `bot_sleep_in` mid-act replace the paused time left), `test_sleepy_transitions` (a mood change in a heavy hold keeps both eyes shut and drops the heavy blink, the 0.5 shut threshold (one at 0.16 hands over part way), no early blink entering sleepy, also mid-blink, no lid jump falling asleep, `bot_sleep` closing from open; since 0.9.76 192 changes out of sleepy with no lid dropping over 0.02 before the mood blink shuts, 169 before), `test_sleepy_still_redraws` (the breathing lid redraws holds at 4-12/s; a fixed lid gives 2), and `breath`, `asleep` and the pixel-scaled lid in `test_pose_same`. `test_moment.c`: not shown while asleep, the overdue glance waits its full interval after waking. Against the 0.9.68 `bot_behavior.c` and `bot_shape.c` (new functions stubbed the old way) they fail. Mutants from the review (asleep closing from a fixed 0.80, `rouse` opening at once, `bot_track` not waking, no 7 s reschedule on entering sleepy, a heavy blink kept over a mood change, a fixed sleepy lid, no mood guard on falling asleep) each fail at least one test. `test_demo.c` parses `asleep` and times it; `run.sh` checks the view's re-render on `asleep` with `bot_rim_alpha`, the act's wiring in `page_tick`, `scene_start` (the pause is its last step) and `scene_end`, and `moment_tick`'s `.shown` while asleep. Host sim (60 fps, seed 77): idle 11.3 redraws/s (9.7 without breath), working 14.7 (12.7), done 10.5 (8.6), sleepy 11.4, asleep 2.0.
- **Cost** (`idf.py size`, origin/main 0.9.68 at cd354f7 → this branch, same machine, IDF 5.5.5): image 1,703,808 → 1,706,848 B (+3,040); DIRAM 102,327 → 102,471 B (`.bss` +144: the new `bot_t` fields and the scene's time left to sleep); PSRAM `.bss` unchanged. `stack_walk.py`: `lvgl` 8,608 (margin 1,888) unchanged; `orbit` 2,144 → 2,192 (margin 1,184 → 1,136).
- **On the knob**: the same way as 0.9.68 (Ember's full diagnostics, 1 h, points after the first 5 min of uptime), 2026-10-13 10:14-11:13 UTC, 58 points. The windows mix pages and moods differently again: 0.9.68 had 27 of 56 points at 11-17 fps, 0.9.69 only 3 of 58, with 31 at 18-24 fps and 21 at 25-32 fps. Breathing redraws the eyes in every hold, so a bot page no longer drops to the 0.9.68 idle rate, which explains part of the fps and core 0 rise; the host sim predicts +1.6-2 redraws/s per mood, less than the aggregate shift, so the rest is page mix. At matched fps: 18-24 fps core 0 26.8 → 25.8 % (3 → 31 points), 25-32 fps 28.0 → 30.5 % (8 → 21), 11-17 fps 20.6 → 20.2 % (27 → 3); frame avg 6.68 → 6.35 ms and 5.87 → 5.95 ms. Frame max median 16 → 47 ms follows the 18-24 fps band, which had a 48 ms median in 0.9.68 too (3 points); the maximum fell 85 → 54 ms. Internal free unchanged (85.3 → 85.4 KB). Confirmed by the maintainer on the knob with Devices › Demo: Idle, Sleepy and Asleep (Ember 0.57.1). Not measured: per-mood load (the demo holds 60 s at most, one stats point) and invalidated areas (#118).

  | | 0.9.68 (aggregate, 1 h) | 0.9.69 (aggregate, 1 h) |
  |---|---|---|
  | Core 0 load | median 19.2 % (13.7–50.8) | median 27 % (19.2–43.9) |
  | Core 1 load | median 8.3 % (6.9–35.5) | median 12.7 % (9–17) |
  | Render fps | median 13.4 (4.2–34.7) | median 21.7 (12.4–36.2) |
  | Frame avg | 6.3 ms (5.74–11.3) | 6.25 ms (5.86–6.66) |
  | Frame max | median 16 ms (12–85) | median 47 ms (15–54) |
  | Internal free | 85.3 KB (84.2–86.2) | 85.4 KB (85.1–85.5) |
  | Invalidated areas/s | not measurable (no counter before 0.9.72) | per mood on 0.9.72: "Invalidated-area count (#118)", on the knob |

### Reading v2 for working (#113, review P3, 0.9.70)

The third batch of [`bot-review.md`](bot-review.md), matched to BotNext (`design/faces/canvas/BotNext.dc.html`, its `workingTarget`).

- **Lines** (`bot_behavior.c` `read_target`, `rd_line`, `rd_lines`, `rd_steps`, `rd_turn` in `bot_t`): a line is 3-6 forward steps (`rd_steps`, uniform) of +0.18-0.38 from x −0.65 ± 0.03, capped at 0.7; it ends when the steps run out or x reaches 0.7. Right of x −0.35 a step is a regression instead 12 % of the time: x −0.10-0.22, not counted as a step. Line y is −0.05 − 0.06·line (± 0.02 on every line, a new page's line 0 too), lines 0-4; a page's sixth line reads at line 4's y (BotNext lets it reach −0.35 ± 0.02, below the mark's bound). The return sweep to the next line's start (and a new page's first line, also on entering working) holds 1.3 × the usual working fixation (lognormal 0.9 s, so 0.46-2.6 s); 0.9.70 gave it the existing blink coupling (amplitude over 0.8, 60 %); since 0.9.71 a sweep over 0.6 always has a blink and waits for the lids (#127 below). x stays within the old reading range (±0.7).
- **Page turn**: after 4-6 lines (`rd_lines`, uniform) the bot glances up (x ± 0.08, y 0.3) or at the viewer (y 0.1), half each, for 1.2 s, with a blink (since 0.9.71 every turn closes both lids to 0.9, and a turn over 0.6 waits for them, #127 below); the next saccade starts a new page at line 0. Entering working counts as a page turn already made, so its glide goes to line 0's start (BotNext rolls the entry like any saccade).
- **Glances**: each saccade except a new page's first is 7 % at the viewer and 5 % at the Clawd mark (x ± 0.04, y −0.35, 0.6-1.0 s), BotNext's split; 0.9.69 had 10 % at the viewer and no mark. The gaze never goes below y −0.35: the working saccades whose `EASE_OUT_BACK` overshoot (7 % of the move) would cross it, every glance down to the mark among them, ease in-out instead, and a micro-saccade that would cross it flips its vertical step (same amplitude). BotNext overshoots to about −0.38 there.
- **Rate**: the same saccade rate as 0.9.69 (a one-off host run of 20 seeds × 600 s: 0.982 → 0.956/s, −3 %: the longer return holds against shorter steps; `test_reading`'s 8 seeds give 0.959), so the device cost is unchanged or lower (redraws below). RNG: the reading state is drawn only on entering working, so every other mood gives the same poses as 0.9.69 for the same seed. No new pose field: the reading moves `gaze_x/y` and the lean, which `bot_pose_same` already quantises.
- **Quiet weather moments** (ours; BotNext has none): a quiet moment in working shows its sprite box above the eyes and its text line from y 310 px, where the icon steps aside, while the eyes keep reading. Lines 3-4 and the mark would put the eye bottoms at up to 318 px and an upward page turn the eye tops at 152 px, inside both. `bot_read_moment(b, on, t)`, called every frame in `page_tick` with `moment_view_busy()` right after the moment tick and before the pose (also covering demo moment scenes), keeps the reading to lines 0-2 (y ≥ −0.19, the same floor rules), skips the mark and turns pages at the viewer while on. When it turns on, the gaze stays in y −0.19..0.15 from that frame: if the gaze, the fixation target or the target of a move in flight (a micro-saccade's own target) is outside the band it starts a reading saccade, else a move in flight lands at once (so no `EASE_OUT_BACK` overshoot past the floor is left); either way the move is dated back so it has landed, since the moment can show its first frame on the next tick. A moment that starts or ends on line 3 or later ends that line, so the y changes only with a return sweep, never mid-line. `test_moment.c` `test_acting` now reaches 304.4 px at most (text from 310).
- **Tests** (`test_bot.c`): `test_reading` (8 seeds × 600 s, every own saccade classified: lines of 3, 4, 5, 6 steps a quarter each, lines ending only on their steps or the margin, steps 0.18-0.38, regressions −0.10-0.22 only right of −0.35 at 0.10-0.14 per step (0.115), y per line, lines 0-5 all read, return sweeps and new pages from the left margin, new pages' line 0 jittered, their hold × 1.3 (1.28) and within 1.3 × 0.35-2 s, return-sweep blinks 50-70 % of the eligible ones (57 %), page turns: target, 1.2 s, a blink every time, half up, 4-6 lines a third each, every 20-40 s on average (34.9 s, 19.6-53.8), the mark 4-6 % (4.5 %) at 0.6-1.0 s, the viewer 5.5-8.5 % (6.8 %; a 200-seed run gives 7.2 % and 4.9 %), |x| ≤ 0.7 for every target, every frame's gaze y ≥ −0.35, the saccade rate within 8 % of 0.9.69's 0.982/s), `test_reading_turn_blink` (a blink that expires on the page turn's frame still gets the forced blink, 20 turns), `test_reading_entry` (300 entries: the glide goes to line 0's start, no glance, held 1.3 × 0.35-2 s), `test_reading_redraws` (10 seeds × 600 s, no more than 0.9.69's 14.65 redraws/s + 5 %), `test_reading_other_moods` (pose hashes of idle, sleepy, waiting, error and done over 150 s, recorded from 0.9.69; a return to working starts a new page) and `test_reading_moment` (24 seeds × 300 s with a moment 4.5 s in every 9.7: y −0.19..0.15 on every frame while on, no mark glance and no upward page turn while on, lines 3-4 and the mark again after, a gaze off the band moved at once, no step along a line moving y by more than its jitter; then 12 seeds × 200 s turning it on in the middle of every move in flight, 16,779 moves, 54 micro-saccades and 87 saccades of which would have left the band, checked at 600 fps for 1 s: none does; no saccade outside working). `test_moment.c` `test_acting` sets `bot_read_moment` for the quiet variant as `page_tick` does, and `run.sh` checks that call's place in `page_tick`. Against the 0.9.69 `bot_behavior.c` (with `bot_read_moment` stubbed to store the flag only) 39 checks fail in `test_reading`, `test_reading_turn_blink`, `test_reading_entry`, `test_reading_moment` and the new-page check; the review fixes' tests (in flight, turn blink, entry and line 0 jitter, mid-line jumps) each failed on the branch before their fix; the cost, rate, range and other-mood checks pass there by design. Mutants: no ease change at the floor (gaze reaches −0.380), no micro flip (−0.374), and BotNext's unconditional page reset on every mood change (other moods' hashes change) each fail. Host sim (60 fps, seed 77, 280 s, `test_breath_redraws`): working 14.7 → 13.7 redraws/s (12.7 → 11.7 without breath); 10 seeds × 600 s: 14.65 → 13.51.

No Ember change.

### Big reading jumps behind a blink (#127, 0.9.71)

The maintainer saw occasional tearing on the eyes in 0.9.70. There is no tear sync (#42), and a saccade lands 35-115 px in one 16 ms frame. 0.9.70 does not step more than 0.9.69, but its biggest moves are the return sweep (6.8/min, about 96 px), the new page (1.6/min, about 74 px) and the page turn (1.6/min, about 60 px, diagonal), and the page turn's forced blink did not hide its jump: the 54 ms saccade ended before the 75 ms lid close.

- **Covered jumps** (`bot_behavior.c` `start_saccade_to`, `SAC_SWEEP` and `SAC_TURN`, `blink_cover`): a return sweep, a new page's first saccade or a page turn whose amplitude is over 0.6 (`COVER_AMP`) always blinks, and its saccade starts once both lids are 0.9 closed (`LID_SHUT`): 0.075 · √0.9 = 71 ms after the blink starts, plus the right eye's lag (0-20 ms), so the jump waits 0-91 ms (79 ms on average; less when a blink is already closing). Duration (25 ms + 45 ms × amplitude) and `EASE_OUT_BACK` stay; the lean starts 30 ms after the saccade, as before, and the fixation counts from the saccade's end. The covering blink never doubles (`double_blink_pending` is cleared whichever blink it is). It is a fresh one, the one already closing, the second half of a double blink pulled forward to now when the jump falls in its 60 ms gap (the lids are open there, so nothing jumps), or, once the blink showing is past its 0.9 close (in its hold or reopening), the same blink closed again from where each lid is. So both lids are ≥ 0.9 for at least 25 ms of every covered flight. Each eye's lid stays continuous, so the right eye can lead: a negative `blink_lag` (at most the original lag), which `blink_on` and the blink's end handle. It survives a mood change made during that blink, and its double blink, which reuses the lag; that is harmless. Covered jumps skip the old coupling (amplitude over 0.8, 60 %); everything else keeps it. It checked the `blinking` flag, which a blink that ended between frames keeps until later in the same `bot_pose`; since 0.9.76 it checks the blink showing (`blink_on`, #124). Only sleepy's heavy blinks can be stale and over 1.2 s old, so no pose hash changed; `test_coupled_blink_expired` pins it with an expired blink still flagged before a big saccade in sleepy (heavy), idle and error: 53 of 85 cases blink, 0 before.
- **Page turns at 0.6 or less** (36 % of turns) keep their blink at the saccade's start, but now also get a fresh or re-closed blink when the one showing is past its close, so every turn shows both lids at 0.9 (#126: in 1.9 % of 0.9.70's turns a reopening blink suppressed the forced one).
- **Unchanged**: forward steps, regressions, the mark and viewer glances (28 % of glances are over 0.6 and still move with open eyes; covering them would clash with BotNext), the entry glide, quiet weather moments (a moment that starts while a covered jump waits for the lids keeps the pending jump when the gaze and its target are in the band, switching it to in-out easing if its overshoot would leave the band; otherwise the jump lands at once, as before; since 0.9.76 an in-flight in-out move whose gaze and target are in the band, such as working's entry glide, goes on instead of landing at once, since it can't overshoot: #125, `test_reading_moment_glide`, 80 cases with the moment on 0.05-0.35 s into the glide, the largest step 0.0084 per ms against 1.18 before), the y ≥ −0.35 bound and every other mood (no RNG draw changes outside working; the 0.9.69 pose hashes still match).
- **Steps** (host analysis, 20 seeds × 600 s of working at 16 ms, per redraw after `bot_pose_same`, worst eye bounding-box edge in px at r 195.7; "open" = an eye more than half open in both frames; per minute):

  | Cause | 0.9.70 open > 8 px | 0.9.71 open > 8 px |
  |---|---|---|
  | Forward step | 37.9 | 37.6 |
  | Regression | 3.1 | 3.2 |
  | Return sweep | 16.9 | 0.15 |
  | New page | 3.3 | 0.2 |
  | Page turn | 2.9 | 0.93 |
  | Viewer glance | 5.0 | 4.8 |
  | Mark glance | 5.3 | 5.8 |
  | Blink (lids) | 12.1 | 14.4 |
  | Entry glide | 0.2 | 0.2 |
  | **All** | **86.7** | **67.3** |

  All steps over 8 px, open or shut: 111.9 → 122.2 a minute (the jumps now happen behind the lids). Edge step p99 75.4 → 62.4 px. Blinks 12.95 → 15.56 a minute. Saccades 0.950 → 0.941/s (−1.0 %: each covered jump waits 79 ms for the lids); `test_reading`'s 8 seeds: 0.959 → 0.946/s.
- **Tests** (`test_bot.c`): `test_reading` now checks every return sweep, new page and page turn over 0.6 (747 in 8 seeds × 600 s): it blinks, both lids are ≥ 0.9 for the first 25 ms from `gaze_start` (1 ms poses), it starts 0-91 ms after it is chosen with the gaze and lean still where they were, its duration and easing are unchanged, no other saccade waits, and every page turn closes both lids to 0.9 (replacing the 50-70 % return-sweep blink check and the `blinking` flag count). `test_reading_open_steps` (10 seeds × 600 s at 16 ms after `bot_pose_same`, gaze, lean and scale steps only, at least one eye open): return sweeps and new pages under 1 a minute (0.24 and 0.12; 0.9.70 16.98 and 3.33), page turns under 1.5 (0.66; 2.86), all under 62 (53.3; 75.4). `test_reading_blink_phases` (was `test_reading_turn_blink`) makes page turns and return sweeps on blinks of every phase: expired, reopening, late and early in the hold, closing, and in a double blink's gap. It sweeps each phase × lag (0-20 ms) × a pending double blink or not × gaze from x 0.6 (jumps over 0.6) or not, 336 cases, so no case depends on the seed. Both lids reach 0.9 on every turn and covered jump. Covered jumps wait 0-91 ms and keep both lids ≥ 0.9 for their first 25 ms. No covering blink blinks again. No lid moves more than 0.06 per ms or snaps open, and the blink ends within 0.8 s. `test_reading_moment` now also turns the moment on in every lead (12 seeds × 300 s, 5,430 cases): the 2,259 jumps whose gaze and target are in the band don't move before both lids are at 0.9, and none leaves the band. `test_reading_redraws` now holds to 0.9.70's 13.51. Against 0.9.70's `bot_behavior.c` 9 checks fail; against this PR's first head (e10a851), the review's tests fail 5 checks. Mutants, each failing: no lead, a lead without the right eye's lag, no re-close, a re-close that restarts the blink (lid pops), a blink end that ignores a negative lag (lid snaps open), threshold 0.8, glances covered, the fixation or the turn's hold counted from the choice, the lean not delayed, double blinks kept (13.66 redraws/s), covering in every mood (pose hashes), small turns not closed, in-out easing; after the review: a reused blink keeping its double, no pull-forward in the gap (150 ms lead), re-closing only once reopening, a moment landing every lead at once, keeping the lead with the gaze off the band, and no easing switch.
- **Redraws** (host sim): `test_reading_redraws` (10 seeds × 600 s) 13.51 → 13.38/s; the 20-seed analysis 13.51 → 13.31; `test_breath_redraws` (seed 77, 280 s) working 13.7 → 13.5 (11.7 → 11.5 without breath). Without the double-blink cut it was 13.65.
- **Cost** (`idf.py size`, origin/main 0.9.70 at c253309 → this branch, same machine, IDF 5.5.5): image 1,709,703 → 1,711,175 B (+1,472, flash `.text`); DIRAM 102,479 B and PSRAM `.bss` unchanged (no new state). `stack_walk.py`: `lvgl` 8,608 (margin 1,888), `orbit` 2,192 and `moment` 3,424 unchanged.
- **On the knob**: measured on 0.9.72 (0.9.71 plus the area counter, #128/#133; no bot change), the same way as 0.9.70 (Ember's full diagnostics, 1 h, points after the first 5 min of uptime), 2026-10-13 18:52-19:52 UTC, 59 points. The bot was asleep for most of that hour (median 2.6 fps, 4.1 areas/s), so the aggregate is not comparable with 0.9.70's mixed hour; matched fps bands are thin (25-32 fps: 16 → 5 points, core 0 29.2 → 30.1 %, frame avg 5.66 → 6.11 ms; 11-17 fps: 22 → 17 points, 22.0 → 19.3 %, 7.55 → 6.33 ms). Working held in Demo on 0.9.72 ("Invalidated-area count (#118)"): 26.9 fps, 39.8 areas/s, core 0 28.1 %, in line with 0.9.70's 25-32 fps band. Confirmed by the maintainer on the knob with Devices › Demo › Working: tearing looks better.

### Waiting escalates, then calms down (#114, review P4, 0.9.73)

The fourth batch of [`bot-review.md`](bot-review.md), matched to BotNext (`design/faces/canvas/BotNext.dc.html`, its waiting schedule and constants). Before, waiting hopped every lognormal(4.5 s, 2.5-9 s) forever.

- **Phases** (`bot_behavior.c` `wait_tick`; `w_t0`, `w_phase` in `bot_t`), timed from when waiting began or restarted:
  - A, 0-20 s (`BOT_WAIT_B_S`): the first hop at 0.6 s as before, then a hop every lognormal(2.8 s, σ 0.25, 2-4.5 s). 25 % are double hops: the second, at height 0.6, starts when the first ends (BotNext chains it at 0.92 × the length, in the landing squash; see below).
  - B, 20 s-3 min (`BOT_WAIT_C_S`): a hop every lognormal(8 s, σ 0.3, 5-14 s), and a peek every 4-6 s, the first 1.5-3 s into B.
  - C, after 3 min: no hops. A nudge every 12-20 s, the first 1-3 s into C. A reminder every 5 min of waiting (`BOT_WAIT_REMIND_S`, the first at 300 s): two hops at height 1, the second 1.05 s after the first, or when it ends if later.
  - Hop jitter: each phase hop draws a height × 0.8-1.15 and a length of 0.9-1.1 s (`hop_h`, `hop_len`). The pose's `hop_dy` carries the height, and the curve stretches to the length. Other hops (a push, a demo's `hop`, a weather act's hop) stay 1 × 1 s.
- **A hop starts only after the last one ended**, on the base outline. A hop that comes due while another runs waits for its end (at most about 0.2 s in phase A, behind a double hop). BotNext's 0.92 × length start of the second hop restarts the curve in the landing squash: the outline jumped 4 variants (−4 → base → −5) in one frame on every double hop (review, PR #138); at the hop's end the squash is back at the base. A nudge waits until no hop runs and no second hop is pending; a reminder waits for the end of a nudge (0.35 s). A mood change ends a nudge at once.
- **Peek**: a saccade to the viewer (± 0.05) with a double blink. A peek over 0.6 (from the sides) moves behind the lids like #127's covered jumps (`SAC_PEEK` takes `blink_cover`: the saccade starts once both lids are 0.9 closed, 0-91 ms later); a shorter one moves at once with the blink starting. The second blink follows 60 ms after the first, as any double blink does. A peek is skipped, the next one 4-6 s later, while acting, tracking or holding the gaze (`gaze_hold`: a knob turn or `bot_look_still` 2.5 s, a half chase's `bot_hold_gaze`, the end of a track 1 s), so a peek never cuts a knob look short.
- **Nudge**: outline variant −2 for 0.1 s, +1 for 0.1 s, then the base (3 swaps), with the badge × 1 + 0.18·sin(π·u / 0.35 s) (peak 1.18 at 0.175 s). That is BotNext's curve; the review's `EASE_OUT_BACK` 1 → 1.18 does not come back to 1. The scale comes from `bot_rim_variant`, which now also builds the view's 11 variants, so the nudge hits variants −2 and +1 exactly. The eyes squash with it, as in BotNext. No new pose field: the nudge moves `scale_x/y` and `badge`, and the hop height moves `hop_dy`, all already in `bot_pose_same`.
- **Restart at phase A** (`bot_wait_restart`, nothing outside waiting), once at least a reminder period (5 min) has passed since waiting began or last restarted, so phase C has been reached: when the bot page is shown after being hidden (`page_bot_show` flags it, `page_bot_frame` restarts with the frame's time: a page step, a demo scene that switches to it, the return from the OTA face); when the lead name changes or the host count goes up while the bot page shows (`ember_host_lead_new`; `ember_host_info_t` now carries the lead name without the ` +N` and the count; a count going down, a tool or a colour change does not count); when quiet calm ends. Without the limit a label that flips (` +1` with another host cycling through waiting) kept waiting in phase A forever, louder than 0.9.72, and flips under 0.6 s stopped the hops altogether (review, PR #138); with it, a restart every 0.5-60 s gives 0.10 hops/s against 0.9.72's 0.22, and phase C every 5 min. A restart keeps an earlier due hop (`fmin`; only a demo scene's restart in A or B can have one, since a gated restart lands in C) and drops a pending second hop. Entering waiting and a demo scene's start or end (below) restart at once. A lead change off the bot page counts when the page is shown. A restart asked before the gate was dropped in 0.9.73-0.9.75, so a host that started waiting in phase C got nudges until the reminder; since 0.9.76 (#139) a new lead or a higher host count is latched (`bot_wait_restart(..., latch)` true, `w_pending`) and `wait_tick` applies it when the gate opens, in place of the reminder due then; several asks give one restart. The page being shown and calm ending pass no latch and are still dropped before the gate (review on #147): a glance at the page at 2 min would otherwise turn the 300 s reminder into a full phase A with nothing new to show, and an earlier calm A was silent anyway, so the reminder's double hop is enough. Restarts still come at most once per 5 min: flapping every 0.5-60 s gives 0.096 hops/s.
- **Calm**: phase hops, double hops, nudges and reminders keep their schedule but do not show (`BOT_ANIM_WAIT_HOP`); peeks go on, since calm stops no saccade. 0.9.72 cleared the hop schedule instead.
- **Demo** (`bot_wait_scene`, called on every `page_tick` with the playing scene's number, 0 for none, just before the mood is set; `scene_start` numbers every scene): while a scene plays, phase C starts at 35 s instead of 3 min and the reminder comes every 50 s instead of 5 min (`BOT_WAIT_DEMO_C_S`, `BOT_WAIT_DEMO_REMIND_S`). Phase A keeps its real 20 s, so its loudness (the issue's open decision) shows as it is. Devices › Demo › Waiting with Hold (60 s) shows A (0-20 s), B (20-35 s: about 2 hops and 3 peeks) and C (35-60 s: 2 nudges and the 2-hop reminder at 50 s). A change of the number restarts waiting at A: a scene starts at phase A even when the real mood was waiting already, a scene replaced by the next one in the same frame starts at A too (a flag alone missed that, review), and real waiting restarts at A when the scenes end. Without Hold a scene plays 8 s, so phase A only. No Ember or protocol change: the knob already knows a scene is playing.
- **RNG**: the new draws happen only in waiting; idle, sleepy, working, error and done give the same poses as 0.9.72 for the same seed (`test_reading_other_moods`' hashes; waiting's is re-recorded).
- **Tests** (`test_bot.c`):
  - `test_wait_phases`: 12 seeds × 620 s at 120 Hz, plus 150 seeds × phase A. First hop at 0.6 s. Phase A gaps 2-4.7 s (a hop waits up to 0.21 s for a double hop's end), median 2.79 s (999 gaps). B gaps 5-14.2 s, median 8.08 s. No plain hop in C. Double hops 25 %, only in A, the second at height 0.6 within a frame of the first's end. Heights 0.8-1.15, lengths 0.9-1.1. Two reminders in 620 s, at 300 s and 600 s (late by at most a nudge's 0.35 s), both hops at height 1, the second at max(1.05 s, the first's length). Peeks only in B, the first 1.5-3 s in, then every 4-6 s, at the viewer, each with a double blink pending; the 79 of 381 over 0.6 start with both lids ≥ 0.9, 0-92 ms late, the rest at once. Nudges only in C, the first 1-3 s in, gaps 12-20 s (up to 2.5 s more behind a reminder). Every hop starts on the base outline with no dy, and no nudge frame has a hop running. Outline moves per minute: C under A/20, B under A/2.
  - `test_wait_nudge` (1 ms steps): the exact −2, +1 and base scale in each window, no outline dy, the badge peaking at 1.18 at 0.175 s and 1 after; the variant table's −2 and +1 values.
  - `test_wait_restart`: no restart in A, in B, or in C before 5 min, but a latched one (a new lead) comes at the gate (300 s, first hop 0.6 s later, no reminder), once for two asks, while the page shown or calm off at 200 s is dropped and the 300 s reminder comes (0.9.76); at 320 s back to A, first hop at 0.6 s; a restart in phase A keeps a hop due within 0.6 s (the `fmin`, 0.9.76); a restart during a reminder drops its second hop and the next hop waits for the end; a restart every 0.5, 2.5, 7.5 or 60 s over 4 × 20 min: under 0.12 hops/s (0.097) and phase C reached 16 times; outside waiting the whole `bot_t` is unchanged; a new waiting mood starts at A; calm: no hop or nudge for 400 s (a reminder due included), peeks go on, calm off restarts A; a knob look holds a due peek for 2.5 s and skips it; no peeks while acting. `test_wait_nudge` also checks that a mood change ends a nudge. `test_ember.c`: `ember_host_lead_new` (a new lead or one more host: new; one fewer, a tool or a colour alone: not).
  - `test_wait_hop_chain` (0.9.76, #139): a double hop's second is set for the first's end, a reminder's for max(1.05 s, the first's length); a plain, second or reminder hop forced due mid-hop waits for its end. The 0.92 × length, no-`fmax` and each `!hopping` guard mutant fail it; before, each failed only the waiting pose hash, since the guard and the timing each prevent the snap alone.
  - `test_wait_demo`: 20 seeds × a 60 s hold: no plain hop after 35 s, the first nudge at 36-38 s, 2 nudges, the reminder at 50 s, peeks in 20-35 s; a scene starting mid-waiting restarts it, the same scene again does not, the next scene in phase C restarts at A, the real timings come back after it.
  - `test_micro_saccades` counts peeks as saccades.
  - Mutants, each failing: a hop starting in the air, a peek not covered, nudge variant −1 instead of −2, no gaze hold on peeks, no restart on calm off, the reminder timed from phase C; after review: the second hop at 0.92 × length (15 checks), a nudge not waiting for a pending second hop, a restart in any phase, a scene flag instead of its number, `enter` keeping the nudge.
  - `run.sh` checks the wiring: `page_bot_show` flags only hidden to shown, `page_bot_frame` restarts on a new lead with the latch, else when shown without it, before recording the host, `page_tick` sets the scene number right before the mood, `scene_start` numbers the scene, `view_host` fills the lead and the count.
- **Host sim** (60 fps, seeds 777, 1234 and 99, 600 s of waiting; redraws after `bot_pose_same`, outline included; an outline move is a change of variant or dy, as in the review's table). 0.9.72 → 0.9.73:

  | Window | Redraws/s | Outline moves/min |
  |---|---|---|
  | 0-20 s (A) | 23.7 → 32.0 | 480 → 939 |
  | 20-180 s (B) | 21.9 → 21.2 | 525 → 305 |
  | 180-600 s (C) | 21.6 → 12.8 | 516 → 22 |
  | 0-600 s | 21.8 → 15.6 | 517 → 128 |

  BotNext in the review: 483 → 132 a minute over 0-600 s, and about twice today's moves in the first 20 s.
- **Cost** (`idf.py size`, origin/main 0.9.72 at 1fdca91 → this branch, same machine, IDF 5.5.5): image 1,712,631 → 1,716,979 B (+4,348, flash `.text`); DIRAM 102,575 → 102,719 B (`.bss` +144: the new `bot_t` fields, the last lead seen and the scene number); PSRAM `.bss` unchanged. `stack_walk.py`: `lvgl` 8,608 (margin 1,888), `orbit` 2,192 and `moment` 3,424 unchanged; `ember` 5,120 (margin 1,792).
- **On the knob** (0.9.73, 2026-10-14): Devices › Demo › Waiting (Hold) three times, 60 s each, live stats in 5 s samples; phases at demo timings (C from 35 s, reminder at 50 s). 0.9.72 is its held waiting from "Invalidated-area count (#118)". Every cell is the median of the 5 s samples (ranges in brackets), frame max is the highest. Samples by end time after the demo start: A 4-21 s (9), B 24-36 s (6), C 39-64 s (14); samples straddling a phase boundary are left out.

  | | 0.9.72 (Demo › Waiting, held) | 0.9.73 A (0-20 s) | 0.9.73 B (20-35 s) | 0.9.73 C (35-60 s) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Invalidated areas/s | 20.8 (14.3–29.7) | 24.5 (17.1–33.4) | 29.8 (21.4–37.7) | 22.9 (16.1–33.6) |

  Phase A costs about 1.7× 0.9.72's pixels and 10 points more core 0, as designed (twice the hops). B and C cost less than 0.9.72. The demo's C includes the 50 s reminder, so a real wait after 3 min sits at or below this C column. Areas/s stays about flat because blinks, saccades and micro-saccades keep small areas going in every phase (peeks in B); the saving is in area size (the outline).

### Mood-change beat and done as a reward (#115, review P6, P7, 0.9.74)

The fifth batch of [`bot-review.md`](bot-review.md), matched to BotNext (`design/faces/canvas/BotNext.dc.html`, its view's swap and settle, its done schedule). Before, `bot_view_update` re-rendered the base outline on the LVGL task at the mood change (about 120 ms with the eyes still open, the colour cut before the lids closed), the badge started growing 0.1 s after the change and the eyes swapped on their own at the first closed blink; done was the stillest mood.

- **The beat** (`bot_behavior.c` `beat`, `swap_eyes`; `face` in `bot_t` and `bot_pose_t`): on a mood change `mood` changes at once, `face` (the mood the outline, badge and label show) only at the beat. The beat is the first frame with a lid at 0.9 or more once the view has the new outline ready (`bot_rim_ready`), or once 0.45 s have passed since the change without it (`BOT_BEAT_MAX_S`, BotNext's limit). It swaps the eye shape and starts the badge tween (0.35 s, `EASE_OUT_BACK` in, `EASE_IN_OUT` out) in that frame; the view swaps the outline, the badge colour and the label in the same frame (below). The lid test counts the heavy lid (sleepy, asleep, an act's lid) with the blink, so a beat due while asleep lands once the sleep lids are shut. A blink that ends without the beat (a frame gap skipped its closed lids: an OTA pause, a slow frame) starts another closure (1.6x, no lag, no RNG draw) instead of swapping on open lids; asleep, the sleep lids carry it instead. A frame gap over 0.1 s while a beat waits (`BOT_BEAT_GAP_S`) gives the outline another 0.45 s from the resume (`beat_by`), so after an OTA hold the beat waits behind the new closure for the re-rendered outline. Only the first gap of a mood change does so since 0.9.76 (`beat_gapped`, #146): with every frame over 0.1 s apart each frame extended it, so the lids waited only for the outline.
- **Lids held for the outline**: while a beat waits for the outline (until `beat_by`), a blink whose closing comes after the change stays shut (the blink clock is held at the point where both lids are at 1, lag included) until the outline is ready or the 0.45 s pass. A blink already opening when the mood changed is not snapped shut: the double blink the change queues carries the beat. A held blink, and a re-closure, stay shut until the frame that can beat (`beat_held`, 0.9.76, #146), so the beat lands in that frame whatever the frame rate; before, releasing the hold ran the blink on past its shut part between slow frames, and a re-closure sampled at the same phase every time missed the closed lids for good (at 0.2 s frames no beat came). Waking from sleepy or asleep starts the blink shut, so the lids stay closed until the beat.
- **Outline off the LVGL task** (`components/bot/rim_slots.c`: the slots, the `rim` task's loop, start, swap and the variant pick; `rim_beat.c`: the beat's state; `bot_view.c` supplies only the LVGL effects through `rim_slots_ops_t`): a mood or asleep change starts a `rim` job for `bot_rim_key(mood, asleep)` (the tone plus `BOT_RIM_TRIANGLE` in error; the outline's shape now follows the mood, not `triangle >= 0.5`, so error renders once instead of twice). The `rim` task (core 1) renders that base first into variant slot 0 (`RIM_SPARE`, the −5 squash, rebuilt anyway: no new buffer), then the variants −1, +1, ... into their slots, skipping slot 0. Meanwhile the old outline shows its base only (its variants are being overwritten). When `face` asks for it and its base is ready, the LVGL task swaps the base and spare buffers, shows the new base, sets the badge colour and redraws the label, and wakes the task to render slot 0 as the −5 variant. A hold (OTA) redoes what is missing: the base while not swapped, the variants by generation. When the job is the outline already on screen (A, B never swapped, A again) the beat does not wait for it. On every page, `page_tick` calls `bot_view_rim_feed(mood, asleep, shown)` right before `bot_pose` (its answer is `bot_rim_ready`) and `bot_view_rim_pose(pose, t)` right after (the job and the swap follow the pose's mood and face). Off the bot page the `rim` task renders the base only (about 120 ms of core 1 per change, time-sliced with `wxbake` and the moment bake at the same priority) and leaves the variants until the page shows again, when the feed wakes it; a swap there adds the label redraw (hidden objects). So the first frame back shows the face's outline (review: a beat off the page used to show new eyes with the old outline for a base render, and A, B, C off the page left B's outline never swapped). Every sync takes the frame's time.
- **Settle**: after a mood change (not an asleep-only fade), once variants −1 and +1 are ready, the outline shows −1 for 60 ms, +1 for 80 ms, then the base (`RIM_SETTLE_M1_S`, `RIM_SETTLE_S`), only while the pose asks for the base (a hop's own variant wins), and not if the variants come more than 1 s after the swap, or the bot page is hidden while it is due (`rim_beat_t.off`, set by `rim_slots_feed`; 0.9.76, #146): a beat off the page and a return within 1 s played it on the page with open lids and no beat. `bot_view_tick` runs `rim_sync` every frame, so the swap and the settle steps land even when the pose is still. The `rim` task goes on into the variants before the swap, so the settle starts about 0.24 s after a beat that comes early (at 120 ms per outline), and in the swap frame itself, −1 behind the closed lids, for a beat 0.36 s or more after the change. During a chase a pose whose `face` changed is drawn whatever the chase throttle, so the eyes swap in the beat's frame.
- **Done's reward** (`done_hop_at`, `giggle_at`, `lap_at` from the beat; `BOT_ANIM_DONE_REWARD`, none while calm):
  - a hop at +0.35 s at 0.7 height (`BOT_DONE_HOP_S`, `BOT_DONE_HOP_H`), waiting for any hop in the air to land;
  - a giggle at +1.3 s: two lid squeezes to 0.45, each a half sine of 120 ms, with 150 ms between them (`BOT_GIGGLE_*`); the happy arc flattens with the lid;
  - one glint lap at +1.8 s for 3 s (`BOT_LAP_AT_S`, `BOT_LAP_S`, pose fields `lap` and `lap_at`, its start): the view runs the 45 cached glint steps once from 12 o'clock (`lap_step`, `bot_lap_step(lap_at, t)`, so a return to the page mid-lap goes on from where the lap is), in the outline's colour (`glint_draw_cb` now recolours with `mood_rgb(s_rim_mood)`; working's glint and a demo chase in working stay green; a demo chase forced in another mood now takes that mood's colour, kept as is), only with the round outline at its base and with the working ring setting on (`bot_set_ring`, fed with the setting: off, the pose runs no lap and keeps no frame rate up for it). The lap is not a glint for the chase (`bot_view_glint_deg` stays working-only), so it never feeds the chase's working time.
  - BotNext times these from the mood change (+0.5, +1.6, +2.2 s) and squashes the arc for the giggle; here they run from the beat (the review's +0.35 and +1.3 s), the giggle is the review's lid squeeze, and the lap starts after the giggle, as in BotNext, instead of at the beat (a lap during the hop would hide on the squash variants).
- **Pose fields**: `face` and `lap` join `bot_pose_same` (a change redraws); `lap_at` is constant through the lap; the hop, giggle and badge use existing fields.
- **RNG**: no new draws. With the outline always ready, idle, sleepy, working and error give the same poses as 0.9.73 for the same seed; waiting and done changed only by the badge starting at the beat (and done's reward): with the badge put back at +0.1 s and no reward, all five `test_reading_other_moods` hashes match 0.9.73's. Waiting and done are re-recorded.
- **Tests** (`test_bot.c`):
  - `test_mood_beat`: outline ready 0.25 s after the change: the beat in that frame (+0.250-0.252 s) with the new eyes and the badge tween starting then, lids shut from ≤ 0.15 s to the beat, nothing changing before it, the lids open 0.1-0.3 s after; never ready: the beat at 0.45 s behind shut lids; ready at once: the beat at the first close, no held lids; a change while the lids open: no lid step over 0.05 in 1 ms, the double blink carries the beat; asleep → waiting: shut until the outline. Then 300 seeds × 12 random changes with outline latencies 0-0.6 s at 120 Hz: every beat (2,742) with a lid at 0.9 or more, the face's eyes and the badge tween starting in that frame, no eye change outside a beat (acts aside), none later than 0.8 s.
  - `test_mood_beat_view`: the behaviour with `rim_beat` and a fake `rim` task (base after 0.05, 0.12, 0.3 or 0.6 s, then 0.12 s per variant), 50 seeds × 6 changes at 60 fps: up to 0.3 s, all 300 outline swaps in the beat's frame with the lids at 0.9 or more, the settle after 292-297 of them (the rest during done's hop) and never later than 0.5 s; at 0.6 s the beat goes at 0.45 s and the outline follows with open lids (the fallback).
  - `test_mood_beat_gaps`: an OTA pause from the closing blink with the outline back 0.12 s after it, or never, and frame gaps of 0.08-0.6 s across the closing blink: every beat with a lid at 0.9 or more and the face's eyes, none before the outline unless 0.45 s passed since the resume. With an act's lid at 0.8 the beat comes once lid and blink reach 0.9 together, with the blink alone under 0.6. Since 0.9.76, 200 runs at 0.11-0.39 s frames: without an outline the beat comes behind the lids within 0.45 s of the first gap plus a frame, with one within 2 s (174 of 200 never beat before).
  - `test_rim_slots_model`: the real `rim_slots` and `rim_beat` with the bot on two threads passing a baton: the LVGL side runs `page_tick`'s feed, `bot_pose` and sync at 60 fps, the `rim` task rasters into fake buffers over 1-7 frames each, and at every `RS_RACE_POINT` and `RJ_RACE_POINT` either side may hand over. 80 seeds × 40 s with mood changes 0.03-1.5 s apart, page absences and OTA holds (before and after the swap), rasters replaced mid-way: in every rendered frame the shown buffer is not being written, holds its slot's variant, matches the badge and label, and never pairs the face's eyes with another outline on open lids; every beat behind the lids; the first frame back on the page matches; off the page no variant raster starts, and back on it −1 and +1 are ready within 1.5 s of a quiet page. The model interleaves only at the race points (RS 1-7, RJ 1-7) and inside a raster, so an ordering slip with no race point between its two stores (say `own` stored before the pointer swap, next to it) passes it; `run.sh`'s order checks on `slots_start` and `slots_swap` guard those.
  - `test_rim_beat`: keys (the triangle bit in error only, `bot_rim_mood` under both flags); a start ignores readiness from before; the old outline stays until the face asks for the job and its base is ready; −1 for 60 ms and +1 for 80 ms (±1 ms step), only while still, not after 1 s, not for an asleep fade or the same outline back; a second change before the beat restarts the job; shown counts as ready; A, B never shown, A: ready at once, swapped in place once the base is rendered; a late beat with −1 and +1 rendered: the settle from the swap frame; since 0.9.76 no settle after an off-page swap, or after leaving the page before the variants, and `rim_slots_feed` sets the flag.
  - `test_done_reward` (1 ms steps): one hop 0.35 s after the beat at height 0.7 (lift 0.154); the lids exactly the two squeezes (peak 0.45); one lap from +1.8 s for 3 s; giggle and lap count as animating; calm: none of it; done left before its reward: none later; no other mood has it; `lap_at` constant, a return halfway through runs steps 22-44; the ring setting off: no lap and not animating for it.
  - `test_calm` (done hops once now), `test_pose_same` (`face`, `lap`).
  - Mutants, each failing: the beat not waiting for the outline (8 checks), no lid hold (8), the badge from the change (6), no 0.45 s limit (3), a full-height hop (3), squeezes 150 ms apart start to start (3), the lap ignoring calm, `face` or `lap` out of `bot_pose_same`, a hold that snaps an opening blink shut, the settle on any swap, a swap before the face asks, a start keeping old readiness (7), the settle on a squashed pose, the settle without the 1 s limit, shown not counting as ready; in the model: the old page flow (ready forced off the page, no sync there), no `own` guard on the pick, `own` before the pointer swap with a race point between them, slot 0 written while not own, `base_gen` before the base raster, variants rendered off the page, no wake on the return; the blink's swap test without the heavy lid.
  - `run.sh` checks the wiring: `rim_task` runs `rim_slots_run`; `bot_view_rim_pose` takes the job from the mood and the swap from the face, `bot_view_update` renders no outline; `rim_slots_run` renders the base into the spare slot first and slot 0 only once swapped; the start shows the base before the new job; the swap swaps the buffers, shows the base, then `own`, the badge and label and the wake; every sync on the frame's time, `bot_view_tick` first; the glint takes the outline's colour; the lap from the pose's start; settings feed `bot_set_ring`; `page_tick` calls `bot_rim_ready(bot_view_rim_feed(...))` right before `bot_pose` and `bot_view_rim_pose` right after; a face change draws through the chase throttle.
- **Host sim** (60 fps, seeds 777, 1234 and 99, 6 s after a change from idle at 5 s; outline ready 0.12 s after the change, 0.12 s per variant; redraws after `bot_pose_same`; an outline move is a change of the shown outline, variant or dy). 0.9.73 → 0.9.74:

  | Change to | Redraws/s | Outline moves per change |
  |---|---|---|
  | working | 14.4 → 14.4 | 1 → 4 |
  | error | 21.1 → 21.1 | 2 → 4 |
  | done | 14.0 → 25.6 | 1 → 41 |
  | waiting | 43.1 → 43.5 | 133 → 136 |

  0.9.73's error rendered twice (the mood, then the triangle at `triangle` 0.5), each a stall. The 3 extra moves are the settle; done's 37 more are the reward hop. BotNext in the review: done's first 6 s 12.7 → 24.7 redraws/s.
- **Cost** (`idf.py size`, origin/main 0.9.73 at 6eb3768 → this branch, same machine, IDF 5.5.5): image 1,716,979 → 1,720,123 B (+3,144, flash `.text`); DIRAM 102,719 → 102,831 B (+112, `.bss` +80: the new `bot_t` fields and the view's beat state); PSRAM `.bss` 55,972 → 49,308 B (−6,664: the LVGL task's own outline scratch is gone; the `rim` task's stays); no new outline buffer. `stack_walk.py`: `lvgl` 8,608 (margin 1,888) unchanged, `rim` 1,328 → 1,376 (margin 1,184; the raster now through `rim_slots_run`'s callback, listed in `stack_walk.json`), `orbit` 2,192 → 2,224; every other task unchanged. The review fixes (`rim_slots`, the re-closure, `lap_at`, `ring`) moved DIRAM 102,831 → 102,839 B and left PSRAM `.bss` at 49,308 B.
- **On the knob** (0.9.76, 2026-10-14; 0.9.74's beat plus the #146 fixes): Devices › Demo › Working (Hold) for 25 s, then Done (Hold) for 40 s, three times, live stats every 5-7 s. Samples are binned by end time after Done was set: "Working" ends at or before the change (12), "Done entry" is the one sample per run whose window holds the change (ends at +2, +3 and +7 s, so it mixes the beat, the reward hop and giggle with a little working), then 6-12 s (3) and 15-40 s (13). The lap runs +1.8 to +4.8 s: run 1's entry sample holds all of it, runs 2 and 3 most of it in the next one. Medians; frame max is the highest sample. 0.9.73 was not measured on the knob before the update, so its column is the host figure: its mood change rendered the outline in the LVGL task, about 120 ms.

  | | 0.9.73 | Working | Done entry (3) | Done 6-12 s | Done 15-40 s |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Frame max | ~120 ms stall (host) | 21 ms | 50 ms | 16 ms | 15 ms |
  | Invalidated areas/s | n/a | 41.5 | 42.3 | 11.1 | 11.3 |

  No sample around the change reaches 120 ms: the entry's 47-50 ms is the range of the hop's full-outline frames (0.9.73 waiting hops: 43-52 ms), and the highest sample before is 21 ms and after 16 ms. Whether 0.9.73's stall would have shown in a 5 s frame max is not measured (its P4 run dropped the samples holding a mood change). The reward costs about +20 points of core 0 and 2.5× the pixels for one sample; core 1 rises to ~43 % in that sample (likely the `rim` task rendering the outline and its variants), then settles at 16-18 %, above working's 11 %; the cause is not measured. Core 0 settles about level with working. Internal free and largest block (31.7 KB) unchanged.

Nits from the re-checks of #120, #123, #138 and #142, each recorded in its feature's entry above (marked 0.9.76): input during an act and the lid leaving sleepy (#121, breathing eyes), the coupled saccade blink (#124) and a quiet moment during an in-out move (#125, big reading jumps), the deferred waiting restart and its tests (#139, waiting), the off-page settle and the beat on slow frames (#146, mood beat). No pose hash changed. Cost (`idf.py size`, origin/main 0.9.75 at 567ab87 → this branch, same machine, IDF 5.5.5): image 1,721,552 → 1,722,368 B (+816, flash `.text`); DIRAM 102,839 → 102,847 B (`.bss` +8: the new `bot_t` and `rim_beat_t` flags); PSRAM `.bss` 49,308 B unchanged.

### Direction-aware tilt, lid-line blink, frustrated error (#116, review P9-P11, 0.9.77)

The sixth batch of [`bot-review.md`](bot-review.md), matched to BotNext (`design/faces/canvas/BotNext.dc.html`: `eyeStrokes`, the view's lid clip, error's `shake_at` and `huff_at`).

- **P9, tilt mirrors** (`bot_shape.c`): the dash's rise (0.04·side·lean), the dash's tilt (27°·lean) and the round eyes' 10° tilt take `sgn(cx)`. Before, `lean` came from `fabs(cx)`, so a left glance tilted "\" and raised the right eye, like a right glance. Now a left glance is the exact mirror of a right glance (a 3/4 turn to the left); angry and happy eyes were already symmetric. The Swift original (`BotRenderer.swift`) has the same `fabs` rise, so this is a change, not a port fix.
- **P10, the lid is a clip line from the top**, as in BotNext, not the review's exponent tweak (which still passed through a dot):
  - `bot_eye_strokes`: the dash, angry and round eyes keep their open height; the width grows ×(1 + 0.35·lid) (dash, angry) or ×(1 + 0.15·lid) (round); the dash's tilt no longer eases to −6° and the angry slash stays at 55°. The eye centre drops 0.015·lid (`BOT_LID_DROOP`, the review's droop; BotNext has none). Each stroke carries its lid (`bot_stroke_t.lid`) and the strip a shut lid leaves (`keep`); the happy arc still flattens with the lid and only a blink clips it.
  - `bot_lid_line_y`: the line in screen px is top + (bottom − keep·width − top)·lid, top and bottom taken 1 px past the stroke's edge so they include every row it touches. A heavy lid (sleepy, asleep, an act's) keeps a bottom crescent 0.3 × width tall (`BOT_LID_KEEP`).
  - A blink that carries an eye swap hides the eyes whole (review #149): the pose carries each blink's part of the lid (`blink_l`, `blink_r`) and whether the blink carries a pending beat or costume (`hide`, from `bot_t.blink_swap`, in `bot_pose_same`). On such a blink, from 0.9 (`BOT_BLINK_HIDE`) the line reaches the bottom and the strip is 0, scaling in below that; a happy arc is never clipped from the top and vanishes as a step at 0.9. Every other blink, ordinary or heavy, keeps the closed-eye line, as 0.9.76 and BotNext do (a first version hid every blink: empty faces up to 1.2 s in sleepy, and happy arcs split in two). A mood beat or a costume swaps the eye shape only when both blinks are at 0.9 or more, so no eye pixel shows in the swap frame; before, the heavy lid plus the blink at 0.9 counted, and the crescent showed the new shape (44 of 306 random swaps, up to 1,589 px). The swap now waits for the lagging eye too (up to 20 ms). A blink takes on a swap only while it still closes (`blink_swap` latches up to its shut point), so a reopening eye never closes again; a request too late for it, beat or costume, closes the lids again at the blink's end and swaps behind that (before, a late costume swapped there with open eyes, #150). Asleep, a beat still lands on the shut sleep lids (dash to dash). `eye_raster` starts the canvas at the even row at or above the line (`bot_raster_lid_box`, bottom kept) and `bot_raster_stroke_below` zeroes the rows above it without rasterising them and keeps the crossed row's part below the line, so a heavy lid costs fewer pixels than an open eye.
  - Sleepy's eyes (lid 0.68-0.92) are now the bottom crescent of the slumped dash, not a short horizontal capsule, and they sit lower: the clip keeps the open eye's bottom edge. In the night moment's act from sleepy that edge reached 311-316 px, into the text line at 310; the night glide's y moves from −0.15 to −0.07 (`wx_moment.c`, `bot2.py`, B2-Night regenerated), and the eyes now end at 307.7 px in every mood.
  - `BOT_LID_REACH` stays 0.2: the lid line moves 0.392 (dash), 0.407 (round) and 0.237 (angry) body radii per lid unit, so a lid redraws per whole pixel of its line (angry about 0.6 px), pinned by `test_lid_quantum`. With 0.4 (half a pixel, the first version of this PR) sleepy's breathing lid redrew 17.5 times a second; now it is 11.4, as in 0.9.76.
- **P11, error is frustrated** (`bot_behavior.c`):
  - an entry shake 0.3 s after the beat (`BOT_SHAKE_AT_S`; the lids are open again by then): the eyes swing sideways 3 cycles in 0.4 s, 6 px (`BOT_SHAKE_AMP` 0.0307 r) decaying linearly, through `offset_x`, so the outline does not move (P5). BotNext starts it 0.45 s after the mood change; from the beat it never plays behind lids held for the outline;
  - looks: 45 % to the viewer (±0.08) and 25 % down-centre (x ±0.15, y −0.5 ±0.1) with fixations lognormal(1.7 s, σ 0.3, 1.2-2.5 s), 30 % side darts at |x| 0.7-0.9, y ±0.3 for 0.4-0.9 s (BotNext's split; the review's 70/30);
  - a huff every lognormal(25 s, σ 0.35, 12.5-50 s) from the shake (`BOT_HUFF_MEDIAN_S`, the issue's 25 s; BotNext uses 12 s, 6-25 s): over 0.5 s the eyes dip 0.03 r (pose field `eye_dy`) and the lids squeeze to 0.45 (half sines; on the angry eyes the squeeze is the lid line). BotNext's eye-only huff replaces the review's squash variant −2 and double blink: no outline swap, no tearing.
  - Shake and huff are `BOT_ANIM_ERROR_FX` and count as animating. Calm gates their start: no huff starts and a pending shake is dropped while calm. One already running finishes, also when error ends or calm turns on (review #149: they snapped up to 20 px of lid and 2.7 px of shake in one frame); `enter` drops only the next huff and a shake not yet started.
  - Precedence: touch tracking drops the shake (it would add ±6 px to the tracked gaze) and holds error's saccades as in every mood, so no darts; a scripted act starts no huff, so the act's lids stay its own.
  - The huff schedule draws its RNG in the beat and at each huff, in error only.
- **Pose fields**: `eye_dy` (half a pixel, like the gaze) and `hide` join `bot_pose_same`; `blink_l` and `blink_r` move with the lids, which it already compares.
- **RNG and hashes**: idle, sleepy and working give the same poses as 0.9.76 for the same seed. Error's `test_reading_other_moods` hash changed with P11 (new targets and fixations, the shake, the huff's lids and draws), then waiting's, error's and done's with the hidden swap (the beat up to 20 ms later); with the old swap condition all of them match the previous recording.
- **Tests** (`test_bot.c`, each failing on 0.9.76's code or with its piece removed):
  - `test_tilt_mirror`: for every eye shape over gazes, lids, triangle, slump, breath, orbit and asleep, a left glance (lids swapped) is the right glance mirrored to 1e-12; looking left raises the left eye and leans the dash "/" (0.9.76: 527 dash and 527 round cases off).
  - `test_lid_shape`: no eye flattens with the lid, the centre drops 0.015·lid, the widening, happy unclipped, the lid clamped to 0..1. `test_lid_line`: the line (none open, a 0.3 × width strip shut, halfway at 0.5, coming down monotonically), the clipped raster against the full stroke cut at the line (433 lines), and the box trim (even top at or above the line, bottom kept, the same visible pixels).
  - `test_error_shake` (against a calm twin, 40 seeds, 1 ms steps): nothing before beat + 0.3 s, peak 6 px, 5 sign changes, over after 0.4 s, nothing if error is left before its beat. `test_error_huff` (12 seeds × 400 s): 175 huffs, median gap 24.7 s (12.5-50), the first 15.1 s or more after the beat, the half sines, none outside error or while calm, counted as animating, `eye_dy` moves the strokes. `test_error_gaze` (20 seeds × 300 s, 4,120 saccades): 68 % viewer or down, 32 % darts, every fixation in its range. `test_pose_same`: `eye_dy`.
  - Review fixes (#149): `test_error_exit_continuity` (leave error at the huff's peak, or turn calm on there: the huff runs on exactly; leave mid-shake: the shake runs on against a calm twin), `test_error_overlaps` (tracking from mid-shake: the offset equals the calm twin's, no darts; an act for 200 s in error: no huff, huffs again after it), `test_beat_hidden` (60 seeds × 8 random mood changes with the outline 0-0.6 s late at 60 fps: 306 eye swaps, each rasterised through the lid line, 0 visible pixels), the act-lid beat test asks the blink alone to hide the eyes, and the calm test counts huffs after calm. `test_blink_line` (6 moods × 4 seeds × 120 s, 873 blinks): no ordinary or heavy blink leaves an eye without pixels, and no happy arc splits into two runs of columns in 2,868 happy blink frames, swap blinks included. `test_lid_quantum` pins `BOT_LID_REACH` to half the line's travel. `test_beat_hidden` also drives 800 `bot_act_eyes` costume asks (534 swaps, 0 visible pixels, none pending 0.8 s after the ask), and `test_blink_reopen` asks a swap mid-blink 3,000 times: no reopening eye closes again. The reading cover test asserts on the blinks, not the combined lids. Each fails with its fix taken out.
  - `test_eye_view`: `bot_view.c` itself on host LVGL 9.5 (built from `managed_components`, `LV_CONF_SKIP`, clib malloc) with pthread stand-ins for its FreeRTOS calls (`test/host/view_stubs`; the `rim` task never starts), a 472 × 466 RGB565 display behind a flush callback. Dash open, heavy lid (trimmed canvas), blink shut (the closed-eye line), swap blink shut (hidden), reopened looking elsewhere, half blink; round open and heavy-lidded; angry half-lidded and hidden; happy open, flattened by a blink (not clipped) and hidden: every frame equals the reference stroke cut at the lid line blended on black, with no stale pixel where the eye was. It catches a misplaced alpha plane, a canvas not moved and a colour plane not refilled; skipping the trim gives the same pixels (its own test and the wiring check cover it). `run.sh` builds LVGL in parallel (about 3 s) and skips this with the font tests when `managed_components` is missing.
  - `run.sh` checks the wiring: `eye_raster` trims the box at `bot_lid_line_y` and rasterises with `bot_raster_stroke_below` only, and `eyes_draw` passes each stroke's lid and strip.
- **Host sim** (60 fps, seeds 777, 1234 and 99; a redraw after `bot_pose_same`; eye box px per second: both eye canvases' areas summed over the redraws, as `eye_box` and the lid trim give them; outline moves: a change of the hop's variant or dy). 0.9.76 → 0.9.77:

  | Mood, window | Redraws/s | Eye box px/s | Outline moves/min |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | idle (2-290 s) | 10.5 → 10.5 | 73k → 73k | 0 → 0 |
  | sleepy, awake (2-590 s) | 11.4 → 11.4 | 36k → 31k | 0 → 0 |
  | asleep (610-1200 s) | 2.0 → 2.0 | 4.1k → 2.8k | 0 → 0 |
  | working (2-600 s) | 12.9 → 12.9 | 92k → 92k | 0 → 0 |
  | waiting (0-600 s) | 15.6 → 15.6 | 201k → 205k | 128 → 128 |
  | done (6-600 s) | 10.8 → 10.8 | 121k → 121k | 0 → 0 |
  | error (2-600 s) | 18.5 → 16.3 | 113k → 98k | 0 → 0 |
  | error, 6 s after a change from idle | 21.1 → 22.9 | 127k → 138k | 0 → 0 |

  Sleepy's breathing lid line moves twice as far as the old capsule tip; with the line redrawn per whole pixel it costs what 0.9.76 did (17.5/s and 49k px/s at half pixels; 152k px/s without the trim). The trim takes area off sleepy and asleep. Error's longer fixations draw fewer redraws; its entry adds the shake's 24 frames. BotNext in the review: sleepy 12.1, error 15.8 redraws/s (its metric also counts a 0.5 px move of the lid clip).
- **Cost** (`idf.py size`, origin/main 0.9.76 at a2f81c3 → this branch, same machine, IDF 5.5.5): image 1,722,263 → 1,725,647 B (+3,384, flash `.text`); DIRAM 102,847 → 102,967 B (`.bss` +120: three doubles and a flag in `bot_t`, `eye_dy`, the blinks and `hide` in the static poses, the lid and strip in the eye jobs and strokes); PSRAM `.bss` 49,308 B unchanged; no new buffer. `stack_walk.py`: `eye` 1,472 → 1,584 (margin 976; the clipped raster calls the stroke raster), `orbit` 2,224 → 2,336 (margin 992), `lvgl` 8,608 (margin 1,888) unchanged; every other task unchanged.
- **On the knob**: to do after the release (fps, core 0, invalidated areas and internal RAM in sleepy, asleep and error against 0.9.76; Devices › Demo › Sleepy, Asleep and Error (Hold); the first huff comes 13-51 s into an Error hold).

## Ember over Wi-Fi (step 3, done 2026-10-03)

`main/ember_client.c`: Wi-Fi STA + poll `GET /state` every 2 s on core 1; mood = Ember's render priority on `render.{waiting,errors,running,done}`. Long hold = demo mood for 20 s, then Ember again. Credentials + URL in NVS since #6; the git-ignored `firmware/sdkconfig.secrets` is only the opt-in dev seed since 0.9.16 (see "Secret-free builds"). Ember on the LAN, e.g. `http://ember.local:3627` (from `~/.config/ember/producer.env`).
Internal RAM budget (the hard constraint): Wi-Fi must start **before** the display; `CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_STATIC_RX_BUFFER_NUM=8`, `CONFIG_SPIRAM_TRY_ALLOCATE_WIFI_LWIP=y`, `CONFIG_LV_USE_CLIB_MALLOC=y` (LVGL's fixed 64 KB internal pool freed). Result: ~64 KB internal free, rendering unchanged (avg ~8.5 ms, max ~23 ms). Tearing tracked in GitHub issue #1. Weather animation then: procedural sprites in one sky canvas (replaced by the Temperature face in 0.9.51).
- Starting Wi-Fi after the display failed with `16 rx buffer, actual is 13` (static RX buffers need internal DMA RAM before the draw buffers take it). HTTP timeout 5 s: a lost SYN or segment costs a 1.5 s RTO plus the retry; 3 s was too short.

## Pages: bot, Pomodoro, weather (step 4, PR #4, verified on hardware 2026-10-03)

`main.c` owns a page index; push and turn (button held while turning) changes page, and that press does nothing else. Push vs long push is decided on **release** (a hold followed by a turn is a page change, so long push must not fire at 1.5 s). Inputs are counted as atomics in the esp_timer input callbacks and applied in the LVGL `frame_cb`, which knows the page. The callbacks keep the atomics; what a turn or a release means (dropped by the reset gesture, page step, detent; push, long push, nothing) is `cinder_cfg/press_route.c`, host-tested in `test_legacy.c`. Hidden pages don't draw; the bot keeps its timers off-page and redraws its full pose on return.
- Pomodoro (`components/pomo`, `pomo_client`, `pomo_view`): poll `/v1/pomodoro/state` 1 s running / 2 s idle, actions POST with bearer from a core-1 queue (action chosen from the freshest state, like Ember's `pomoMiddlePress`). Bezel arc = one 472×466 PSRAM canvas, per-second dirty rect ≤24×24 px. Offline only after 3 failed polls (each online/offline flip is a full redraw); since 0.9.40 the shared link state plus the Pomodoro data's own freshness, and offline the page keeps counting (Offline fallback, below). Token: NVS (`dev_tok`, see issue #6 section); dev builds still compile the seed in.
