After a flash, measure with an `input.py swipe_up` loop, diagnostics full, and real swipes.

## Secret-free builds (0.9.16, user decision 2026-10-06)

- No build carries a secret. `CMakeLists.txt` layers `sdkconfig.secrets` only with `-D CINDER_SEED_SECRETS=1` (opt-in dev seed, own build dir); `CONFIG_CINDER_WIFI_SSID/PASSWORD` and `CONFIG_CINDER_EMBER_URL` `depends on CINDER_DEV_SEED`, so they are not even in `sdkconfig` otherwise. `CONFIG_CINDER_EMBER_TOKEN` is gone: the dev seed never wrote a device token, only the master token. A dev-seed image keeps the marker `CINDER-DEV-SEED-BUILD` as a used const read once at boot (not a log string, so a log level can't drop it); Ember refuses it on upload (`dev_seed_build`) and `secret_scan.py` checks it. The build stops (CMake) when `sdkconfig` has `CINDER_DEV_SEED=y` without `-D CINDER_SEED_SECRETS=1`, so a stale `sdkconfig` can't compile secrets in.
- **Token**: a token counts only together with a device id (`cfg_paired`, host-tested). At boot a `dev_tok` is erased from NVS only when reading `dev_id` gives a definite `ESP_ERR_NVS_NOT_FOUND` (`cfg_drop_token`, host-tested; any other read error keeps it); only the `dev_tok` key is erased, nothing else is rewritten (log `token without a device id removed from NVS: not paired`). `config_store_token` returns nothing unless paired, and `http_conn` then sends no `Authorization` header. Unpaired knob: "Not paired" label, Pomodoro note `NOT PAIRED`, no Pomodoro or now-playing commands, no checkin; it still reads the unauthenticated legacy endpoints (`/state`, Pomodoro and weather state, brightness). A paired knob (`dev_id` + device token in NVS, the live knob) is unchanged by an app-flash.
- Code paths that read the token, checked: `http_conn` (bearer for POST/PUT and view GETs), Pomodoro actions (`pomo_client`), now-playing commands, checkin and core dump upload (device only), view polling (device only, `view_policy`). Weather, `/state`, Pomodoro state and brightness reads never sent it.
- **Check**: `tools/secret_scan.py <bin> <elf>` looks for every string value in `sdkconfig.secrets`, `EMBER_TOKEN` from `~/.config/ember/producer.env` and the dev-seed marker; prints names and booleans only; `build_release.sh` and `publish.sh` run it. Host-tested (`test/tools/test_secret_scan.py`, run by `test/host/run.sh`): each source of secrets, escaped values, the marker, empty and non-string values, and that no value is printed. 0.9.16 default local build (`idf.py build`) and `build_release.sh`: all absent. The 0.9.15 local build (secrets layered automatically): Wi-Fi SSID, password, URL and token all present.

## OTA from Ember (#10, Ember#225, 0.9.16)

User decisions (2026-10-06): upload only (Ember never contacts GitHub); Ask first per knob (Automatic opt-in, `release` channel, idle 10 min); rollback after 30 min without a good checkin; input ignored during a download, no cancel; signed images later (non-goal); one-time USB bootloader flash after review; hardware tests on the live server, never repointing the knob.

- **Bootloader**: `CONFIG_BOOTLOADER_APP_ROLLBACK_ENABLE=y`, no anti-rollback (it burns eFuses), `CONFIG_BOOTLOADER_PROJECT_VER=2`. The app reports `ota.rollback` only when the app itself is built with rollback and the bootloader description (`esp_ota_get_bootloader_description`) says version ≥ 2 (`ota_rollback_capable`); Ember offers nothing without it. An app built without rollback writes `UNDEFINED` instead of `NEW` on `set_boot_partition` (no rollback at all), so `ota_client.c` has an `#error` and `main/CMakeLists.txt` stops the build for a `sdkconfig` without rollback or with `BOOTLOADER_PROJECT_VER` < 2. The new bootloader is 20,032 B (19,952 before; 12,736 B left below the partition table at 0x8000). It takes effect only with the one-time `idf.py flash` (`workflow.md`); partition table bytes unchanged (SHA-256 `dbcebbd6…` before and after).
- **Checkin** (keys sorted): `fw_build` (first 4 bytes of `app_elf_sha256` as 8 hex, the running app's), `ota` = `{"image":"valid|pending_verify|new|undefined","last":{"attempt","error","result","version"},"phase":"idle|waiting|rebooting","rollback":bool,"slot":0|1}`, `diag.crash.elf` (8 hex from the core dump summary). `last` lives in NVS until a new attempt starts and carries the offer's `attempt` (Ember ignores a `last` without it, and mints the next id as max(its counter, `last.attempt`) + 1). Lasting declines report `{"result":"failed","error":"refused"}` for that offer's attempt, once: the bad-image guard and the per-boot cap. Transient waits (Pomodoro, input not idle, backoff, not ready) report nothing. Body buffer 1280 → 1536 B (PSRAM).
- **Offer** (checkin reply `ota`, `ota_offer_parse`): `attempt` ≥ 1, `build` 8 hex, `sha256` 64 lower hex, `size` 288 B..4 MiB (and ≤ the slot), `version` strict semver, `url` exactly `/v1/devices/self/firmware/<version>` (the knob uses its stored Ember URL; anything else is ignored), `auto`, `retry`.
- **Start** (`ota_start_decide`, on the ember task after the checkin that carried the offer): rollback bootloader, running image `valid`, Wi-Fi, paired; refuse when `sha256` = NVS `ota/bad` (cleared by `retry:true`) or after 3 started attempts of that attempt id this boot (Ember's Retry is a new attempt and goes at once); wait while a Pomodoro runs or is paused, for an `auto` offer until 10 min without knob input, and in backoff (1, 5, then 30 min after a failure; reset by a new SHA or `retry`).
- **Download** (`main/ota_client.c`, `http_conn_stream`): `GET` on the ember task's kept-alive connection with the device token, read in 4 KB pieces into one 4 KB internal DRAM staging buffer (PSRAM fallback, logged), streaming SHA-256 (mbedtls, HW SHA), `esp_ota_begin(next, OTA_WITH_SEQUENTIAL_WRITES)` (4 KB sector erases as data arrives), `esp_ota_write`. The first 288 bytes are checked before the first write: image magic, chip 9, app desc magic, project `cinder`, version and build (`app_elf_sha256` prefix) = offered, IDF major = the bootloader's (`desc`). The offer is copied when the attempt starts, so nothing later changes the pinned values. `alive()` per piece; before each flash write (so before each sector erase) a measured yield once 200 ms have passed since the last one (`ota_piece_write`; a 1-tick delay every 64 KB until 0.9.48), and the connection's esp_http_client rx buffer is 4 KB for the download only (below, GitHub #61). Network error or 5xx: up to 3 resumes (5, 15, 30 s) with `Range: bytes=<written>-` and `If-Range: "<sha256>"`; a 206 must start at `written`, a 200 restarts from 0 (`esp_ota_abort`, begin again). 5 min cap per attempt (`net`). Then size, SHA-256 (`sha256`, image blocked), a measured yield, `esp_ota_end` (`image`, blocked).
- **Download watchdog** (GitHub #61, 0.9.49): two of four OTAs on 0.9.46-0.9.47 reset through the task watchdog mid-download (IDLE1 not run for 5 s; the retry worked both times). Core dumps: core 1 in `tiT` (`lwip_standard_chksum` ← `tcp_send_empty_ack` ← `tcp_recved`) with `ember` in `esp_http_client_read`, and core 1 in `wifi` (`lmacTxDone`) with `ember` in `esp_partition_erase_range` under `dl_write`; in both, `rim` is runnable mid-raster (`bot_raster_stroke` ← `rim_raster`). Core 1 runs flat out for the whole download: `ember` (prio 3) does the erases and writes with the cache off plus the SHA, `tiT` (18, unpinned, but woken by the core-1 `wifi` task so it mostly preempts there) handles every 512 B receive and its window update, `wifi` and `esp_timer` are pinned to core 1, and the lwIP and Wi-Fi buffers are in PSRAM. The only guaranteed yield was `vTaskDelay(1)` every 64 KB: ~3 s apart at ~20 KB/s against the 5 s idle watchdog, and a 1-tick delay can end at the next tick boundary, under 1 ms later.
  - **Who can take a yield**: a delay in `ember` only takes `ember` off core 1; the scheduler then runs the highest ready core-1 task, and IDLE1 (0) runs only when all of these are blocked: `wifi` (23), `esp_timer` (22, LVGL tick at 1 kHz, encoder and button polls), `tiT` (18), `eye` (5), `reset` (4, factory reset only), `pomo`/`weather` (3, legacy pollers only), `rim` (2), `prov` (2, USB listener), `link` (1, every 5 s), `Tmr Svc` (1, unpinned, no app timers) and `stats` (1, unpinned, every 30 s). Under the OTA face `eye` has no frames to raster, and `rim` is the one that can stay runnable for long: a job is 10 variants at ~0.12 s CPU with a 1-tick delay after each (task watchdog section), ~1.2 s in all, and a job pending when the download starts used to get every yield (both dumps). The network tasks go quiet within a millisecond or two of `ember` stopping to read: with `LWIP_TCP_WND_DEFAULT=5760` the sender stalls after ≤ 5.7 KB in flight, and that is all `tiT` and `wifi` have left to process.
  - **Measured yield**: `ota_piece_write` (`components/ota_policy`, host-built) is what `dl_write` calls for every piece: when `ota_yield_due` (200 ms since the last yield ended) it runs `ota_yield`, then the write (SHA update and `esp_ota_write`, which erases a 4 KB sector each time the data enters a new one, one per piece). `ota_yield` reads `ulTaskGetIdleRunTimeCounterForCore` for the calling core, blocks `OTA_YIELD_TICKS` (2), then keeps blocking 1 tick at a time while `ota_yield_more` holds: counter unchanged and under `OTA_YIELD_MAX_MS` (50) since the yield began. The counter is FreeRTOS's per-task run time (`CONFIG_FREERTOS_GENERATE_RUN_TIME_STATS=y`, 64-bit, esp_timer µs; IDF 5.5.5 `freertos_tasks_c_additions.h`, the same call `diag` uses for `cpu_pct`); it grows when IDLE1 is switched out, so a change means IDLE1 ran in between. What this guarantees: per yield, either IDLE1 ran (it feeds the watchdog in its idle hook) or `ember` stayed off core 1 for 50 ms and continues anyway, so a task that never blocks cannot hang the download. Between yields `ember` uses at most one piece's write past the 200 ms (a write starts < 200 ms after the last yield ended, so the next yield starts within 200 ms plus that write and the next read). Cost with a free core: 2 ticks per ~200 ms (≤ 1 %, no extra ticks); with a task hogging core 1, up to 50 ms per yield (~20 %). The same `ota_yield` runs before `esp_ota_end` and before `esp_ota_set_boot_partition`: each runs `image_validate` (IDF 5.5.5 `esp_image_verify`: mmap, a per-word checksum and HW SHA-256 in 1 KB chunks over the ~1.56 MB image) with no delay, a few hundred ms by estimate (not measured), so it starts on a fresh IDLE1 budget.
  - **`rim` held under the OTA face**: `main.c` `ota_frame` calls `bot_view_rim_hold(true)` when it pauses the pages for the face and `bot_view_rim_hold(false)` when it resumes them. The hold bumps the job generation (`components/bot/rim_job.c`, `rim_job_hold`), so the variant loop stops before its next variant (at most the one in progress, ≤ ~0.12 s CPU at prio 2) and no job starts while held; the release wakes the task to redo the job for the current outline. Until then the face falls back to the base outline, as after any mood change; a successful OTA reboots instead. The weather face's `wxbake` job is held the same way since 0.9.51 (`temp_face_hold` on every OTA frame, see the Temperature face's OTA note). The job carries its outline (`rim_job_new(j, shape, mood)` stores the shape and mood, then bumps the generation; `rim_job_take` reads the generation, then the outline): until 0.9.50 `bot_view_update` bumped the generation, rastered the base outline (~120 ms) and only then stored the shape and mood, so a rim task woken by an earlier notify in that window could stamp variants built from the old outline as current (#63). `test_rim_job_order` (`test_bot.c`) uses host-only race points (`RJ_RACE_POINT(j, n)`, compiled out on the device): `rim_job_new` has one after each atomic step (1-3), and the test takes a job at each; `rim_job_take` has one after the hold check and after each load (4-7), and the test runs a full `rim_job_new` at each. A job taken current must carry the new outline, and the hook counts the steps or loads already done, so a point moved away from its step fails too. Mutants: the old order (bump, then stores) fails at points 1 and 2, a bump between shape and mood at 2, the points moved to the top of `rim_job_new` on the count, take loading the outline before the generation at 5 and 6. `run.sh` greps that `rim_task` (`bot_view.c`, not host-built) does `if (!rim_job_take(&s_rim, ...)) continue;` and `if (!rim_job_live(&s_rim, gen)) break;`, so ignoring either result or dropping the `break` fails; the same run checks that `weather_client_get` reads the timer between `xSemaphoreTake` and `xSemaphoreGive`. Cost of 0.9.50 (#59, #60, #63; `idf.py size`, origin/main 0.9.49 at 91f4f23 → that branch, same machine, IDF 5.5.5, `sdkconfig` from `sdkconfig.defaults`): image 1,557,232 → 1,557,216 B (-16, flash `.text`); DIRAM 102,791 B and IRAM unchanged. `stack_walk.py`: `rim` 1,344 → 1,328 (margin 1,216 → 1,232); every other estimate unchanged; unresolved and tabled call counts move by ±1 on `ember`, `lvgl` and `main` (link layout).
  - **Tests**: `test_yield` (`test/host/test_ota.c`) drives `ota_piece_write` itself on a simulated core-1 clock: 397 pieces of a 1.6 MB image with fast (4-6 ms writes), slow (20-48 ms writes, 2-25 ms reads) and erase-heavy steps; a read gives IDLE nothing (worst case: the network tasks fill it) and is not counted as `ember` work. With the core free, IDLE waits at most 205 / 245 / 202 ms and no extra ticks are spent; with a 10 x 120 ms `rim` job pending (no hold, so the measured yield alone), at most 733 / 838 / 724 ms; with a task that never blocks, the download finishes and each yield is ≤ 50 ms. Mutations, each run on a scratch copy: `ota_yield_more` always false (the unmeasured 2-tick yield) fails the `rim` cases at 14,240 ms (slow) and 8,945 ms (erase); no yield in `ota_piece_write` fails 9 checks; `rim_job_live` ignoring the hold fails `test_rim_job` (`test_bot.c`). `ota_client.c` and `main.c` are not host-built, so `run.sh` checks the wiring by grep, as it does for `ota_client_checkin_done`: `dl_write` calls `ota_piece_write(&io, &d->yield_ms, ...)` and no `esp_ota_write`, `esp_ota_write` appears only in `io_write`, `ota_yield(&YIELD_IO)` sits right before `esp_ota_end` and `esp_ota_set_boot_partition`, and `ota_frame` calls `bot_view_rim_hold` both ways. Reverting `dl_write` to an inline delay and `esp_ota_write` (this PR's first push), or dropping the hold or the yield before `esp_ota_end`, fails `run.sh`.
  - **4 KB rx buffer for the download**: esp_http_client reads the socket in `buffer_size` steps (IDF default 512 B), so a 4 KB piece took 8 `recv` calls, each a `tcp_recved` message to `tiT` with its window-update ACK. `http_conn_set_rx_buf` (owner task) sets the size of the next handle and frees the current one on a change; `run_update` sets `OTA_RX_BUF` (4096) before `dl_begin` and 0 (IDF default) after the download, success or not, so the ember connection is rebuilt twice per OTA (two TCP connects; the second, after a success, costs a reconnect before the reboot, kept for one code path) and runs with 512 B the rest of the time. The buffer is a plain `malloc` in `esp_http_client_init`; under `SPIRAM_MALLOC_ALWAYSINTERNAL=16384` it is internal RAM (if internal heap is short it falls back to PSRAM rather than failing), so a permanent 4 KB would cost 3.5 KB of internal heap on every boot; for the download only it is +3,584 B internal during the download (with the 4 KB staging buffer, ~8 KB of OTA buffers), against the 27 KB `health_heap` floor and a since-boot internal minimum (`diag.heap_internal_min`) of 54 KB measured on 0.9.28, about +10 KB expected from 0.9.35 (health gate and PSRAM sections below). The new image's gate counts only its own boot; on the old image the download can lower the reported `heap_internal_min` by up to 3.5 KB. `test_http.c` checks the handle is built with 0, then 4096 after the switch, the stream reads the whole body, the same size keeps the handle, and 0 rebuilds it.
  - **`tiT` stays unpinned**: pinning it to core 0 (`CONFIG_LWIP_TCPIP_TASK_AFFINITY_CPU0`) would put a priority-18 task on the core that runs the 60 fps chase at 80-81 % (0.9.11 table above, ~19 % headroom) above the priority-6 LVGL task, and every Wi-Fi ↔ lwIP hand-off would cross cores; 0.9.10 grouped the network tasks on core 1 for that reason. During an OTA core 0 is nearly idle (the face redraws on a whole-percent change), so the gain would be the download only. Revisit if the on-device core-1 numbers still show IDLE1 starved with these fixes.
  - **Cost** (`idf.py size`, origin/main 0.9.48 at 8feaf89 → this branch, same machine, IDF 5.5.5, same `sdkconfig`): image 1,556,451 → 1,557,123 B (+672: flash `.text` +656, `.rodata` +16); DIRAM 102,783 → 102,791 B (`.bss` 16,080 → 16,088, `.data` 23,480 unchanged); IRAM unchanged. `stack_walk.py`: `ember` 5,152 (margin 1,760) unchanged, unresolved 242 → 243 and tabled 216 → 225 (`ota_piece_write` and `ota_yield` call through `ota_piece_io_t`; their targets are listed in `stack_walk.json`); `rim` 1,328 → 1,344 (margin 1,232 → 1,216, `rim_job_take`); every other task unchanged. Runtime: +3,584 B internal heap during a download only; 2 ticks per ~200 ms of download, up to 50 ms per yield while another core-1 task stays ready.
  - **Not measured yet** (deferred: the release goes out and the next real OTAs verify it): ~10 OTAs with no `task_wdt`, some started right after a mood change (an agent switching working → done) so a `rim` job is pending; download time against 0.9.48; core-1 `cpu_pct` and `heap_internal_min` during the download; how long `esp_ota_end` takes; `ota_fault.py` `net` and `sha` paths.
- **Switch**: a plain view poll for a fresh Pomodoro state. Pomodoro running or paused: `phase:"waiting"`, normal UI, boot slot untouched, checked each loop pass. The verified image is recorded as `att_state=ready` (a reset while waiting reports `failed/reset_waiting`, not blocked; until 0.9.37 the load dropped `att_state=3`, so the knob forgot the attempt instead). Else NVS `att_state=boot` first, then `esp_ota_set_boot_partition` (a failure reports `failed/flash`), checkin with `phase:"rebooting"`, "Restarting" on the face, `esp_restart()`.
- **First boot** of the new image (bootloader: `NEW` → `PENDING_VERIFY`; any reset before the mark → `ABORTED`, old slot): mark valid at the first `200` checkin with uptime ≥ 60 s, at least one rendered frame, one good view poll and the health gate passed (`ota_valid_ready`, health gate below); 3 failed marks roll back (`mark_valid`). No such checkin within 30 min: `last={rolled_back,no_checkin}` (or the first failing health reason when a checkin succeeded, below), `esp_ota_mark_app_invalid_rollback_and_reboot()`, deferred up to 60 min more while a Pomodoro runs; if the rollback call fails it is retried after 1, 5, then 30 min, and NVS is written only when the record changes. A power cut after the boot slot was set boots the new image, which verifies and reports `ok`. The old image after a rollback finds `att_state=boot` and its own last invalid partition holding the attempt's build (read only in that case: `esp_ota_get_last_invalid_partition` hashes the whole slot, so later boots skip it); another image there means `interrupted`: `rolled_back/boot` unless a `no_checkin` record is there; `att_state=dl` at boot = `failed/interrupted`. A rolled-back image is blocked (`ota/bad`) until Ember's `retry`.
- **Health gate** (#1, 0.9.29): while the image is pending verification, marking it valid also needs `ota_health_check` (`components/ota_policy`) to pass. Checks, in order, each with its rollback reason (all match `^[a-z0-9_]{1,24}$`, so `ota.last.error` carries them):
  - `health_display`: the panel link check (`link_task`) completed at least once without a confirmed failure at the 40 MHz clock. No completed check yet (all errors) is pending. The check runs on the LVGL task and `link` waits 1 s for it (until 0.9.33 it took the LVGL lock with a 1 s timeout), so an LVGL task stalled before the first completed check leaves the link pending, and the deadline rolls back with `health_display`, not `health_render`. A confirmed failure at 40 MHz (boot: one check plus 2 of 3 rechecks; soak: two in a row, the same rule as the 80 MHz fallback) is a **hard fail**: `ota_client_service` rolls back at once, before the deadline and even before a checkin. A failure at 80 MHz is not a gate failure: its fix is the 40 MHz fallback reboot, held until the image is valid. The mapping is `ota_link_result` (host-tested). Known cost: the hard fail is about the hardware, not the image. A knob that is already on 40 MHz (`fast_link` off or the fallback active) and fails the check there confirms it at boot on every new image, so every update rolls back with `health_display` and is blocked (`ota/bad`) until Ember's retry, though the old image runs on the same panel. By design the hard fail also ignores the Pomodoro deferral and the 60 s rule ("at once"). Marking valid re-reads the link state right before the otadata write and refuses on a fail, so a fail noted between the health snapshot and the mark cannot be accepted (Codex P1 on #6).
  - `health_render`: ≥ 20 refreshes since boot that had pixels to draw (`refr_cb` counts a `LV_EVENT_REFR_READY` only when the refresh had invalidated areas; `REFR_READY` also fires for empty runs) and the 16 ms `frame_cb` LVGL timer ran in the last 30 s (`ota_client_note_loop`, called in `frame_cb` before the OTA face, reset and setup early returns; only the `no_render` and `lvgl_stall` test faults return before it). This proves LVGL rendered and handed areas to the flush; flush errors and a dark panel (bad init, brightness never set) are not seen, only the link check talks to the panel. A "frame in the last 30 s" rule was rejected: LVGL refreshes only after an invalidation (`lv_display_refr_timer` pauses itself), and an idle or sleepy bot, a static page (weather, a paused Pomodoro, now playing paused), the OTA face or setup can go more than 30 s without one. The ~750 refreshes per 30 s baseline was measured in the "working" mood only. Checked: no code path stops the LVGL timer handler. Dimming, night levels and brightness 0 only write the panel's brightness register. Since 0.9.35 the ember task only queues the level (`panel_check_brightness`, never blocks) and the LVGL task writes it, so a stalled LVGL task cannot block the ember task: it keeps checking in, and the deadline rolls back with `health_render` instead of the ember watchdog resetting the knob (Codex P2 on #6; the 90 s ember watchdog still fires if the ember task itself hangs). A level queued during a stall is written when the LVGL task runs again (until 0.9.33: a 1 s lock wait per step and `dim_fade_retry` with a 5 s hold); the adapter's auto sleep is off (`ESP_LV_ADAPTER_DEFAULT_CONFIG`) and nothing calls `esp_lv_adapter_pause` or `sleep_prepare`; USB quiet mode only lowers log levels.
  - Input is **not** gated (user decision 2026-10-08, after review of #6): no passive input signal is both cheap and independent. The indev callback runs every 16 ms but reaches the CST820 over I2C only after an INT, while a finger is down, and once at start, and the chip enters standby 2 s after the last touch; `iot_button` and `iot_knob` keep their poll timers private. A gate on the start read alone could roll back a good image if that read failed. The counters stay and are logged with every gate state change: successful CST820 reads since boot (`bsp_knob_15_md50et_touch_ok`) and whether a real turn or press happened (`ota_client_note_input`).
  - `health_heap`: since-boot minimum free internal heap ≥ 27 KB and minimum largest internal block ≥ 15 KB (`diag_health`; the largest block is sampled at checkins, every 30 s and at boot).
  - `health_stack`: stack high-water mark ≥ 1024 B for `lvgl`, ≥ 512 B for every other task in `diag.c` `TASKS[]`.

  Limits are the `OTA_HEALTH_*` constants in `components/ota_policy/include/ota_policy.h` (one place, host tests use the names), about half of what 0.9.28 measured: heap 27 KB from the lowest internal free heap since boot, 54 KB on the knob running 0.9.28 (Ember app, `diag.heap_internal_min`); largest block 15 KB from about 30-31 KB (the app's documented example ~30 KB, the 0.9.28 log 31 KB); LVGL stack 1024 B from 2,484 B headroom in the 0.9.28 log. The 512 B floor for the other tasks is a margin, not a measured half. Outcome (`ota_verify_rollback`): a hard fail rolls back at once with `health_display`; at the existing deadline (30 min, 2 min in a test build, Pomodoro extension kept) a knob whose checkins succeeded rolls back with the first failing reason; a knob that never had a `200` checkin, or that is healthy but still not valid, keeps `no_checkin`. The knob logs the gate state when it changes (`health pending: health_render (...)`, `health pass (...)`, with the input counters). Firmware cost: counters only (frames, last `frame_cb` tick, successful touch reads (logged only), link state, checkin seen, the USB mark request), the gate input in the PSRAM OTA state, no new task; image +2,484 B.
  - **Override**: CINDER1 `{"op":"ota_valid"}` (every build; `tools/ota_fault.py valid`) marks the pending image valid, skipping only the health checks: it still needs a `200` checkin this boot, 60 s uptime, a frame and a view poll (`ota_override_check`). The USB task only checks the preconditions and posts the request; the ember task (6 KB stack, where the checkin mark already runs) does `esp_ota_mark_app_valid_cancel_rollback`, `ota_rec_valid` and the NVS write on its next loop pass (woken with `ember_client_wake`), and the USB task replies when it is done, so the otadata write never runs on the 3.5 KB `prov` stack and every mark and rollback is on one task. Replies: `ok`; `not_pending` (image not pending verification); `no_checkin` (no `200` checkin this boot); `not_ready` (60 s, frame or view poll missing); `busy` (another `ota_valid` is running, or the ember task did not take the request within 25 s). A pending request (`ota_client_mark_pending`) ends a long-poll at the next idle slice (≤ 1 s) and the wait between polls (checked at least once a second), with or without the wake eventfd (#7; before, a knob without it could answer `busy` during a long-poll). Only those two waits are cut short: while the ember task is in another request (a plain view or state GET, a checkin, a now-playing art fetch, a core dump upload) the request is taken when that one ends, within the 25 s budget; `failed` (the otadata write failed, or the link failed at 40 MHz and the rollback is due). The reply can take up to ~30 s; `ota_fault.py valid` waits 35 s.
- **LVGL liveness watchdog** (#7, 0.9.31): a valid image no longer gets the incidental reset (an LVGL task stuck holding the lock used to block the fade, then trip the ember watchdog). `frame_cb` counts its ticks (`s_loop_ticks`, next to `ota_client_note_loop`); the stats task checks the count every `STATS_PERIOD_MS` (30 s), and `LVGL_STALL_CHECKS` (2) checks in a row without a tick (60-90 s stalled) log `LVGL loop stalled` and `abort()`. Skipped while the image is pending verification (the health gate owns that window: `health_render` or `health_display`, and a reset there would be a bootloader rollback without a reason) and while the OTA face is requested. If the rollback itself fails (`ESP_ERR_OTA_ROLLBACK_FAILED`, retried after 1, 5, then 30 min), the image stays pending, so the watchdog stays off for the rest of that boot. No timer or task: a counter in `frame_cb` and a compare in the stats task. False positives need `frame_cb` itself to stop for a minute: the adapter's auto sleep is off and nothing pauses the LVGL timer handler (health gate notes above).
  - **Attribution**: before the abort, an RTC_NOINIT note (`dev_stall_note_t` in `diag.c`, magic-guarded like the TWDT culprit note) is marked. On the next boot, a `panic` reset with the mark reports `reset_reason` `lvgl_stall` in `diag` and `stats`. A core dump is written only when the coredump partition is empty (`CONFIG_ESP_COREDUMP_FLASH_NO_OVERWRITE`). Then that dump's `diag.crash` is `{"reason":"lvgl_stall","task":"lvgl"}` (`dump_rr` 0x40 in NVS; the dump's own task is `stats`, where `abort()` ran, and every task's backtrace is in it). If an older dump is still waiting for upload, no new one is written: `diag.crash` keeps describing the old dump, and only `reset_reason` shows `lvgl_stall`. Both fields fit Ember's checkin schema (`diag.reset_reason` and `crash.reason` `^[a-z0-9_]{1,24}$`, `stats.reset_reason` `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,15}$`); the macOS app shows unknown reasons raw.
  - **Restart-loop guard** (`dev_stall_boot`, `dev_stall_check`, host-tested): the note counts consecutive stall resets. A `sw` reset (USB reboot, OTA restart) keeps the count; any other reset, including a panic without the mark, clears it. After `DEV_STALL_MAX_RESETS` (3) stall resets in a row the watchdog only logs (`not restarting`). A boot that runs `DEV_STALL_CLEAR_MS` (10 min) without a stall clears the count and re-arms the watchdog in that boot. So a stall that repeats on every boot costs three restarts (about 3-5 min), then the knob stays up with a frozen face: it still checks in, so a fixed image can arrive over the air.
- **Stack floors** (`OTA_HEALTH_STACK_MIN` 512 B) for `eye`, `stats` and `prov` are a margin, not measured. The 30 s stats log prints every tracked task's high-water mark on one line (`stack free B: ember 1880, eye 900, ...`, `diag_stacks_line`, the `diag.c` `TASKS[]` order; `TASKS[]` is `char[][8]` and `IDF_TASKS[]` `char[][10]`, each name checked at compile time so one with no room for its NUL (8+ or 10+ characters) does not build; another `_Static_assert` checks the worst case, every task with a 10-digit value, against `DIAG_STACKS_LINE_MAX` (184 B; 344 B since 0.9.32, which appends IDF tasks after `| idf`), so the line cannot be cut; it replaces the LVGL-only and one-time stats-task headroom lines) so the floors can be measured over USB on a release build; floors unchanged until then. Cost of #7 (fade retry, `ota_valid` wake, watchdog with its restart-loop guard and attribution, stack line): image 1,674,727 → 1,675,731 B (+1,004), DIRAM `.bss` +224 B (mostly the 184 B line buffer, static to spare the 3 KB stats stack), RTC slow memory +16 B (the stall note).
- **Planned reboots are held while pending verification**: the panel link check's 40 MHz fallback (at boot or two failed checks) and a `display.fast_link` change would otherwise reset the new image before it is marked valid, and the bootloader would roll back a good update. `ota_reboot_request` holds them (a fallback outranks a restart) and `ota_reboot_release` runs the held one once the image is valid (host-tested). User-initiated reboots (USB `reboot`, `set_ember`, Improv, factory reset) are not held. If the image never becomes valid and its rollback also fails (`ESP_ERR_OTA_ROLLBACK_FAILED`, retried after 1, 5, then 30 min), a held 40 MHz fallback never runs: the display may glitch (link-check mismatches at 80 MHz) until the image is valid or reflashed. Recover over USB: `app-flash erase-otadata` with a known-good release build (NVS kept). The fallback check then runs again at its next boot.
- **NVS namespace `ota`**, one key per field (`att_state` 0 none, 1 dl, 2 boot, 3 ready, `att_attempt`, `att_sha`, `att_ver`, `att_build`, `last_res`, `last_att`, `last_err`, `last_ver`, `bad`): an older image after a rollback reads what it knows. The field ↔ key table is `ota_rec_load` / `ota_rec_save` in `components/ota_policy` behind a key/value seam (`ota_kv_t`: get/set u8, u32, str, erase); `main/ota_client.c` only supplies the NVS callbacks and the commit. An empty string erases its key, and a missing key, a wrong type or an out-of-range enum loads as zero/empty. `test/host/test_ota_rec.c` round-trips a fully populated record through a fake store (`memcmp`, plus a `_Static_assert` on `sizeof(ota_rec_t)` so a new field fails the host-test build, run in CI, until it is persisted), and pins the key names, types and write order, empty-string erases, missing and wrong-type keys, a record as older images wrote it, and stop-at-first-error. Rule: NVS changes stay additive; a migration the previous version can't read needs its own release step.
- **Face** (`main/ota_face.c`): its own LVGL screen, black, 12 px arc on the Pomodoro ring radius (456 px box, r 222 stroke centre) filling clockwise, title **Updating Firmware** (**Restarting** once the boot slot is set) in Montserrat Bold 36, Ember orange `#FF8A3D`, one line, vertically centred (label 384 px wide, `dy` -2: ink y 215-251 around the arc centre y 232); the target version directly under it (Montserrat 30, `#9A9A9A`, `dy` 38, one line, a long prerelease ends in dots, 9 px below the title's ink); the percentage near the bottom (48, `#F4F4F2`, `dy` 150, ink y 366-400, 48 px above the arc's inner edge). Clearance from the arc's inner edge (r 216) to the farthest ink corner: title 31 px, "100 %" 35 px; 36 px keeps the title on one line with that margin (38 leaves ~22 px). Title and version are set only by `ota_face_show` / `ota_face_restarting`; during the download only the arc and percentage redraw, on a whole-percent change (#61). While it shows, the pages are hidden (bot frame updates stop, weather and now-playing via their hide paths), page dots and wipe stop, and every input (turn, push, touch, swipe, USB `input`, the reset gesture) is dropped, not queued. Brightness unchanged.
- **Title font** (#61): the firmware had no bold font. `main/font_title_bold.c` is Montserrat Bold 36 px, 4 bpp, only the 16 glyphs of the two titles, no kerning (Montserrat keeps it in GPOS). Generated by `tools/gen_title_font.py` (Pillow with FreeType) from the `Montserrat-Bold.ttf` in LVGL's test fonts (SIL OFL 1.1); rerun it when a title or the size changes. Line height, baseline and underline are those of the built-in `lv_font_montserrat_36` (the host test compares them, and checks each title fits `OTA_FACE_TITLE_W` on one line), so a new title does not move the face layout. `test/host/test_title_font.c` decodes every title glyph with LVGL's own `fmt_txt` code, so a title letter missing from the subset fails the host tests. Cost: image +2,412 B at 28 px (font ~2.3 KB `.rodata`); 36 px adds ~1.5 KB (bitmap 2,112 → 3,557 B), DIRAM +0; at run time one more label (the version line) while the face shows. Rejected: faux bold (label drawn twice 1 px apart; soft stems) and LVGL's text outline stroke (vector fonts only).
- **Pure, host-tested** (`components/ota_policy`, `test/host/test_ota.c`): semver, offer parse, start decision table, backoff, per-boot cap, retry, resume delays and response checks (206 offset, If-Range 200 restart, sizes), Content-Range parse, header check against the first 288 bytes of a real 0.9.16 image (`test/vectors/ota_header_0.9.16.bin`), progress, mark-valid conditions, the health gate (every check at its threshold, reason order, hard vs soft fail, the reason charset, the reason in the `ota` JSON), the rollback reason at the deadline, the link check mapping (`ota_link_result`) and when the service reads health (`ota_verify_needs_health`), the `ota_valid` preconditions, rollback timer with the Pomodoro extension, NVS record transitions across simulated resets (interrupted, rolled back by the bootloader, no_checkin kept, USB-flashed meanwhile), the `ota` JSON (the design's example as golden), checkin body key order with `fw_build`, `ota`, `crash.elf`.
- **Dev/test**: `CONFIG_CINDER_OTA_TEST` (default n; `build_release.sh --ota-test`): 2 min rollback timer; `CONFIG_CINDER_OTA_TEST_FAULT` `crash_boot` (abort 20 s after boot), `no_checkin` (no checkins) or `no_render` (10 s after boot `frame_cb` stops running the app and noting the loop tick, and `lv_display_enable_invalidation(false)` freezes the screen; LVGL itself, the LVGL lock, touch, USB and the ember task keep running, so the rollback and USB paths cannot hang; expect `rolled_back/health_render` about 2 min after boot, or about 62 min if a Pomodoro runs: stop any Pomodoro before the hardware test), only while that image is pending verification; `lvgl_stall` is the opposite window, for the stall watchdog: once the image is valid, `frame_cb` freezes as in `no_render`, the watchdog aborts 60-90 s after the freeze (on the boot that marks it valid, the freeze starts at the mark), and the next boot (`diag_stall_reset()`) reports `lvgl_stall` and does not freeze; every other boot freezes again until the image is replaced (let the boot after an abort run 10 min or power-cycle before another run: a `sw` reset keeps the stall count, and after 3 in a row the frozen boot only logs); CINDER1 `{"op":"ota_fault","fault":"net|sha"}` arms a network cut at 50 % or a SHA mismatch for the next download (else `unknown_op`).
- **Cost (static, default build)**: image 1,642,480 → 1,668,976 B (+26.5 KB: esp_ota_ops and image verification, mbedtls SHA-256, the client, face and policy; the estimate was 6-10 KB); DIRAM 102,971 → 103,431 B (+460: IRAM `.text` +356 from IDF's OTA/flash code, `.bss` +88, `.data` +16). During a download: 4 KB internal staging buffer, ~0.6 KB download state in PSRAM, the face's LVGL objects (~1 KB internal). At boot only ~0.7 KB of OTA state in PSRAM.
- **Trust model** (by design): plain HTTP on the LAN with the device token, like every other knob request. The SHA-256 pin and the image come over the same channel, so an on-path attacker on the LAN could swap both. Signed images (ECDSA, `SECURE_SIGNED_APPS_NO_SECURE_BOOT`) are deferred. What holds: the URL is only the offer's relative path on the stored Ember URL; size against the offer, `Content-Length` and the slot; header, version and build before the first write; the whole-image SHA-256 (across resumes) and `esp_ota_end` before `esp_ota_set_boot_partition`; every failure ends in `esp_ota_abort` (`esp_ota_end` frees its own handle); the boot slot is set only after both checks.
- **Not measured yet** (needs the one-time USB step): download and flash time, frame stalls, internal heap minimum during a download, rollback paths on hardware. Test plan in the original PR for cinder#10.

## Task stacks and internal RAM (GitHub #10, 0.9.32)

Goal: size every task stack from its worst case, cut internal RAM waste, never trade away safety. Rule: at least 1 KB over the measured or estimated worst case for every task, 1.5 KB for `lvgl` and `ember`. All figures below are **estimates** from a static model, not proven bounds, and nothing is measured on hardware yet; the paths to exercise are at the end.

**Method.** `firmware/tools/stack_walk.py build/cinder.elf [task …] [--path]` (config `tools/stack_walk.json`) walks the call graph from each task entry and prints, per task, the estimate, the configured stack, the margin and the **indirect call sites** it reached that it cannot resolve statically (counted as 0 B): `unresolved` in functions with no pointer-table entry, `tabled` in functions with one (assumed covered by the listed targets, not checked). A long call whose literal is not a function counts as unresolved, not as no call: mostly a null-guarded call to an undefined weak hook (`tlsf_free`, `tlsf_realloc`, `esp_startup_start_app`), else a register the linear scan carries across a branch join (`listener_task`, `esp_wifi_register_if_rxcb`) or a symbol offset. Frame sizes come from each function's Xtensa `entry a1, N` (equal to `-fstack-usage` for compiled code; it also covers the precompiled Wi-Fi, newlib and ROM code, read from the ROM ELF in `~/.espressif/tools/esp-rom-elfs`). Calls: `call8`, `call0`, and the `l32r` + `callx8` long calls (most calls in a 1 MB image); a register loaded by `l32r` counts as overwritten by any later instruction that writes it (only stores, branches, calls and special-register writes keep it). **Decoding** (GitHub #15): `objdump -d` sweeps each function linearly and loses sync after an unconditional jump followed by alignment padding: it decodes the padding plus the start of the next real instruction as one garbage instruction, so a real call can vanish, and what it decodes depends on the link layout (`l32r` offsets are PC-relative). The tool now walks each function from its entry along fall-through, branch, loop and `jx` jump-table targets. A table counts only when the `jx` register traces back through `l32i reg, x, 0` ← `addx4` (or `slli i, 2` + `add`) ← the `l32r` of the table; an unrelated literal in between is ignored. The table also needs an unsigned bound check on the index on the way in (`bltui`/`bgeui`, or `movi` + `bgeu`/`bltu`), which gives its length; every entry up to that length (at most 1,024) must land in the function. The bound check must be the only way in: the trace goes back only through instructions with one known predecessor, a loop body start or a table case counts as having an unknown predecessor, and tables are traced again once the walk has found every branch. The table is read from the ELF. A target the sweep decoded inside another instruction is disassembled again from that address (`objdump --start-address`). Bytes this walk never reaches (padding, garbage, C++ landing pads) are not scanned. A call there to a symbol start (`call8`, or `l32r` of a function + `callx8`, the `l32r` possibly in the reached code just before) counts as unresolved; any other is dropped as decode garbage. A function with a `jx` whose table it cannot trace or read keeps its whole sweep. A `jx` straight from an `l32r` of an instruction-bus address (the ROM `__call_*` thunks, e.g. `esp_rom_printf`) is a tail-call edge, and so is a branch or `j` to another function's start. A branch into another function's middle (ROM libgcc float helpers share code) is an edge to that whole function, which over-counts its frame. A target the re-disassembly still can't decode, or a branch that lands in no function, counts as unresolved on its function. A `call8`, or an `l32r` + `callx8`, to `<sym+0x..>` past the symbol's `nm` size goes to a stripped static function (Wi-Fi blob). It is decoded as an anonymous function; every other `entry` in that gap that the walk doesn't reach starts a sibling anonymous function. A call to `<sym+0x..>` inside a function counts as unresolved. The tool prints these counts; 0.9.37:
- 5,768 targets re-disassembled, 2 left undecoded; 12 functions kept whole.
- Branches into other functions: 62 edges, 3 unresolved.
- 528 `<sym+0x..>` calls in the sweep. In reachable code, 109 go to anonymous functions and 3 are inside a function; the rest are in bytes the walk never reaches.
- 34 unreachable calls to a symbol start; 286 dropped.

A `-fstack-usage` scratch build found no unbounded dynamic frames in app code (9 `dynamic` frames in IDF: touch point VLA, `spi_flash_mmap_pages`, I2C, ADC, heap init; none on a deep path). Assumptions, all in the JSON:
- **Function pointers** resolve only through listed edges: LVGL timers (`frame_cb`, refresh, indev, anim), event classes in use (obj, label, image, arc) and the app's callbacks, the SW draw unit, draw-buffer, font, bin-decoder and cache handlers, the adapter flush, `tp_read`; HTTP (`on_event`, parser callbacks, `alive`, `coredump_read`, `dl_sink`, OTA ctx callbacks), transport (TCP only: the Ember URL is http only), flash chip and HAL drivers, NVS partition I/O, cJSON hooks; logs go `vprintf` → USB-Serial/JTAG. Any event callback may call any other in-use one. The rest stay unresolved (unresolved / tabled per task, with the checkin `config` depth check: ember 227 / 216, lvgl 140 / 59, prov 170 / 146, reset 146 / 135, stats 105 / 14, pomo 53 / 63, weather 53 / 63, link 15 / 14, orbit 1 / 0, rim 0 / 0, eye 0 / 0; mostly NVS C++ vtables, newlib stdio hooks, Wi-Fi blob ops, LVGL cache/anim/decoder ops, SPI and GDMA HAL).
- **Infeasible edges cut**: argument-check error logs that cannot fire (`esp_cache_get_alignment`, `esp_mmu_vaddr_to_paddr`); `__kernel_rem_pio2` (only for |x| > 2^19·π/2; our angles are a few turns); logs inside flash-driver code (cache off, they take the ROM printf path); `__sbprintf` (stdout is line-buffered, newlib only uses it for unbuffered streams); `abort`/`assert` paths (fatal anyway), including `assert_valid_block` → ROM `ets_printf` (heap-corruption report before `abort`, reachable since the tail-call edges of #15).
- **Recursion and cycles**: a path never repeats a function; a result is cached only when no cut depended on a caller further up, so a node reached from another ancestor is recomputed (the earlier version cached it and could under-count); a cached result is reused only when its worst path shares no function with the current path, else it is recomputed (before, a reused result could repeat a function: `main`'s 5,728 B path ran nine LVGL layout functions twice). The path to a recursion cycle head uses the same rule (before, its cache could under-count); it is then joined with the head's own worst path without checking for shared functions, which can only over-count. Seeded brute-force tests check both walks against exhaustive search on small random graphs. `--path` prints the path behind the reported row, with the recursion budget marked at the cycle head. LVGL event dispatch is cut at **2 entries** per path into `event_send_core` / `lv_event_send`, so a path gets one object-class dispatch plus one list or display dispatch. This is a heuristic, not a bound: any event handler can reach any other through setters, so without a cut the 74-function event cycle makes a path of the whole cycle, but a real nested object event (label `SIZE_CHANGED` → `GET_SELF_SIZE`) is cut too, because a path never repeats a function. Letting each nesting level re-run functions, with the same cut at 2, raises `lvgl` to 8,736 B (margin 1,504, under its 1.5 KB rule) and `main` to 6,064 B (margin 848, under the 1 KB rule); at 3 levels `main` overflows. Those deeper paths are judged infeasible (they repeat `lv_arc_event` → `lv_obj_draw` → drop shadow at every level, which the any-handler pointer table allows); the hardware high-water marks decide. Budgets, added on the deepest path through the cycle head: LVGL redraw 4 object levels (640 B per level; deepest tree: screen → now playing root → album circle → note label); cJSON parse and delete 32 levels (`CJSON_NESTING_LIMIT=32` for the whole build and the host tests, default was 1000; a host test checks 32 parses and 33 does not); cJSON print 5 levels: the only print is `dev_checkin_parse` re-printing the `config` block of Ember's checkin answer, and it drops (one `ESP_LOGW` per `config_version`) a `config` nested deeper than `DEV_CONFIG_MAX_DEPTH` = 4 object/array levels (the block itself is level 1) before printing, so the bound holds whatever the server or the plain-HTTP link sends (4 container levels + their scalar leaves = 5 `print_value` frames). Ember sends 3 (`knobSettings` → `pages[]` → `{id,on}`); a dropped block is not applied and the next checkin retries it (host test in `test_device.c`).
- **Fixed overhead 768 B per task**: coprocessor save area 316 B (+ alignment), the initial exception frame 192 B (`XT_STK_FRMSZ`), one interrupt frame 192 B, window base save area and slack. Calibrated on 0.9.31: the LVGL path through `tri_build` estimates 7,056 + 768 = 7,824 B, measured 7,756 B used.
- Error-path logs count (e.g. lwIP's out-of-memory log in `sys_thread_sem_init`, NVS → `esp_mmu_map` failure). One `ESP_LOG` costs ~1.2 KB of stack (`_vfprintf_r` alone is 800 B), and it sets the worst case of most tasks.

**Per task** (stack bytes; measured = 0.9.31 boot free / used; estimate = path + recursion budget + 768; with the checkin `config` depth check. Builds of the same source used to differ by up to 32 B per task; that was the old cache, fixed. Estimates are from the #15 decoder (0.9.37); the old linear sweep missed real call sites and under-counted, its figure follows "was"):

| Task | Core / prio | Before → after | Measured free (used) | Estimate | Margin after |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ember` | 1 / 3 | 6144 → **6912** | 3044 (3100) | 5056 (checkin answer parsed at 32 levels; without recursion budgets 4944: OTA service → fresh view → Pomodoro action → HTTP → lwIP OOM log) | 1856 |
| `eye` | 1 / 5 | 2560 | 1260 (1300) | 1472 (raster; was 1456) | 1088 |
| `link` | 1 / 1 | 3072 → **4096** (PSRAM since 0.9.35) | 752 (2320) | 2960 (link check → SPI → error log); 0.9.35: 2704 (log line, the check runs on `lvgl`) | 1392 |
| `lvgl` | 0 / 6 | 10240 → **10496** | 2484 (7756) | 8896 (render, event cut at 2 entries, 4 redraw levels; SW draw dispatch → label → glyph image → bin decoder → malloc; was 8368); 0.9.39: 8592 (page table, `page_tick` off this path) | **1600**; 0.9.39: **1904** |
| `prov` | 1 / 2 | 3584 → 5120 → **5376** | 2316 (1268) | 4320 (NVS write → entry erase → partition write → mmap error log; was 4000, CINDER1 parse at 32 levels) | **1056** |
| `rim` | 1 / 2 | 6144 → **2560** (PSRAM since 0.9.35) | 4988 (1156) | 1328 (ring + raster; was 1312) | 1232 |
| `stats` | any / 1 | 3072 → **3840** (PSRAM since 0.9.35) | 568 (2504) | 2640 (log line); 0.9.35: 2688 (deletes `orbit`) | 1152 |
| `pomo` (Ember without view) | 1 / 3 | 5120 → **5632** (PSRAM since 0.9.35) | — | 4384; 0.9.35: 4368 | 1264 |
| `weather` (Ember without view) | 1 / 3 | 6144 → **5120** (PSRAM since 0.9.35) | — | 3936; 0.9.35: 3920 | 1200 |
| `orbit` (boot, ~5 s) | 1 / 1 | 2560 → **3328** (PSRAM since 0.9.35) | — | 2144 | 1184 |
| `reset` (factory reset) | 1 / 4 | 3072 → 4864 → **5120** | — | 4032 (NVS write → entry erase → mmap error log; was 3632) | **1088** |
| `main` (IDF, freed after `app_main`) | 0 / 1 | 3584 → 6912 → **7232** | — | 6192 (LVGL init events reaching the SW draw dispatch; was 5616) | **1040** |
| `esp_timer` (IDF) | 1 / 22 | 3584 → **4096** | — | 2768 (Wi-Fi reconnect log; Wi-Fi blob timers unresolved) | 1328 |
| `sys_evt` (IDF) | 0 / 20 | 2304 → **3840** | — | 2784 (`esp_netif` connected log) | 1056 |
| `ipc0` / `ipc1` (IDF) | 0, 1 / 24 | 1280 (IDF default, exempt) | — | 1168 (mostly the fixed overhead) | 112 |
| `Tmr Svc` (IDF) | any / 1 | 2048 → **2304** | — | 1136 (no app timers) | 1168 |

**0.9.38 restores the rule** after #15 raised the estimates (`lvgl` margin was 1344 < 1.5 KB, `prov` 800, `reset` 832, `main` 720 < 1 KB): `lvgl` 10496, `prov` 5376, `reset` 5120, `main` 7232 (`CONFIG_ESP_MAIN_TASK_STACK_SIZE`): +512 B internal RAM steady (`lvgl`, `prov`), +1088 B peak (`reset` is transient, `main` is freed after `app_main`).

**`ipc0`/`ipc1` are exempt from the 1 KB rule** (user decision 2026-10-08): no app code runs there, IDF sizes them for its own IPC callbacks, and their 1168 B estimate is mostly the model's fixed 768 B overhead; they stay at IDF's 1280 B. Not estimated: `tiT` (3072, lwIP callbacks unresolved), `wifi` (blob), `IDLE0`/`IDLE1` (1536), the ISR stacks (2096 B per core, `port_IntStack`). The stats log line now appends the IDF tasks after `| idf` (`esp_timer`, `ipc0`/`ipc1`, `sys_evt`, `tiT`, `Tmr Svc`, `wifi`), so they can be sized from field data.

Since 0.9.35 `rim`, `stats`, `link`, `orbit`, `pomo` and `weather` have PSRAM stacks (architecture follow-ups below); the other app stacks are internal RAM (`xTaskCreate*`; the adapter's `stack_in_psram` is false). The same estimate of 0.9.31 found gaps: `prov` (4000 > 3584), `reset` (3632 > 3072), `main` (5728 > 3584; over-counted by the old cache, 5552 with the fix) and `sys_evt` (2784 > 2304) could overflow on an error-log path; `link` and `ipc` had 112 B, `stats` 240 B, `esp_timer` 816 B; `rim` would have overflowed (7216 > 6144) had it built the triangle table before the LVGL task did. Tracked in `diag.c` `TASKS[]`: all app tasks except `orbit` (deletes itself before the first checkin) and `reset` (reboots within a second); `main` is gone after boot.

- **Main fix: the triangle table.** `bot_shape.c` built it lazily on whichever task drew the first outline, with `double raw[720]` (5.76 KB) on that task's stack: the LVGL task in practice, where it set the 7756 B high-water mark. Now `bot_shape_init()` builds it once from `bot_view_create`, before the `rim` task exists and before the LVGL timer runs; the scratch is static in PSRAM, and `bot_body_ring` asserts the table is ready.
- **stats**: its frame 336 → 144 B (the stats, refresh-kind and Wi-Fi structs and the stack line are static in PSRAM); the rest is the log path.
- `lvgl` is sized from the estimate (10496 since 0.9.38: 8896 + 1.5 KB); the measured high-water mark is far lower (5316 B free at its worst on 0.9.34), so a measured size could be smaller.
- **Health floors** (`OTA_HEALTH_STACK_MIN` 512 B, `OTA_HEALTH_STACK_LVGL` 1024 B) unchanged: every gated task now has ≥ 1.1 KB estimated headroom and should idle well above the floor (eye ~1.26 KB, rim ~1.4 KB, stats ~1.5 KB, link ~1.8 KB expected), so the floors still catch a regression without tripping on a healthy image.
- **Stale local `sdkconfig`**: an existing `firmware/sdkconfig` keeps the old stack sizes and features; delete it before building (CI and `build_release.sh` start fresh).

**Removed or moved** (`sdkconfig.defaults`, memory audit block): HTTPS in `esp_http_client` (the URL validator already refused `https`), the certificate bundle, Wi-Fi SoftAP (STA only) and WPA-Enterprise (open or PSK only), LVGL widgets and features never created (all but obj, label, image, canvas, arc; simple theme, flex, grid, observer, examples, demos). Static buffers to PSRAM: the triangle-table scratch, `parse_state` sessions (384 B), the legacy Pomodoro body (512 B), the stats task's structs and stack line.

**Before → after** (`idf.py size`; runtime numbers are estimates until measured):

| | 0.9.31 | 0.9.32 | Δ |
|---|---|---|---|
| Image | 1,675,731 B | 1,543,883 B | −131,848 (LVGL −76 KB; Wi-Fi libraries −50 KB for SoftAP and WPA-Enterprise) |
| DIRAM (static) | 103,687 B (`.data` 23,432, `.bss` 17,088) | 102,519 B (23,352, 16,000) | −1,168 |
| IRAM | 16,384 (`.text` 15,356) | unchanged | 0 |
| PSRAM `.bss` | 42,060 B | 49,092 B | +7,032 |
| App task stacks, normal run | 34,816 B | 35,328 B | +512 |
| IDF task stacks (esp_timer, sys_evt, Tmr Svc) | 7,936 B | 10,240 B | +2,304 |
| Internal heap free, normal run | — | — | ≈ −1.6 KB (stacks +2.8 KB, static −1.2 KB) |
| Boot transient | | | `main` +3.3 KB, `orbit` +0.75 KB on top: boot minimum may drop up to ~6 KB (54 → ~48 KB; floor 27 KB) |

**Waste found, not changed** (needs a decision or hardware measurement):
- `CONFIG_SPIRAM_MALLOC_ALWAYSINTERNAL=16384`: every malloc ≤ 16 KB lands in internal RAM (HTTP client buffers ~2.5 KB per kept handle, cJSON trees, LVGL objects). Lowering it, or cJSON hooks on a PSRAM allocator, could free 5-15 KB, after auditing plain-malloc users that need internal or DMA memory.
- cJSON builds a tree of small internal allocations for every changed view, checkin answer and settings blob: heap churn and fragmentation; recursion grows with nesting.
- The newlib log path (~1.2 KB) is most tasks' worst case. Nano formatting would cut it but drops `%lld` and floats (used in the stats lines).
- Wi-Fi: 8 static RX buffers (12.8 KB internal DMA), AMPDU RX/TX on; fewer buffers or AMPDU off may suit a polling client, but only with an on-air test.
- mbedTLS TLS and X.509 (~35 KB flash) stay linked: `esp_http_client` references esp-tls and the SSL transport even with HTTPS off.
- `weather_view` `s_scene` + draw list (1.3 KB `.bss`): gone with `weather_view` in 0.9.51 (Temperature face). The bot raster scratch, `added`, `st`, `ex`, `ey` (3.3 KB) stay internal: per-frame hot path.
- The legacy `pomo` and `weather` tasks idle forever after Ember's view returns (10.5 KB); deleting them on the switch back would return it. Done in 0.9.35 (below).
- `label_draw` creates and deletes a temporary canvas object for every host-label change (rare).

**Architecture findings** (analysis only; ranked by value / effort):
1. **Done in 0.9.35** (below). **PSRAM stacks** (`xTaskCreatePinnedToCoreWithCaps(…, MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM)`; `CONFIG_SPIRAM_ALLOW_STACK_EXTERNAL_MEMORY` is already on). Only for tasks that never touch flash (no NVS read or write, no OTA, no partition I/O: flash ops disable the cache, and IDF rejects a PSRAM stack there). Candidates: `rim` (pure compute), `stats` (logs, heap queries; its `abort()` panics on the core-dump stack, which is internal for this reason), `link` (SPI polling with a 32 B buffer on the stack; the SPI driver bounces non-DMA buffers), `orbit`; `eye` after a frame-time check. ~10 KB internal (13 KB with eye, +3.3 KB at boot), small effort, low-medium risk. Not candidates: `ember`, `prov`, `reset`, `main` (NVS/OTA), the LVGL task (10 KB, but every stack access goes through the cache at 60 fps; measure first), Wi-Fi/lwIP.
2. **Done in 0.9.35** (below). **One owner for the panel and LVGL.** Lock takers after boot: the LVGL task, `link` (panel read every 5 s, waits up to 1 s), `ember` (dimmer brightness writes with a timeout), `prov` (frees the snapshot buffer). The OTA face, views and snapshot already pass messages. A small request queue serviced by the LVGL task between frames (brightness, link read, snapshot release) removes all other lock holders: no memory change, medium effort, removes the "task stuck holding the lock" class behind #7's stall watchdog.
3. **Re-measure `lvgl`, then shrink to ~8 KB**: ~2 KB, trivial, after this release.
4. **cJSON in PSRAM done in 0.9.35** (below; the threshold stays). **Internal malloc threshold / cJSON in PSRAM** (above): 5-15 KB, medium effort and risk.
5. **Done in 0.9.35** (below: delete on the switch back). **Legacy pollers**: delete `pomo`/`weather` on the switch back, or poll the legacy endpoints round-robin from the ember task on its connection (same host): 10.5 KB stacks + ~5 KB for two HTTP handles, only while Ember has no view; medium effort. Dropping the fallback altogether is a product decision (old Ember servers).
6. **Merge `link` and `stats`** into one low-priority housekeeping task (5 s tick, stats every 6th): ~4 KB internal (none if item 1 is done first); the link check can slip by a stats log (ms, more if the USB console blocks). Small-medium effort.
7. **A streaming JSON tokenizer** (fixed token pool in PSRAM) for the view: no allocation churn, no recursion; medium-high effort.
8. **Kept separate on purpose**: `eye` (LVGL waits on it every frame; merging `rim`'s 120 ms jobs would stall frames), the single `ember` task (long-poll, checkin, OTA, core dump, Pomodoro pushes already share one task and one connection), `prov` (must answer USB while `ember` blocks for up to 20 s).

**Exercise on hardware** (0.9.32; watch the 30 s `stack free B:` line and `diag.stack_free`/`heap_internal_min` in the checkin):
- Boot to the bot face (`main`, `orbit`), then the 60 fps chase and a USB `snapshot` (`lvgl`: free should rise well above 0.9.31's 2.5 KB).
- Mood changes for the outline variants (`rim`, expect ~1.4 KB free).
- USB: `status`, `diag_override`, Improv Wi-Fi with the same network, and `set_ember` with the same URL and token (`prov` NVS path; do not point the knob elsewhere).
- An OTA download from channel `test` and a core dump upload (`ember`), a Wi-Fi drop and reconnect (`esp_timer`, `sys_evt`, `tiT`).
- Ember without the view (proxy answering 404) for `pomo` and `weather`.

### Architecture follow-ups (#12, 0.9.35)

**PSRAM stacks: `rim`, `stats`, `link`, `orbit`, and the legacy pollers `pomo`, `weather`** (`xTaskCreatePinnedToCoreWithCaps(…, MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM)`; the TCB stays internal; `CONFIG_FREERTOS_TASK_CREATE_ALLOW_EXT_MEM=y` is the S3 default). A PSRAM stack must never be in use while the cache is off, so each task was checked for flash access (NVS, OTA, partitions, `esp_core_dump`, `spi_flash`):
- `rim`: raster into PSRAM buffers, `bot_body_ring`, `vTaskDelay`. No flash. `orbit`: table fill and `vTaskDelay`, then it sets a flag and suspends itself; the `stats` task deletes it by handle on its next 30 s tick (`vTaskDeleteWithCaps(handle)`). A self-delete (`vTaskDeleteWithCaps(NULL)`) would create a helper task with an internal stack and `abort()` if that create failed (Opus L1 on #16); deleting by handle needs no helper. Stack 3328 B (PSRAM), estimate 2144.
- `stats`: logs, heap queries, `esp_wifi_sta_get_ap_info`, `uxTaskGetStackHighWaterMark`, `diag_stall_check` (RTC no-init memory only). Its `abort()` on an LVGL stall panics on the task stack (PSRAM, cache on); the core dump then switches to its own 1792 B internal stack before it writes flash (`CONFIG_ESP_COREDUMP_USE_STACK_SIZE`, on whenever external task stacks are allowed), and `esp_restart_noos` moves SP to internal RAM before it disables the cache.
- `link`: settings copy (RAM), OTA health notes (atomics), `esp_restart` (shutdown handlers: Wi-Fi stop, timekeeping; no flash; SP moved as above) and, until the single panel owner below, the panel read over SPI (the driver bounces non-DMA buffers; cache on).
- `pomo`, `weather`: HTTP over lwIP, cJSON, `config_store` getters (RAM copies under a spinlock), atomics. No flash (call graph checked as above).
- Not moved: `eye` (frame-time check first), `lvgl`, `ember`, `prov`, `reset` (NVS or OTA).
- Internal RAM: −10,496 B of stacks in a normal run (`rim` 2560, `stats` 3840, `link` 4096), −3,328 B more at boot (`orbit`), −10,752 B while Ember has no view (`pomo` 5632, `weather` 5120). The TCBs stay internal. PSRAM +13,824 B, +10,752 B more while polling legacy endpoints. PSRAM stacks are slower than internal ones; none of these tasks is on the frame path.

**One panel and LVGL owner** (`main/panel_check.c`, pure part `components/panel_req`, host-tested in `test_panel.c`). After boot only the LVGL task takes the LVGL lock and talks to the panel; `frame_cb` calls `panel_check_frame()` first, also while the OTA test fault `no_render` freezes the screen.
- Brightness: `panel_check_brightness(level)` stores the newest level (any task, never blocks, also before `panel_check_init`); the next frame writes it. A failed write is retried every 100 ms unless a newer level arrived. The dimmer's 1 s lock wait and 5 s retry hold are gone (`dim_fade_retry` removed, and with it the BSP's lock-taking `set_brightness_level[_wait]`): if the LVGL task stalls, the newest level waits and is written when it runs again. The startup level goes the same way.
- Link check: `panel_check_run(seed, raw, 1000)` posts a ticket and polls for its result every 10 ms, at most 1 s; the LVGL task runs the loopback as steps over frames (read 0x52, write the level XOR 1 or XOR 2 by seed parity, ≥ 60 ms later read back and restore, ≥ 60 ms later read back), so it never sleeps in the LVGL task. A timeout returns -1 (counted as an error; `ota_link_result` and the fallback see nothing, as with the old lock timeout); the late check still finishes and restores the level, and its result is dropped. A newer ticket replaces one not yet started. A check starts only 60 ms after the last panel write returned (timed after the SPI call, so a write that blocks behind a flush does not eat into the settle time), because RDDISBV can lag a write (the fake panel in `test_panel.c` models that lag): without the wait, a check that starts in the frame of a dimmer step reads the old level and its restore puts the old level back. Brightness requests wait while a check waits or runs (≤ ~200 ms), so the old -2 ("dimmer wrote meanwhile") is gone. A check that fails half-way writes the old level back; if that write fails too, the old level is queued like a brightness request and retried every 100 ms (a newer level wins).
- Snapshot: `screen_snap_release()` only marks the buffer; the LVGL task frees it on its next frame, also while `no_render` freezes the screen. A new `snapshot` waits for that within its timeout.
- The LVGL stall watchdog is unchanged (`s_loop_ticks` in `frame_cb`). Estimates: `lvgl` 8368 B (the check adds 1968 B from `frame_cb`, below the render path), `link` 2704 B (no SPI on its stack).

**cJSON in PSRAM** (`app_main`: `cJSON_InitHooks` with `heap_caps_malloc_prefer(n, 2, MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM, MALLOC_CAP_DEFAULT)`, `free`), chosen over lowering `CONFIG_SPIRAM_MALLOC_ALWAYSINTERNAL`:
- Scope: the hooks move only cJSON's own allocations (nodes, key and value strings, print buffers); the threshold would move every `malloc` ≤ 16 KB of the app, LVGL (`LV_USE_CLIB_MALLOC`), lwIP, `esp_http_client` and IDF drivers, and would need an audit of every plain `malloc` that must stay internal (cache off, DMA, ISR). cJSON users are all task code (`knob_view`, `device_api`, `knob_settings`, `cinder_line`, `ota_policy`, `np`, the ember, pomo and weather clients); none runs with the cache off or hands a cJSON buffer to DMA. Printed strings go to `esp_http_client` (lwIP copies them), USB (copied into the driver's ring buffer) or NVS (`esp_flash_write` copies non-DRAM sources through a bounce buffer).
- PSRAM full: the hook falls back to internal RAM, as before.
- No realloc: `cJSON_Hooks` has no realloc slot, and cJSON uses `realloc` only with the default `malloc`/`free`. With hooks a print buffer grows by allocate, copy, free (doubling from 256 B) and ends with one exact-size copy, so printing peaks at about 3× the printed size during growth and 2× at the final copy. The only print is `device_api.c` re-printing the checkin `config` block (a few hundred bytes to ~2 KB), so the peak stays a few KB and is in PSRAM unless PSRAM is full.
- Effect: every parsed view, checkin answer, settings blob and CINDER1 line now builds its tree in PSRAM. The 410 B view fixture of `test_view.c` makes 78 allocations; bigger views (now playing, hosts) scale with their size. This lowers the internal peak and churn (`heap_internal_min`, `heap_largest_min`), not the idle free figure. Parse time in PSRAM is not measured (one view per change).
- The threshold stays at 16 KB: the remaining small internal mallocs (HTTP client buffers, LVGL objects) are per-frame or long-lived; moving them needs the audit above and a frame-time measurement.

**Legacy pollers end when the view is back** (`net_policy/legacy_task.c`, host-tested in `test_net.c`). The ember task decides on every pass (`pomo_client_legacy(legacy)`, `weather_client_legacy(legacy)`): on at a fallback, off when the view returns, as before. The lifecycle is a lock-free state (gone, run, stop, parked):
- Off: the task notices within one wait (pomo: one poll period or a queued action, which it puts back for the ember task; weather: 1 s slices; a request in flight finishes first), frees its HTTP handle (it owns it) and parks (suspends itself); only after a successful park does it report its stack as gone to `diag`. It holds no lock when it parks.
- Reap: the ember task's next pass deletes the parked task by handle (`vTaskDeleteWithCaps`, no helper task), frees the weather buffers (16 KB body, connection struct; allocated by the ember task before the create), clears the stack figure and logs `legacy poll task removed`. A park that lands between the ember task's state load and its compare-and-swap is still reaped before a new task is created (host test `test_legacy_park_race`). No task deletes itself, so nothing is freed behind a reader.
- On again before it parked: it keeps polling; no second task. On after it parked: reap, then a new task. A failed create or allocation backs off 5 s, doubling to 5 min (no `assert`; Opus L3).
- Stack figures: `pomo` and `weather` report their own high-water mark to `diag` at the top of each loop and again after the poll's HTTP, parse and log work, before they wait (`diag_note_stack`; -1 once parked or removed). `diag_health`, `diag_fill` and the stats line read those values and never call `xTaskGetHandle` for a task that another task deletes (Codex High, Opus L2: the old self-delete could free the TCB between `xTaskGetHandle` and `uxTaskGetStackHighWaterMark`).
- Stacks in PSRAM (above), so a view that flaps no longer allocates 10.7 KB of internal stack per cycle; only the two TCBs (~0.7 KB) come and go (Opus L4). Rate-limiting re-creation was the other option; it would still churn internal RAM and delay the fallback.
- Old Ember servers keep the fallback: with no view the tasks are created at the first fallback and never stopped.

**Before → after** (main at 0.9.33 → 0.9.35, `idf.py size`; runtime figures are estimates, not measured):

| | main (0.9.33) | 0.9.35 | Δ |
|---|---|---|---|
| Image | 1,544,175 B | ≈ 1,546,600 B | ≈ +2.4 KB (± ~100 B between build environments: 1,546,591 here, 1,546,704 in a reviewer's build) |
| DIRAM (static) | 102,519 B (`.bss` 16,000) | 102,663 B (16,008) | +144 |
| App task stacks in internal RAM, normal run | 35,328 B | 24,832 B | −10,496 (`rim`, `stats`, `link` in PSRAM) |
| Boot transient | `orbit` 3,328 B internal | 3,328 B PSRAM until the first stats tick (30 s) | −3,328 |
| Ember without the view | `pomo` + `weather` 10,752 B internal | PSRAM | −10,752 |
| After a view fallback and back | pollers idle: 10,752 B stacks + pomo HTTP handle | reaped | ≈ −13 KB (stacks were internal) |
| cJSON trees | internal | PSRAM | lower `heap_internal_min`; size depends on the documents |
| PSRAM | | | +13,824 B stacks (+10,752 B while polling legacy endpoints) |

Exercise on hardware (0.9.35; the pre-merge Test-channel build of this branch was uploaded as 0.9.34): the stats line `stack free B:` for `rim`, `stats`, `link` (PSRAM now, expected free ≈ before: rim ~1.2-1.4 KB, stats ~1.2 KB, link ≥ 1.4 KB); `heap_internal_min` and the `internal free` in the net line (expect ~+10 KB over 0.9.33); the link soak lines (`link check N at 80 MHz: 0 bad`, `link soak: … 0 mismatches, 0 errors`) and `ota.image` turning `valid` after the update (health gate: link ok); brightness fades from Ember's dimmer; a USB `snapshot` twice in a row; Ember without the view and back (proxy answering 404, then normal) for `legacy poll task removed` (pomo and weather), then `pomo`/`weather` gone from `stack free B:` and from the checkin `diag.stack_free`; a stall abort, if one ever fires, still writes its core dump.

## Page table (#25, 0.9.39)

Pages are one descriptor table, `PAGES[]` in `components/pages/pages.c` (const, flash): `{id, view_block, flags, show, input, frame}`. It is the only list of page ids. The component also owns page navigation, as pure C with no LVGL: `pages_nav_t {order, n, pos, page}` (`pos` is the slot in the settings order, `page` the table index shown). `pages_settings` orders the table with `knob_settings_page_order` (settings `pages[]` order and `home`; an unknown id stays in the settings but is not shown; when the shown page is disabled, or on the first settings, it shows `home`). `pages_step` (push and turn, swipe), `pages_show`, `pages_resume` (after the OTA face) and `pages_hide_all` call every page's `show`. `pages_dispatch` routes one frame's input to the shown page's `input`, and `pages_frame` calls its `frame`. `main.c` keeps one `pages_nav_t` and calls these from `frame_cb` and `page_tick`. No behaviour change intended.

- Hooks: `page_ops.h` declares them; `main.c` defines them on the LVGL task (`page_bot_*`, `page_pomo_*`, `page_weather_*`, `page_np_*`). Weather has no `frame`: its update runs every frame on every page, as before. Its `input` (`PAGE_TURN`, 0.9.53) takes detents to switch faces ("Weather face switching"). `page_bot_show(true)` forces a bot redraw (`s_drawn = false`, which `show_page` did for every page before; only the bot reads it).
- Input routing (`pages_route`): a page without `input` drops all input; without `PAGE_TOUCH_RAW` taps count as pushes; without `PAGE_TURN` detents are dropped (Pomodoro ignored them before too); now playing (`PAGE_TOUCH_RAW | PAGE_TURN`) gets taps as touches and wakes. `PAGE_GLINT` (bot) runs the working glint and chase. The bot simulation (mood, chase, pose) still runs every frame; only the bot draw is the bot page's `frame`.
- One order change: Pomodoro's and now playing's per-frame update now runs after the bot pose step instead of before it; the two share no state.
- `pages_ids()` lists the compiled ids in table order; it is the checkin's `caps.pages` (#27, capabilities section).
- `view_block`: the knob view key each page reads (`mood`, `pomo`, `weather`, `nowplaying`).
- Tests (`test_pages.c`, recording stubs in `page_stubs.c`): the table against `knob_settings` defaults and Ember's `view_full.json` and `config_default.json`; what each page's `input` receives for nonzero turns, pushes, long pushes, taps and wakes; dispatch and frame following `page`, not `pos`; show and hide order on a switch, pause and resume; step with a reordered list; settings reorder, disabled shown page, unknown home. Each check was run against a deliberate mutant of `pages.c` (no routing, `pos` for `page`, no hide, fallback to slot 0, keeping a disabled page, Pomodoro detents, now-playing taps as pushes, capped pushes, step and resume by slot, frame by slot) and fails on it.
- Add a page: Ember first (its `config_default.json` default page list and the view block, then the fixture sync, `docs/workflow.md`), because `test_pages.c` requires Ember's default list to equal the table. Then one table row and `PAGES_N` in `pages.c`/`pages.h`, its hook declarations in `page_ops.h`, the hooks in `main.c`, recording stubs in `test/host/page_stubs.c`, and its hooks in `tools/stack_walk.json` (`show` under `pages_show`, `pages_hide_all`, `pages_resume`, `pages_settings`, `pages_step`; `input` under `pages_dispatch`; `frame` under `pages_frame`).

**Cost** (`idf.py size`, origin/main 0.9.38 at 6c6e7d9 → this branch): DIRAM 102,663 → 102,655 B (`.data` 23,432 → 23,432, `.bss` 16,008 → 16,000), IRAM unchanged, image 1,547,440 → 1,547,664 B (+224). `stack_walk.py` `lvgl`: 8896 → 8592 B (margin 1600 → 1904): `page_tick` is `noinline`, so its 576 B frame (input, frame context, weather sample, chase reference pose) is no longer under `frame_cb`'s deepest path (`screen_snap_frame`; `frame_cb`'s frame is 160 B, was 464). Without the split `frame_cb` grew to 656 B and the margin fell to 1408, under the 1.5 KB rule. With the hook entries in `stack_walk.json` every task's unresolved indirect-call count equals origin/main's. These are estimates; boot time, task stacks (`diag.stack_free`) and heap on the knob are not measured yet (on-device check in the PR).

## Offline fallback (#26, 0.9.40)

Principle: Ember stays the only authority. Offline the knob shows what it last knew, labelled, and never advances shared state, decides or replays a press. Firmware only; server and protocol unchanged.

**Link state** (`components/link_state`, pure C, host-tested in `test_link.c`):
- Levels: `BOOT` (no answer yet; pages treat it as not offline), `ONLINE` (the last counted request succeeded), `DEGRADED` (1-2 counted failures in a row; pages show no change), `OFFLINE` (3 or more, `LINK_OFFLINE_AFTER_FAILS`, the old threshold). `BOOT` goes `ONLINE` on the first answer and `OFFLINE` after 3 failures, so a normal or post-OTA boot never shows the offline glyph or drops a press. `offline_since_ms` (monotonic) is the first failure of the streak that went offline; it reaches the transition log only.
- What counts (`link_counts`, table-tested): 200 and 304 with a body the knob understood are `OK`; a transport error (`-1`: connect, DNS, timeout, reset), 5xx, 3xx other than 304 and a 200 the knob cannot parse are failures; 401/403 (they lead to the existing "Not paired" state through checkins and the view fallback), 404, 429, other 4xx and an aborted long-poll change nothing. A loop pass without Wi-Fi is a failure, except in `BOOT` (association and DHCP take seconds; "Can't join" covers a knob that never joins).
- Wrong host (#39): until 0.9.43 a URL that answered only uncounted statuses (a captive portal's 302, a proxy's 301, an HTML 200 from another host) kept the knob in `BOOT` or `ONLINE` forever. Ember never redirects the knob's routes (the stored URL has no trailing slash, so no `ServeMux` clean-path 301) and its `/state` and `/v1/pomodoro/state` are always JSON, so three such answers in a row now go `OFFLINE`. A body that does not fit the buffer (`cap - 1`, 16 KiB for `/state`) is dropped: `http_conn` drains up to `2 × cap + 4 KiB` (about 36 KiB), and past that stops reading and drops the socket (unread bytes are left on it; a body that ends exactly on a read past the limit keeps it). Either way it keeps the status, leaves the buffer empty and sets `.truncated`, so no caller can act on a prefix (a checkin reply cut after a complete `new_token` or `config` would otherwise be applied, since `cJSON_Parse` ignores trailing bytes). Only `/state` turns a truncated 200 into a skip (`ember_state_answer`, which `ember_client.c` calls): Ember sends `/state` with every session and no limit, so a valid but oversized `/state` must not take a reachable Ember offline (until #44 a body past the drain limit returned `HTTP_FAIL_BODY` and counted). A truncated checkin is a failed checkin (`dev_checkin_answer`: nothing applied, backoff, no OTA checkin credit: the OTA gate gets -1, so a pending image whose checkin replies never fit rolls back with `no_checkin`) and logs `checkin -> HTTP 200, reply too large: not applied` once. `dev_checkin_settle` decides all three (answer, OTA status, log line) and `ember_client.c` passes only its `ota_status` to `ota_client_checkin_done`, so `test_http.c` covers that decision on the host (`run.sh` fails if any `ota_client_checkin_done` call in `ember_client.c` passes anything else, and the raw status is out of scope there); a truncated view does not parse, so it is not a knob view (below); weather and Pomodoro bodies fail to parse; now-playing art past `JPEG_MAX` is skipped. A body the server closes before the limit and short of its length (or chunked without its last chunk, or a read error) is still `HTTP_FAIL_BODY`. A cut logs one warning, quiet while the same route keeps being cut: `http_conn` remembers the last cut route, so a cut on a different route warns again, and any whole answer on the cut route re-arms it (a whole answer on another route, or a drained but truncated one, does not). `test_http.c` drives the real `http_conn.c` against a fake `esp_http_client` and feeds each answer to `ember_state_answer` and `dev_checkin_parse`/`dev_checkin_answer`. The legacy Pomodoro body is a few fixed fields in a 512 B buffer. A view the knob cannot read (`answer is not a knob view`) still changes nothing: the view policy counts it as a server error and falls back to `/state` after three, and that answer decides; so a real Ember with a view this firmware cannot read never flashes offline. Still uncounted, by design: a host that answers every route with 404 (the same answer as an Ember without the view route, or with Pomodoro off), 401/403 or other 4xx stays `BOOT`/`ONLINE`: no fresh data, but no offline marks either.
- `gen` counts entries into `OFFLINE`. Level and `gen` are packed in one word (`link_state_word`) that the ember task stores in one atomic, so other tasks read a consistent pair; the zero word is `BOOT`, gen 0, so the atomic needs no initializer.
- Fed only by the ember task (`link_note` in `ember_client.c`). Pages read `ember_client_offline()` (the word is `OFFLINE` and an Ember URL is set). Bot, Pomodoro, weather and now playing all use it (now playing used to flip on the first HTTP error through `ember_client_link()`, which stays for diagnostics and "Not paired").

**Presses cannot be replayed** (Codex review of #38): a press is stamped at press time with Ember's link word and the Pomodoro source word (`pomo_press_t`). It is dropped at once when either is offline, and at send time `pomo_press_ok` sends it only if neither word was offline at the press, neither is offline now and neither `gen` moved, so a press from before an outage is never sent, even when the link is back by then.
