The 0.9.40 draft checked only "offline now": three presses queued while degraded, the third failure in flight, two drops while offline, then a successful GET, and the third press went out after the reconnect (`test_no_replay` shows that rule passing the press). When Ember's link enters `OFFLINE` the ember task drains the queue and the pending long-poll press at once, so `OFFLINE: NOT SENT` (4 s, `POMO_ACTION_OFFLINE`) shows when the press is dropped, not at the reconnect. When only the Pomodoro source enters `OFFLINE` (`src_note`, on the ember task in view mode or the pomo task in fallback) it drains the queue only: the pending long-poll press is still dropped by its stamp, but when it is next run (the next poll's `longpoll_idle` aborts at once and gates it), so its `OFFLINE: NOT SENT` can come up to one poll late.

**Pomodoro freshness**: the Pomodoro data has its own `link_state_t` in `pomo_client.c`. It is fed by every view attempt that is not aborted or a 429 (ok when the view was applied, the pomo block included or off) in view mode, and by every legacy `/v1/pomodoro/state` poll in fallback through `pomo_poll_counts` (host-tested): a 404 means off and counts as an answer, everything else follows `link_counts`, so 401/403, 429 and other 4xx change nothing (until 0.9.43 the legacy poll counted them as misses). Three misses make the page offline (estimate, labels, dropped presses) even while Ember's link is `ONLINE`; `pomo_offline` combines both words (host-tested in `test_pomo.c` `test_source_stale`).

**Per page**:
- Bot: offline sets the neutral `BOT_IDLE` mood (dim grey outline, no host label; it turns sleepy after `sleepy_after_s` like any idle) and shows a small grey link glyph (`LV_SYMBOL_WIFI`, Montserrat 24, `0x5A5A5A`, 140 px below the centre, clear of the "Can't join" label). A demo mood (long push) still wins for its hold time.
- Pomodoro: `pomo_estimate` + `pomo_est_label` (`components/pomo`, host-tested). Running: remaining is the last view's `ends_at` aligned to Ember's clock (`pomo_clock_sync_end`, the `X-Ember-Now` offset) minus now, clamped at 0: the same local clock as online, so the count does not jump at the disconnect. Phase line `OFFLINE` (note grey). At 0 the time shows `00:00` (dim) and the phase line `WAITING FOR EMBER`; the phase never advances on the knob, nothing is recorded (the knob has no chime). Paused: `PAUSED OFFLINE`. Parked or idle: `OFFLINE`; no timer: `--:--`. No state yet and not offline (boot): `CONNECTING` (was `OFFLINE`). The built-in Montserrat has no `·`, so the spec's `00:00 · waiting for Ember` is the time line plus the phase line.
- Weather: keeps the last observation; offline the Temperature face's sub-line shows the observation's age in grey, inside the face root, so it follows the 2 px burn-in shift (until 0.9.50 a separate label under the temperature, root height +34 px). `wx_age_label` (host-tested) picks the text, always through `wx_age_text` (`just now` under 1 min, `N min ago`, `N h ago`, `N d ago`, capped at 999 d):
  - **Observation age (0.9.48, #53)**, when the view's `weather.observed_at` (Ember v0.48.0+, integer Unix seconds of the provider's own time, Ember `DEVICE-PROTOCOL.md` "Weather observation time") and the knob's server clock are both known. On each applied view `pomo_client_feed` returns the server's time at receipt, `received + offset` from `pomo_srv_clock_feed`, which notes that answer's `X-Ember-Now` before it reads the offset (the one the Pomodoro uses); `apply_view` hands it to `weather_client_feed`, and `wx_store_put` stores `server time - observed_at` via `wx_obs_age`, clamped at 0 because the provider's clock can run ahead of Ember's. The label shows that age plus the local time since the view arrived (`age_s`), so it keeps counting offline without a clock. A known age of 30 min or more wins over `stale`: Ember's `stale` means its last fetch is 30 min or more old, and the observation's age says how old. Under 30 min a stale observation still reads `stale`, never `just now`: Ember sets `stale` from its own fetch time, so a provider clock ahead of Ember's can clamp the age to 0 while Ember says stale. The age has the provider's granularity as a floor: `observed_at` is the start of Open-Meteo's 15 min step or MET's hour, so fresh data can read up to `14 min ago` (Open-Meteo) or `59 min ago` (MET) the moment the knob goes offline. That is the observation's real age, not staleness.
  - **Fallback**, as before 0.9.48: no `observed_at` (older Ember, a provider without a time), a value that is not an integer in [1577836800, 1e13), i.e. from 2020-01-01 up to the knob's upper bound (ignored at parse) or no `X-Ember-Now` yet. Then `stale` when Ember flagged the observation stale (never `just now`), else the time since Ember last confirmed the observation (`wx_obs_t.age_s`; every view answer, 304 included, refreshes it). The legacy weather endpoint always takes this path.
  - The stale rule (`stale` or older than 1800 s since Ember confirmed it) still stops the animation; the observation age does not change it.
- Now playing: its "Ember offline" face, now on the shared state.
- Navigation, brightness and local animation: unaffected.

**Reconnect**: the first good answer sets `ONLINE`; the view replaces every estimate (`pomo_clock_sync_end` rebases on a new phase or `ends_at`, the bot takes Ember's mood, the age label hides). No reconciliation, no NVS writes. A reboot without the server starts in `BOOT` and shows the offline marks after 3 failed requests.

**Tests**: `test_link.c` (`link_counts` table; `test_wrong_host`: only 301/302 or unparsed 200s go offline from boot and from online, only 401/403/404/429 never do; boot: no-Wi-Fi passes ignored, 1-2 failures stay boot, a press at boot is sent, 3 failures go offline; 0/1/2/3 failures, the start of the streak, the generation, recovery; `test_no_replay`: the Codex sequence sends nothing, an offline press is never sent later, an outage between a press and its send drops it); `test_pomo.c` (`test_offline_estimate`: no timer, running across the disconnect equal to online every 0.25 s and within 1 s of `ends_at - (now + offset)`, reaching 0 and clamping without a phase advance, a reconnect that brings a short break with a new `ends_at`, paused, paused at 0, parked, idle; `test_source_stale`: stale Pomodoro data with Ember online gives the offline estimate and labels and drops presses, recovery, Ember offline; `test_poll_counts`: the legacy poll's table, every status except 404 equal to `link_counts`, a rejected token never makes the data stale); `test_weather.c` (`test_age_text`: boundaries, negative, NaN, truncation, the stale label; `test_obs_age`: server time minus `observed_at`, a provider clock ahead clamped at 0, unknown inputs, the observation age over `stale` from 1800 s, `stale` below it, the fallback without a server time); `test_view.c` (`test_observed_at`: integer, 0, negative, fractional, string, null, huge, the `X-Ember-Now` window 1577836800 to 1e13; the rest of the block still parses); `test_fixtures.c` (`observed_at` in `view_full` and `view_caps_limited`, `view_single_host_paused` without it labels `stale` as before; `test_obs_age_wiring` runs the parsed view through `pomo_srv_clock_feed` and `wx_store_put`/`wx_store_get` in `apply_view`'s order: the first view without a server clock, the first view whose own answer sets it, a 304 re-apply with and without a clock sample, elapsed minutes, hours and days, stale with a provider clock ahead). `apply_view` itself is not host-built.

**Cost** (`idf.py size`, origin/main 0.9.39 at 67fc9a9 → this branch, same machine and IDF 5.5.5):

| | main | 0.9.40 | Δ |
|---|---|---|---|
| Image | 1,547,547 B | 1,550,275 B | +2,728 |
| DIRAM (static) | 102,655 B (`.data` 23,432, `.bss` 16,000) | 102,663 B (23,432, 16,008) | +8 (`.bss`: the two atomic words) |
| IRAM | 16,384 | 16,384 | 0 |
| PSRAM `.bss` | 49,092 B | 49,172 B | +80 (two `link_state_t`, the age text) |
| `stack_walk.py` | `ember` 5,056, `pomo` 4,368, `lvgl` 8,592 | `ember` 5,088, `pomo` 4,416, `lvgl` 8,592 | `ember` +32 (margin 1,824), `pomo` +48 (margin 1,216) |

Unresolved indirect calls: equal to main for every task except `pomo` and `weather` 52 → 53 (`tcp_connect` in lwIP, not touched here: decode depends on the link layout, see the stack section). Press queue items grow from 4 to 12 B (4 deep). No new task, timer or NVS key. At run time two LVGL labels (bot glyph, weather age; LVGL objects use the internal heap through `LV_USE_CLIB_MALLOC`).

**On-device runtime numbers (2026-10-09, knob-61fc8c, app-flash of the secret-free `build-release` image, Ember container stopped for about 4 min then restarted).** From the live checkin; 0.9.39 is the local build of the page-table change.

| | 0.9.39 | 0.9.40 |
|---|---|---|
| Internal free at boot | 102,575 B | 102,115 B |
| Internal minimum since boot | 65,284 B | 64,448 B (unchanged through the outage) |
| Internal free / largest block after about 20 min, incl. the outage | not recorded | 101,967 / 31,744 B |
| Stack free `lvgl` | 5,576 B | 5,572 B |
| Stack free `ember` at boot / after the outage | not recorded | 4,128 / 3,712 B |
| OTA image after boot (health gate) | `valid` | `valid` |
| Offline pages (after about 60 s) | n/a | bot: idle face + link glyph; Pomodoro: `--:--` `OFFLINE`, a push shows `OFFLINE: NOT SENT`; weather: last observation + `3 min ago`; now playing: `Ember offline` |
| Recovery | n/a | Pomodoro page back to `PUSH TO START` within seconds of the restart; server Pomodoro still `idle`, so the offline press was not replayed |

PSRAM free and fps per page are not in the checkin and were not measured.

## Quiet hours (#28, Ember#343, 0.9.41)

Display only: the knob has no speaker. Ember owns the window (`quiet_hours`, one rule for the clock and the knob); the knob only follows the view. Contract: Ember `docs/DEVICE-PROTOCOL.md`, "Quiet hours"; fixtures synced from Ember main at 1d944f9, the #357 merge (`view_quiet.json`, `quiet` in `config_default.json`, `config_custom.json`, `checkin_reply_config.json`).

- **Wire**: view `"quiet":true` while the window is on, absent otherwise (absent, `false` or a non-bool = not quiet; `knob_view_t.quiet`). Config `"quiet":{"calm":bool,"dim_level":1-255}`, per knob; a missing block or key keeps the defaults, `calm` true and `dim_level` 20 (`KS_QUIET_DIM_DEFAULT`, Ember's default night level, above the default `floor` 10); `dim_level` is clamped to 1-255, a non-number ignored. The settings store keeps the config text as received, so `quiet` round-trips through NVS with nothing new to store; Ember puts its largest valid config at 648 B, the fixtures and the host tests check the stored text against `CFG_SETTINGS_MAX` (1024 B).
- **Brightness** (`dim_quiet_level`, `dim_quiet_floor` in `components/dim`): the ember task keeps the requested level (view `brightness.level` when following Ember, the legacy poll, or the manual `level`) and applies `max(level, floor)`, capped at `dim_level` while quiet. The fade floor drops to `dim_level` when that is lower, so a `floor` above `dim_level` does not raise a quiet knob (Ember's contract: quiet may go below the minimum brightness). A level already darker than `dim_level` stays. Leaving quiet re-applies the kept level through the usual 2 s fade on the next view. Only a view changes quiet (`knob_quiet_next`, host-tested): a view without the key clears it. The legacy (per-endpoint) fallback and offline keep the last view's value and its cap, since neither brings fresh quiet state; otherwise three 5xx or a rejected token at night would bring back full brightness and the hops. Accepted trade-off: a knob stuck in legacy or offline through the window's end stays dim and calm until the next view, a reboot or a re-pair. The cases with no next view are a revoked token (401/403, re-probed every 60 s) and a server without the view route (404/405, re-probed every 10 min).
- **Calm** (`bot_calm`, `bot_anim_allowed`, `bot_set_calm` in `bot_behavior`): while quiet and `calm`, the bot skips the attention and excited animations: waiting's repeating hops, the pop on a mood change, and the glint chase (the working glint still orbits; it is the "working" signal, not an attention cue). Blinks, glances, eye shapes, the error triangle and the outline colour stay, so the mood still reads. User input is never calmed (`BOT_ANIM_USER_INPUT`): a push still hops and a long-press demo mood still pops (`bot_set_mood_user`); they answer the user, not the agent. The LVGL task reads `ember_client_quiet()` every frame; calm on cancels the next hop (a hop or pop already running finishes, at most 1 s), calm off while waiting re-arms the hops 0.6 s later. A glint chase already running when quiet starts finishes its laps and label return (`bot_chase_on`); calm only blocks new chases, automatic or forced over USB.
- **Tests**: `test_view.c` `test_quiet` (true, false, absent, non-bool, quiet then a view without it; `knob_quiet_next`: legacy keeps quiet, only a view sets or clears it); `test_device.c` `test_settings_quiet` (defaults, partial blocks, clamps, bad types, round trip through the stored text, size); `test_ember.c` `test_quiet_dim` (cap, floor below and above `dim_level`, Ember's night level 20 against `dim_level` 20 and 5, a night level under the floor, fade on and off); `test_bot.c` `test_calm` (hops and pops off, push still hops, demo mood still pops, on and off while waiting, pop back after quiet, `bot_chase_on`); `test_fixtures.c` (`view_quiet.json`, quiet absent in the other views, `quiet` in the configs, config sizes).

**Cost** (`idf.py size`, origin/main 0.9.40 at c521572 → this branch, same machine and IDF 5.5.5):

| | main | 0.9.41 | Δ |
|---|---|---|---|
| Image | 1,550,275 B | 1,551,251 B | +976 |
| Flash `.text` / `.rodata` | 1,052,464 / 394,484 B | 1,053,376 / 394,532 B | +912 / +48 |
| DIRAM (static) | 102,663 B (`.data` 23,432, `.bss` 16,008) | 102,679 B (23,448, 16,008) | +16 (`.data`: the requested level starts at -1) |
| IRAM | 16,384 | 16,384 | 0 |
| PSRAM `.bss` | 49,172 B | 49,172 B | 0 |
| `stack_walk.py` | `ember` 5,088, `lvgl` 8,592 | `ember` 5,088, `lvgl` 8,592 | 0 for every task (margins `ember` 1,824, `lvgl` 1,904) |

Unresolved indirect calls move by one in `ember` (243 → 242), `pomo` and `weather` (53 → 52) and `main` (380 → 381): objdump decode noise from the shifted layout, as in the offline fallback section. No new task, timer, NVS key or LVGL object. Not measured on the knob yet: the on-device check (quiet on and off, with `calm` on and off, `dim_level` against `floor`, a legacy fallback during quiet keeps the knob dim and calm) is in the PR.

## Capabilities and the view major (#27, Ember#341, 0.9.42)

The knob says what it can do in every checkin, and the view parser only reads view majors it knows. Contract: Ember `docs/DEVICE-PROTOCOL.md`, "Capabilities"; spec `Specs/ember/2026-10-09-device-protocol-capabilities-design.md` in the vault. Fixtures pinned to Ember main 4a8c20c, after the #363 merge (`checkin_req_caps.json`, `checkin_reply_caps.json`, `view_caps_limited.json`; bytes unchanged from the PR head 638985e).

- **Caps** (`components/caps`, `knob_caps()`; serialised by `dev_checkin_body` in `device_api.c`, first key, keys sorted like the rest of the body):

  ```json
  "caps": {"features": ["view_wait", "np_control", "ota_rollback", "coredump", "stats_intervals"],
           "limits": {"config_bytes": 1024, "view_bytes": 16383},
           "pages": ["bot", "pomodoro", "weather", "nowplaying"], "view": [1, 1]}
  ```

  `pages` comes from the page table (`pages_ids()`), `view` from `KNOB_VIEW_V_MIN`/`KNOB_VIEW_V_MAX` (`knob_view.h`), `view_bytes` from `KNOB_VIEW_BUF - 1` (the view buffer, `RESP_MAX` in `ember_client.c`, less its NUL), `config_bytes` from `CFG_SETTINGS_MAX` (the settings store rejects anything longer). Features, each checked against the code: `view_wait` (`?wait=` after `X-Ember-View-Wait`, `view_wait.c`), `np_control` (`POST /v1/nowplaying/control`, `nowplaying_client.c`), `ota_rollback` (Ember's offers, `esp_ota_mark_app_invalid_rollback_and_reboot`; the build refuses an sdkconfig without app rollback), `coredump` (upload on `coredump_wanted`, erase on `coredump_ack`), `stats_intervals` (`stats_interval_s`, `live_interval_s` from the config). The serialiser applies Ember's patterns and leaves out a page id not matching `^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]{0,15}$`, a feature not matching `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,31}$` (Ember drops the whole caps for one bad id, so one bad id must not reach it) and a limit of 0. `test_caps.c` checks every page table id against the page pattern. The caps are built once at the ember task start into its PSRAM context.
- **Old server**: `caps_ack` is never read. A server without #341 ignores `caps` (lenient decode) and its reply parses exactly as before (`test_fixtures.c`: `checkin_reply_caps.json` and `checkin_reply_current.json` give the same result).
- **Blocks may be absent**: a caps server leaves out the blocks of pages that are not in `caps.pages` or are off in the knob's `pages`. The view parser no longer needs `mood` (it used to reject a view without it); `knob_view_t.has_mood` is false and the step's mood is -1 (unknown), so the ember task keeps its last mood and host label instead of forcing idle; the bot page is off whenever Ember leaves `mood` out. A missing `pomo` or `weather` is read like `null`, as before. A view is now any object with a numeric `v`; Ember has sent `v` since the view route was added (`devices_view.go`, 1ee45e2), so a view without `v` is "not a knob view", as a view without `mood` was.
- **View major** (`knob_view_read`, host-tested): a `v` that is not a whole number is `KNOB_VIEW_BAD` (checked before the range, so 1.5 or 0.5 is a bad view, not an update state); a whole `v` below `KNOB_VIEW_V_MIN` is `KNOB_VIEW_TOO_OLD`, above `KNOB_VIEW_V_MAX` is `KNOB_VIEW_TOO_NEW`; in both cases nothing else in the body is read and the last good view stays.
- **Per-answer step** (`knob_view_step` in `components/knob_view/view_step.c`, pure, host-tested in `test_view.c` `test_step`): the ember task hands it each view answer and acts on the result. State `knob_view_state_t` {last good view, `have_view`, `unsupported`, `compat`, `major`}; `knob_view_conditional` (send `If-None-Match`) is `have_view || unsupported`.

  | Answer | Action | ETag | State |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 200, supported | `APPLY`: feed the pages, mood from the view | set | `have_view`, compat OK |
  | 200, too old/new | `RESTORE` (mood and host label from the kept view, nothing fed) or `NONE` without a view | set, so the long-poll waits on it | `unsupported`, compat update Ember/knob |
  | 200, bad | `UNPARSED` (500 to the link and fallback logic) | cleared | no view, not unsupported; the label stays until a good view |
  | 304, supported | `APPLY` of the cached view | kept | |
  | 304, unsupported | `RESTORE` or `NONE` | kept | |
  | 304, no view | `REFETCH` | cleared | |
  | error, disconnect | `NONE` | kept | |

  `RESTORE` exists for the case Codex found in review: v1 applied, then v2, then a Wi-Fi drop sets the mood to -1; the next 304 brings back the kept view's mood and host label (before the fix the bot stayed idle until a supported view). The stale view is never fed again (no Pomodoro, weather or brightness refresh). The link counts every 200/304 as good (no offline marks, no legacy fallback). The Pomodoro source stays unconfirmed, so after three polls it shows its offline estimate and drops presses, as for any stale Pomodoro data. `knob_view_state_reset` (legacy fallback, token change) clears it. Known trade-off (Opus review, kept per the spec): the bot, weather and now playing keep the last view's state while the label shows; the state only happens when a server ignores `caps.view`.
- **Update state** (`ember_client_view_compat()`, read by `join_frame` in `main.c`): the existing amber bottom label that shows "Not paired" (Montserrat 24, `0xF5A623`, 40 px above the bottom, on the top layer so it shows on every page) reads **"Update knob"** when the server's view is newer than the firmware parses and **"Update Ember"** when it is older. Priority below "Can't join" and "Not paired". No new LVGL object. One log line per change (`view: major 99 outside 1-1, keeping the last view: update the knob`).
- **Checkin buffer**: `P->body` 1536 → `DEV_CHECKIN_BODY_MAX` 2048 B (PSRAM, inside the ember task context). A body over the buffer is logged once and counted as a failed checkin (`dev_sched_done`, backoff) instead of returning silently and retrying every loop pass. The worst-case body (12 tasks with 16-char names, a crash, full OTA report, 8-CPU stats, 32-char `fw`, every number at its maximum) is 1,732 B with caps; without the bump the caps would have pushed it past 1,536 and `dev_checkin_body` would have returned 0, which skips the checkin. `test_caps.c` keeps 256 B of headroom.
- **Tests**: `test_caps.c` (the firmware's caps from the page table, every table id against Ember's page pattern, limits from `KNOB_VIEW_BUF` and `CFG_SETTINGS_MAX`, key order, no caps = the old body, filtering of ids and tokens against Ember's patterns (case, first char, length 16/32, dash only in pages), single limits, the worst-case body); `test_view.c` `test_major` (v 99, 2, 0 and -3 keep the last view byte for byte, a huge v; 1.5, 0.5, 99.5, a string or no v return `KNOB_VIEW_BAD`; supported again), `test_step` (the table above: v1 → v2 → disconnect → 304 restores the mood, an unsupported first view long-polls instead of refetching, bad after unsupported, reset; mutants without `|| unsupported`, without `RESTORE` or with the range check first each make tests fail) and the view without `mood`; `test_fixtures.c` (`checkin_req_caps.json` against a checkin built with `knob_caps()`, `checkin_reply_caps.json` equal to the old server's reply, `view_caps_limited.json` without `pomo` and `nowplaying`, `view_full.json` without `mood`, `pomo` or `weather`, a synthetic `v:99` from `view_minimal.json` keeping `view_full.json`, then `v:1` applied); the pre-caps fixtures still pass unchanged.

**Cost** (`idf.py size`, origin/main 0.9.41 at c82fc39 → this branch, same machine and IDF 5.5.5):

| | main | 0.9.42 | Δ |
|---|---|---|---|
| Image | 1,551,211 B | 1,553,083 B | +1,872 |
| Flash `.text` / `.rodata` | 1,053,336 / 394,532 B | 1,054,808 / 394,932 B | +1,472 / +400 |
| DIRAM (static) | 102,679 B (`.data` 23,448, `.bss` 16,008) | 102,679 B (23,448, 16,008) | 0 |
| IRAM | 16,384 | 16,384 | 0 |
| PSRAM `.bss` | 49,172 B | 49,172 B | 0 |
| PSRAM heap (ember context) | | | about +560 (body +512, caps and page id pointers about 50) |
| `stack_walk.py` | `ember` 5,088, `lvgl` 8,592 | `ember` 5,152, `lvgl` 8,592 | `ember` +64 (margin 1,760) |

Unresolved indirect calls: `lvgl` 141 → 140, the rest equal (decode noise). No new task, timer, NVS key or LVGL object; nothing new in internal RAM. Not on the knob yet: the on-device check (caps stored and `caps_ack` from Ember #363, `effective_caps.source` `reported`, internal heap and `ember` stack free in the checkin, the "Update knob" label, which needs a server answering `v:99` and so the user's approval to point the knob at one) is in the PR.


## Display rotation (#45, Ember#386, 0.9.44)

The user can turn the knob's image upside down from the Ember app. Contract: Ember `docs/DEVICE-PROTOCOL.md` (Ember#388, merged as 01864b5); fixtures synced from 01864b5 (`config_default.json` and `checkin_reply_config.json` with `display.rotation: 0`, `config_custom.json` with 180, `checkin_req_caps.json` with `caps.rotations: [0,180]`).

- **Setting**: `display.rotation` in the knob config (next to `display.fast_link`), 0/90/180/270, default 0, always sent. `parse_rotation` in `knob_settings.c` (the one place that reads the key) keeps a whole number the firmware supports and turns anything else (90, 270, 45, 180.5, `"180"`, `null`, a top-level `rotation`) into 0. Stored in NVS with the rest of the settings blob, so it survives a reboot.
- **Caps**: `rotations: [0,180]` (`KR_SUPPORTED` in `components/rotation`). The serialiser leaves out values other than 0/90/180/270 and repeats, and leaves the key out when 0 is missing: Ember drops every caps field for an invalid list.
- **Why only 0 and 180**: the CO5300 MADCTL (36h) has only MY (bit 7) and MX (bit 6); bit 5 (MV, row/column exchange) is "don't care" in the datasheet (V0.00, 7.5.29). The `esp_lcd_co5300` driver sets MV in `swap_xy`, but the panel ignores it. LVGL software rotation needs a rotate buffer in internal DMA RAM that the knob does not have (2 draw buffers already take 113 KB), so 90 and 270 are not offered.
- **180 = hardware mirror** (`kr_panel`, `components/bsp_knob`): MX + MY plus `esp_lcd_panel_set_gap`. The glass shows GRAM columns 6..471 and rows 0..465 (at 0 these are the same frame columns and rows). The gap depends on the window MX/MY mirror over: `KR_MIRROR_W` = 480 columns, `KR_MIRROR_H` = 466 rows. The 466-row span is empirical, not a controller fact: it was derived from the user's photos of 0.9.45 (below) and confirmed by the device check of 0.9.46 and 0.9.47. The datasheet (V0.00) points both ways and does not settle it: §1 (p. 6) says "the source resolution can be adjusted 480RGB and the gate resolution only can be set 480 lines", and both resolution lists (§1 p. 6, §2 p. 7) start at 480RGBx480; but CASET and RASET default to 0..453 (end 01C5h, a 454x454 default), and it never says which span MY mirrors over. The 466-row span is the empirical answer, from the photos and the device checks. The gap is the mirror window minus the frame, (480 - 472, 466 - 466) = (8, 0): frame column x lands on GRAM 471 - x and row y on 465 - y, so the frame mirrors in place and the UI (laid out centred on the 472-wide frame) sits on the same GRAM columns and rows at 0 and 180. The glass then shows frame columns 0..465 at 180 (6..471 at 0); frame columns 466..471 land on GRAM 5..0, written but off the glass. Every CASET stays inside 0..479 and every RASET inside rows 0..465. Both gaps are even, so 2 px windows (even start, odd end) stay 2 px pairs. At 0 degrees: gap (0, 0), no mirror, as before. Device check (the user, 0.9.47, 2026-10-11): no band, no stale pixels, and the Pomodoro ring stays put when switching 0 ↔ 180, so the rows mirror over 466 and the columns are mirrored in place with gap (8, 0).
- **The 480-row mistake (#52, fixed in 0.9.46)**: 0.9.44 and 0.9.45 assumed MY mirrors over 480 rows and set gap (2, 14). The panel mirrors over its 466 rows, so at 180 the image sat 14 rows low on the glass, the top 14 glass rows were never written (old pixels from an earlier orientation stayed there: a second ring arc above the Pomodoro ring), and frame rows 452..465 asked for RASET rows 466..479, past the panel. Every full-width refresh ends with the strip at rows 420..465 (60-row draw buffer), which became RASET 434..479; that strip did not land on its rows, leaving a black band about 14 rows tall near the bottom (frame rows ~420..435, measured on the user's photo of the Pomodoro page) and cutting the bottom of the ring. This mechanism is inferred from the photos, not from the controller: what they show is that the first ~14 rows of the last strip never landed and the rest landed correctly. What the CO5300 does with a RASET past row 465 (reject, clip or wrap) is unknown; a wrap would have painted the ring's bottom cap inside the band, and the band has no red pixel. The bot's hop moves the full-screen rim canvas, so every hop frame is a full refresh and redrew the band. The glint (the white dot) has a small window that ends above row 452 and stays inside the panel, so it would repaint the band where it passes; that is consistent with the photos but was not separately verified. The 0.9.44 check ("no edge strip") could not see the stale rows: they sit at the very top of a black round screen. Measured on two photos from the user: the stale arc is 14.9 panel rows above the ring and the band 14 rows tall. The fix does not depend on the mechanism: since 0.9.46 no RASET goes past row 465 (host-checked for every rounded window), and the 0.9.47 device check saw no band. No panel RAM clear on a switch: the switch's full redraw writes every visible GRAM pixel, which `test_panel_frame` and `test_switch_over_stale` check.
- **The glass-centre shift (#52, fixed in 0.9.47)**: 0.9.44 to 0.9.46 used column gap 2 (gap (2, 14) in 0.9.44 and 0.9.45, (2, 0) in 0.9.46), which mirrors the columns about the glass centre (GRAM 6..471, centre 238.5): frame column x landed on GRAM 477 - x. LVGL centres the UI on the 472-wide frame (centre 235.5), so the UI centre sat on GRAM 235.5 at 0 and 241.5 at 180, and switching 0 → 180 → 0 on the Pomodoro page moved the red ring about 6 px sideways (the user saw it; about 4-5 px measured on the user's photo). The column offset of about 4 px in the 0.9.45 photos, first put down to photo error, was most likely the same shift (gap 2 predicts 6 px; the offset was not measured separately); the 0.9.47 device check shows no column offset left. The user wants the ring not to move, so 0.9.47 mirrors the frame in place instead (gap 8). The cost: the frame's centre sits 3 px off the glass centre (towards GRAM column 0) in both orientations, so a viewer of the turned-over knob sees the UI 3 px right of the glass centre instead of 3 px left; it no longer moves on the glass when the rotation switches.
- **If the mirror window is narrower** (a persistent strip at an edge at 180): set `KR_MIRROR_W` (and `KR_MIRROR_H` if the strip is at the top or bottom) to the real even width. The frame still mirrors in place (GRAM 471 - x) in any window down to 472 wide, where the gap is 0. Below 472 the gap goes negative (466 gives -6); `kr_panel_in` then returns `min_x = -gap_x` and the rounder (`kr_round`, used by `area_rounder_cb`) starts every rounded window at `min_x`. Host-tested for every even window from 472x466 to 480x480 (in place, every visible column and row written once) and the rounder at 466. That is not enough on its own: LVGL 9.5 rounds an area when it is invalidated, not when it is flushed, and two paths skip the rounder: an `inv_areas` overflow (`LV_INV_BUF_SIZE`) stores the raw full-screen area from column 0, and areas invalidated before the switch keep the old geometry's rounding. Either one would send a CASET start below 0 (466 gives -6, `FF FA`). So a window under 472 would also need a draw-time clamp in the flush path (trim columns below `-gap_x` before `esp_lcd_panel_draw_bitmap`). Not implemented: a window narrower than the frame cannot show the visible columns anyway, and at the shipped 480 window `min_x` is 0 and the gap is +8.
- **Touch**: `tp_read` maps the CST820 point with `kr_touch` for the orientation last written successfully: at 180, x' = 471 - x, y' = 465 - y (the inverse of the in-place mirror: the finger lands on the frame pixel drawn under it), clamped to the 472x466 frame. Like the panel mapping, it does not depend on the mirror window.
- **Applied live, retried until it holds**: `set_rotation` only stores the wish (`kr_want`). `bsp_knob_15_md50et_rotation_frame()` runs at the top of every `frame_cb` (LVGL task, lock held, between frames, like the brightness queue in `panel_check.c`) and steps `kr_step`: when the panel's orientation differs from the wish it writes gap + MADCTL (`tx_param` first waits for colour transfers in flight), and on success invalidates the screen, so the next refresh (about 25 ms of DMA at 80 MHz) redraws everything. A failed write is rolled back to the old orientation and retried after 100 ms, doubling to at most 5 s, whether or not a new config arrives. If the rollback fails too, the orientation is unknown: it is logged as an error, touch keeps the last orientation written, and the wish is written again on the same backoff (no rollback to an unknown state). A new wish resets the backoff. At boot `main` sets the stored wish before `bsp_knob_15_md50et_init`, and `lcd_init` steps once before display on, so the first frame is already the right way up; a failure there is retried by the frames instead of stopping the boot.
- **Unchanged**: encoder direction, the button, the factory-reset gesture and snapshots (`snapshot.py` reads LVGL, so it is always upright).
- **Tests**:
  - `test_rotation.c`: supported list, fallback, gaps and mirror, coverage and non-negative windows for every even mirror window 472..480 x 466..480.
  - Panel model of the assumed panel (columns mirrored over 480, rows over `MEASURED_ROWS` = 466, as derived from the photos and the device check; a regression guard that pins the fix, not a proof of the hardware): replays a full refresh in 60-row strips and a small window by the bottom strip at 0 and 180 and checks that every visible pixel is written, shows its own frame pixel and stays inside the panel's rows (fails on gap (2, 14) with 6,608 pixels past row 465 and 6,524 visible pixels stale).
  - Model-free window check: for every even mirror window 472..480 x 466..480 at 0 and 180, every rounded window of an area overlapping the frame, plus the gap, stays inside that window's columns and rows (at the shipped 480x466: columns 0..479, rows 0..465, the property that removes the band). Until 0.9.50 this check capped rows at the panel model's 466, the empirical figure itself.
  - Switch sequence 0 → 180 → 0 → 180 through `kr_step` over GRAM filled with stale pixels, redrawing in full only when `kr_step` reports the switch applied: every visible glass pixel shows the current frame after each switch (fails with 212,548 visible pixels stale when the test's own redraw step is skipped, and with 217,156 visible pixels wrong and 6,608 past row 465 on gap (2, 14)). It pins the contract that a switch reported as applied is followed by a full redraw; the firmware's side of it, `rotation_step` returning `r == KR_APPLIED` and `bsp_knob_15_md50et_rotation_frame` calling `lv_obj_invalidate` on the active screen when it does (`bsp_knob_15_md50et.c`, not host-built), is checked by grep in `run.sh` since 0.9.50.
  - Failed switch over stale GRAM (`test_rollback_over_stale`, 0.9.50): a switch 0 → 180 and 180 → 0 whose apply fails and whose rollback succeeds keeps the old orientation for state and touch, and the next partial redraw leaves every visible pixel on the old picture (fails with 2,304 visible pixels wrong when the rollback reports the wish as shown); its retry applies. An apply and a rollback that both fail leave the orientation unknown, and the retry's full redraw writes every visible pixel.
  - The rounder at 0, 180 and a negative gap; 2 px pairs; the frame mirrored in place for every window (every frame column and row on the same GRAM column and row at 0 and 180, the centre columns 235-236 and a centred 300 px ring on the same GRAM columns; fails on gap (2, 0) with the centre on GRAM 241-242 and the ring on 91..392 instead of 85..386); touch on every 5th visible pixel and every visible frame pixel mapped back to itself; clamping.
  - The state machine with an injected `orient`: no write for the same value, a failed apply retried with backoff and no new wish, the backoff cap, a failed rollback giving unknown + retry of the wish, recovery. Each new state machine and rounder test fails on a mutant restoring the old behaviour (retry only on a settings change; rollback result ignored; rounder clamped at 0).
  - `test_device.c` `test_settings_rotation` (default, 180, every fallback, the round trip through a checkin's stored config); `test_caps.c` (`[0,180]` between `pages` and `view`, invalid values, repeats, no 0); `test_fixtures.c` (config rotation 0 and 180, the caps request byte for byte).
- **On the knob** (0.9.44, 2026-10-10: steps 1 and 4 checked, the rest not yet; step 4 failed at the top and bottom edges, #52; rows confirmed by the user on 0.9.46; the ring moved sideways on a switch; 0.9.47, 2026-10-11: steps 3 and 4 pass, no band, no stale pixels, the ring stays put on 0 ↔ 180):
  1. Set 180 in the app: the image is upright with the knob turned over, within a checkin and without a reboot.
  2. Taps and swipes land where the finger is; swipe up/down still go the right way for the viewer.
  3. The ring and the bot sit on the same glass columns and rows at 180 as at 0: switching 0 → 180 → 0 → 180 on the Pomodoro page does not move the ring sideways or up/down (0.9.47); no second arc above the ring after a few switches.
  4. No persistent line, blank strip or shifted image at any edge at 180, including the bottom of the Pomodoro ring and the bot's rim while it hops. A persistent strip means the panel mirrors over a narrower window; see "If the mirror window is narrower" above.
  5. Reboot: it comes back at 180 with no upside-down first frame.
  6. Set 0 again: the image is as before, with no edge line.

Image about 1,555 KB (`cinder.bin`); no new task, NVS key or LVGL object.

## Temperature face (#33, Ember#350, 0.9.51)

The weather page is now the Temperature face (canvases AT4/AT5, [`face-design.md`](face-design.md)), the first device face. It replaces the 200×140 sky canvas (`main/weather_view.c`, removed). `components/weather/weather_scene.c` (the rain, snow and storm sky) stays in the component and in `test_weather.c` but is out of the firmware `SRCS`; rain, snow and storm come back with the Rain now face (next section). Fixtures synced to Ember v0.50.0 (`view_faces.json`, `view_faces_met.json`, `view_faces_polar_dry.json`, `view_np_cover.json`). **Not checked on the knob yet.**

- **Data** (`parse_detail` in `knob_view.c`): with `wx_faces` in the caps, Ember adds the detail keys to `weather`. The face reads `now.cloud`, `now.uv`, `today.min_c`/`max_c` and `moon.phase`/`illum`/`up`; the rest (wind, rain spells, days, twilight, `aqi`, `utc_offset`) is left for the other faces. A key that is missing, not a number or out of range is "unknown", never 0. A legacy view, the legacy `/v1/weather/state` poller or an Ember before v0.49.0 gives no detail, and the face degrades:

  | Missing | Face |
  |---|---|
  | `today` | Gauge on the fixed scale −20..40 °C, no end labels |
  | `now.cloud` | Cloud cover from the condition: clear 0 %, partly cloudy 55 %, anything else 95 % |
  | `now.uv` | No advice line; the sky word ("Partly cloudy", "Rain", ...) instead |
  | `moon` | No moon at night (stars only when clear) |
  | `moon.up` false, missing or not a boolean | No moon: the moon shows only when Ember says `up: true` (the open design question, decided; `face-design.md`) |
  | `temp_c` or the whole block | Grey track, "--°", no marker, no labels |

- **Caps**: `wx_faces` is the sixth feature token. `checkin_req_caps.json` still has 0.9.41's five tokens (Ember keeps it until the firmware sends the token) and `NOT_YET_SENT` cannot exempt one array element, so `test_fixtures.c` has `FEATURES_AHEAD[] = {"wx_faces"}` (see [`workflow.md`](workflow.md)). The worst-case checkin body is 1,763 of 2,048 B.
- **cJSON on PSRAM**: the hooks were already in place (`json_malloc` prefers PSRAM, set first in `app_main`, commit 03871a4). `run.sh` now checks that wiring, and `test_fixtures.c` checks that every node of `view_faces.json` goes through the hooks and is freed (235 allocations against 78 for the legacy `view_caps_limited.json`).
- **Selection** (`components/weather/wx_temp.c`, pure, host-tested): value = `temp_c` rounded; gauge `today.min_c..max_c` widened to the value, 1° each side on a flat day; colour scale and gauge points from `design/faces/tools/edit.py`. Cloud bands (< 10, < 40, < 70, < 90, else) pick the canvas cloud layouts (none, one white, two white, four light grey, four dark grey; night tones at night); a cover within 5 % of the shown band's edges keeps that band (`wx_cloud_band_keep`, `WX_BAND_HYST`; the shown band is the baked key's), and the sky word follows the kept band. Moon illumination is rounded to 5 % (`WX_ILLUM_STEP`); of the phase only waxing or waning reaches the picture. Hero: sun by day under 40 % cloud (AT4), big moon at night under 70 % (AT5), small moon behind the clouds under 90 % (AT6), the moon only with `moon.up` true; stars at night under 40 %. Sub-line: UV advice by day at UV ≥ 3 (3-5 "Sunglasses on", 6-7 "Use sunscreen", 8-10 "Sunscreen & shade", 11+ "Avoid the sun", UV colours), else the sky word; offline: the observation's age, as before. Stale or old data greys the value.
- **Rendering** (`components/weather/wx_paint.c`, pure, host-tested; `main/temp_face.c`, LVGL):
  - One 466×466 RGB565 background per scene: the sun's 22 rays (static, rim fade, feathered keep-outs from the canvas), the static stars, the thermometer outline and the ring. The ring (per-pixel annulus with round caps and a 541-entry colour LUT along the angle; not `pomo_ring`, which has a fixed start and two colours) is painted into a packed strip (`wx_ring_t`: the annulus r 215..229 as 888 row runs, 19,512 px, 44,360 B) and copied into the background (`wx_ring_blit`); nothing else paints there, so the copy equals painting in place. A new value only moves the marker and changes the number.
  - Sprites (RGB565A8, rim fades baked): hero 164² (sun, moon with phase from `phase`/`illum`), two cloud images: the front cloud painted last (`wx_clouds_split`, the drift sprite, at most 208×76) and the others (the still layer, at most 466×144; none with one cloud), mercury box 42×76, marker 36² (painted on each show). The still layer under the drift sprite equals the old single layer inside r 175 (`test_bake`); in the rim fade each sprite fades on its own, so where two clouds overlap there the edge is a little more opaque.
  - Cloud shape (#99, 0.9.63): every cloud on every face (Temperature, Rain forecast, the Next days icons, the bot moments) is the canvas's closed path (`cloud_path` in `design/faces/tools/temp.py`): each puff's arc from one upper crossing with its neighbour to the next, the outer puffs down to the base line, closed by the straight base, filled, with a 4.5 px stroke (2 px on the Next days icons) and round joins. `wx_cloud_shape` builds the vertices once per cloud (base ends and upper crossings, whether each arc is over 180°) and `wx_cloud_sd` (`wx_paint.c`) is the signed distance to that path: the nearest of the arcs (only inside their own angle range), the vertices and the base, negative inside the path; exact within 4 px of it, so the stroke is `|d| <= 2.25` with the canvas's round joins at the creases and base corners. Until 0.9.62 the faces took min(circle distances) clipped at the base: under a junction where neither puff reaches the base nothing was filled, so the device drew a row of separate circles with notches and bare circle bottoms along a broken base (user photo of the dry Rain forecast face, 2026-10-12); the moments already filled the gap with a polygon but kept the min-distance outline (a pointed inner stroke at each crease, square outer base corners); both are round now, as on the canvas. Contract (`wx_cloud_shape` returns false, an empty shape with no vertices and no ink, otherwise): 1-4 puffs, positive radii, every puff's top above the base, the outer puffs crossing the base, neighbours properly intersecting (not apart, touching or contained) above the base, crossings left to right and on no other puff. `test_cloud.c` holds `wx_cloud_sd` to a brute-force distance to the densely sampled path (independent crossings by angle scan) on every table (bands, forecast variants, day icons via `wx_day_cloud`, moment clouds via `wxm_clouds`) and five odd valid shapes (one puff, deep overlap, base above the centres, ...): within 0.05 px where the path is under 4 px away, right sign and past ±4 beyond. It fails with the large-arc flag forced off (6.8 px on the dry forecast cloud), and checks that 15 invalid inputs (one puff with the base tangent or below, puffs apart, touching, contained, a hidden middle puff, a puff or a crossing below the base, 0 or 5 puffs, zero radius, NaN base) are refused and paint nothing. The sprite boxes were never the cut: they end 3 px under the base (`base + 2.75` is the stroke's soft edge). `test_temp.c` `test_cloud_path` (fails on the old painter: the base stroke is missing under the junctions of every cloud, the fill too under the overcast, dry-forecast and heavy-forecast junctions) checks, cloud by cloud for all bands day and night and the three forecast variants, the column under each crossing (fill colour down to the base stroke), one unbroken base stroke, its soft lower edge inside the box, no ink outside the box, and the distance at known points (2 px under a crossing is 2 px in: a round join); `test_days.c` checks the icons' base line under the middle puff. Host paint cost (M-series, -O2, one band's still and drift sprites): 0.31 → 0.39 ms; a forecast variant 0.29 → 0.35 ms (4 runs each); the S3 bake log will show the rest (not measured; the clouds are a part of the 0.51-0.54 s Temperature bake). `stack_walk.py`: `wxbake` 2,528 → 2,656 of 5,120 (margin 2,464; the shape on `wx_paint_clouds`' frame), `moment` 2,864 → 2,800 of 4,608 (margin 1,808). The vertical stripes inside the cloud in the photo are camera moiré: the baked fill is one flat colour (host render) and the stripes have the same 14 px period (photo pixels, about 8 display pixels) in the white digits of "12+". On the knob (to do): a snapshot of each cloud face once 0.9.63 is installed.
  - Arrival on page show (1.32 s, ease-out cubic): the marker sweeps from the gauge start in one box, the number counts up in `font_digits_96` (one step per degree up to 30, else 23 steps; step k lands at T(1-(1-k/n)^(1/3)) as on the canvas) and colours with it, the mercury rises in its box, hero and clouds fade in at 15 fps, the sub-line fades in over 3 steps after it. Then still, except the cloud drift: the front cloud moves ±4 px in 2 px steps over 8 s (one box, 1 step/s; rule 5). The end labels use `font_face_36`; both face fonts are now linked.
- **Bake off the LVGL task**: a data change (or the first show) hands the bake key to the `wxbake` task (core 1, prio 2 like `rim`, 4 KB stack in PSRAM), which runs `wx_bake` (`wx_paint.c`, pure: background, hero, both cloud images) and publishes the boxes. The LVGL task applies a done job (canvas buffers, positions, end labels, one invalidate each) and logs `tempface: baked lo..hi hero H clouds B in N ms`, N measured on the task. Until the first bake lands the face stays hidden, so the page-switch wipe never waits for it; the arrival starts when it lands.
  - **Job handoff** (`components/weather/wx_job.c`, pure, host-tested like `rim_job.c`): the job state (idle, queued, running, done; one atomic in internal RAM) plus visibility, buffers held, baked and the OTA hold. `temp_face.c` holds no job state: `wx_job_show`, `wx_job_hold` and `wx_job_step` (every frame) return `WX_DO_*` actions (release, acquire, detach, bake, apply, paint) and `act` runs them; the task takes a job with `wx_job_take` (compare-and-swap queued → running) and publishes it with `wx_job_done`. Hiding or a hold cancels a queued job (queued → idle, so the task never takes it) and frees at once; a running job keeps the buffers until it ends, then the next frame frees them. Freeing resets the state to idle, so a done job from before a hide is never applied over new buffers. `wx_job_step` writes the key before publishing queued, and `wx_job_done` the bake time before done.
  - **Re-bake on a shown face** (`wx_temp_bake_kind`, three parts): **ring** (`has_range`, `lo`, `hi`), **hero** (`hero`, moon waxing/waning, `moon_illum` in 5 % steps) and **scene** (`have`, `night`, `cloud_band`, `stars`, and whether the hero is the sun, which owns the rays); `value`, `frac`, `still`, `sub` never re-bake. Only the changed parts are baked:
    - Ring: the bake task paints the strip; the shown background is not touched. `apply` copies the strip into it on the LVGL task (one `memcpy` per run) and invalidates it: one full redraw, no blink.
    - Hero: two hero buffers; every bake paints the back one, `apply` swaps them. The shown sprite is never rewritten, so no blink.
    - Scene: background and clouds are rewritten in place, so the face fades out (3 steps of 85 at 15 fps to one black step), detaches (`WX_DO_DETACH`: root hidden, the four baked canvases on the 12-byte placeholder; nothing on screen can read a half-painted buffer, whatever redraws in that window), bakes, applies, and fades back in over 3 steps, without replaying the arrival. This is M8's fade through black ([`face-design.md`](face-design.md)), except that the black lasts the bake. `wx_job_step` drives the opacity (`opa`) and `paint` applies it per object (background, thermometer, marker, number, end labels, sprites, sub-line), not as a root layer, so the fade needs no layer buffers; where a sprite overlaps the background the mid-fade blend differs a little from a group fade.
    - Partial bakes keep painting while queued and running (they write only back buffers). A parked partial job keeps the key and comes back as a partial job; a parked scene job clears it, so the next step bakes again and `apply` re-attaches (re-check 2 nit: a same-key paint on detached canvases).
    - Expected rate: scene at dawn and dusk (2/day) plus real cloud band moves (0 on a steady day, a few on a changing one; the 5 % hysteresis stops the flip at an edge) and data loss; ring when today's forecast range changes, on the new day and for each degree past the forecast min or max (0-10/day); hero for each 5 % illumination step (≤ 3/day near the quarters, the illumination moves up to ~12 %/day), moonrise and moonset. Before this change every one of these blacked the face, and `moon_illum` (1 % per fetch) re-baked every few hours, the full phase percent every ~7 h, and a cover near 10/40/70/90 % on every fetch.
    - Bake time: host (M-series, -O1) full 0.5-1.6 ms, ring ~0.2 ms, hero ~0.25 ms (`test_bake`). S3 full bake 0.51-0.54 s (0.9.61 log, 2026-10-12; the black between the fades), estimate tens of ms for ring or hero (invisible: the old picture stays). The log line `tempface: baked parts K ... in N ms` gives N on the knob.
    - Cost against full double-buffering: the ring strip and the hero back buffer add 125,048 B while the page shows; back buffers for everything would add 763,736 B (background 434,312, still clouds 201,312, hero 80,688, drift cloud 47,424), about 1.54 MB in all with the now-playing face's 434 KB picture held for life. Back buffers for the clouds alone (248,736 B) would still leave the background, so a scene re-bake would fade anyway.
  - **Tests** (`test_wx_job.c`, race points `WJ_RACE_POINT(j, n)` compiled out on the device): first bake and scene re-bake (fade out in 3 steps to black, detach, no paint while queued or running, fade in over 3 steps), ring and hero re-bakes (no detach, paint while queued and running, no fade), a scene change behind a running hero bake, a fade cancelled when the data goes back, a ring bake landing mid fade-in (keeps the fade-in's opacity; only a scene bake restarts it at 85), a parked partial job coming back partial and a parked scene job re-baking and re-attaching, a done job dropped across hide and show, hide while queued or running, the OTA hold, the allocation retry, and the two orders (key and kind before queued, for a scene and a ring job: a take at points 1-2 must carry the new key; time before done: a step at points 3-4). Mutants killed: `wx_job_released` without the idle store, queued published before the key, no busy check before paint, no detach on a re-bake, a park that keeps a queued job, a hold that does not park, no retry, done published before the time. `run.sh` greps that `temp_face.c` goes through the module: `wx_bake` only in `bake_task` (via `wx_job_take`, skipping an empty take, publishing with `wx_job_done`), no `atomic_` or job enum in `temp_face.c`, `s_job` in internal RAM, show/hold/update call `wx_job_show/hold/step` and paint only on `WX_DO_PAINT`, update keys off the shown band (`wx_temp_from_shown`) and paints at `s_job.opa`, `act` handles every action, the only `xTaskNotifyGive` is in `act`, `detach` hides the root and detaches the four baked canvases, the background attaches only in `apply`, and no bake pass (`wx_paint_clear/ring/rays/stars/thermo/hero/clouds`) runs in `temp_face.c`; `wx_ring_blit` runs only in `apply`, and `apply` shows the front hero buffer and hands the back one to the bake (`s_front ^= 1`, then `sprite(s_hero, s_hero_buf[s_front], ...)`, then `s_buf.hero = s_hero_buf[!s_front]`). The park/take race window exists only inside a load-then-store mutant, so race points cannot reach it: `run.sh` greps that `park` cancels with `atomic_compare_exchange_strong(&j->state, &queued, WX_JOB_IDLE)` and checks RUNNING with an `atomic_load` after it (no load before it, no store), and that `wx_job_take` claims with the queued → running CAS and nothing else. The three re-check 2 mutants (park load-then-store, take load-then-store, park reading the state before the CAS) each fail `run.sh`.
  - **OTA**: like `rim`, the bake is held under the OTA face: `ota_frame` calls `temp_face_hold(true)` on every OTA frame (after `pages_pause` hid the page) and `temp_face_hold(false)` when it resumes the pages. A queued job is parked (cancelled), no job or allocation starts while held, and if a bake was running when the page was hidden, the first OTA frame after it ends frees the ~900 KB (until 0.9.51's review they stayed allocated for the whole OTA). `run.sh` greps both calls. Host: the whole bake (background, hero, clouds) takes 0.5-1.5 ms per case on an M-series Mac at -O1 (`test_bake`; AT4 sun with one cloud is the slowest); the S3 figure comes from the log line on the knob (not measured yet).
- **PSRAM only while shown**: showing the page allocates the images (`acquire`), hiding frees them (`release`, at once, or on the next frame once a running bake ends). Every buffer is freed through `DROP`, which NULLs the pointer, and the canvases point at a 12-byte static placeholder while freed, so LVGL never holds a freed buffer. If an allocation fails, the log says `no PSRAM for the face (free, largest)`, everything allocated so far is freed and the page stays black; `wx_job_step` retries every `WX_JOB_RETRY_S` (5 s) on screen, and the next show tries at once (a knob with weather as its only page no longer stays black until a reboot). `run.sh` checks that `heap_caps_free` appears only in `DROP` and that `release` drops as many buffers as `acquire` allocates.
- **Burn-in**: as the old sky page did, the face root steps 2 px every 120 s on screen around a square (0,0 → 2,0 → 2,2 → 0,2) with `translate_x/y`, one full redraw per step; the baked images stay valid. `wx_shift_tick` (`wx_temp.c`) is host-tested; a non-finite or negative `dt` counts as 0, so one NaN frame no longer stops the shift for good (`temp_face_update` also clamps `dt` the same way for the arrival clock); `run.sh` checks that `temp_face_update` calls it and that the step moves `s_root`.
- **Areas/frame (estimate, not measured)**: arrival ≤ 6 (marker ~1.4k px, number ~300×107 = 32k px, mercury 3.2k px, hero 27k px, still clouds ≤ 67k px, drift cloud ≤ 16k px; hero and clouds only on the 15 fps steps, and LVGL joins overlapping boxes when that is smaller). Rule 1's ~4 ms per area was measured on opaque copies up to 12.8k px; the number and the cloud images are larger and blended, so the arrival rate needs the stats log on the knob before any fps claim. Then the sub-line fade (1 area, 3 steps). Steady state: 0 areas on a clear face; with clouds 1 area per drift step, the front cloud's box plus the 2 px step: one cloud 136×68 = 9.2k px, two 160×78 = 12.5k, four light grey 174×86 = 15.0k, overcast 210×76 = 16.0k px (the whole layer was 9.2k, 38.6k, 64.8k and 67.4k px). One full redraw every 120 s for the burn-in step.
- **Cost** (`idf.py size`, 0.9.50 at origin/main 62d370f → 0.9.51, same machine, IDF 5.5.5):

  | | 0.9.50 | 0.9.51 | Δ |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Image (`cinder.bin`) | 1,557,216 B | 1,595,232 B | +38,016 (the two face fonts ~28 KB; partial re-bakes +896) |
  | Flash `.text` / `.rodata` | 1,058,384 / 395,348 B | 1,066,084 / 425,676 B | +7,700 / +30,328 |
  | DIRAM static (`.data` / `.bss`) | 102,791 B (23,480 / 16,088) | 101,575 B (23,464 / 14,888) | −1,216 (the old sky scene state; `wx_job_t` +16) |
  | PSRAM `.bss` | 49,180 B | 49,636 B | +456 |
  | PSRAM heap, weather page shown | 164 KB (sprites 80 KB + canvas 84 KB, for life) | 903,330 B (background 434,312, still clouds 201,312, two hero buffers 161,376, drift cloud 47,424, ring strip 44,360, mercury 9,576, marker 3,888, LUT 1,082); back buffers only for the hero and the ring | about +721 KB while shown |
  | PSRAM heap, weather page hidden | 164 KB | 0 (plus the `wxbake` task, 4 KB stack + TCB) | about −160 KB |
  | `stack_walk.py` | `ember` 5,152, `lvgl` 8,592 | same; new `wxbake` 2,496 of 4,096 (margin 1,600) | 0 |

  Internal RAM goes down; one new task (`wxbake`, PSRAM), no timer or NVS key. PSRAM free at runtime was about 2.4 MB on 0.9.0 (bot page) and the now-playing face keeps its 434 KB picture for life and decodes another ~434 KB per track; not measured since. The face's ~900 KB come and go with the page, so two full-screen buffers coexist only while the weather page shows; a fragmented heap shows up as the `no PSRAM` log line (with the largest free block), not a crash.
- **Deviations from the canvas**: the sun's UV pulse and glow pulse, the heat shimmer (AT4), the star twinkle (AT5) and the frost sparkle (AT3) are left out (static); the hero and the clouds fade in instead of sliding in; only the front cloud drifts, the others stay still; face dots only for the built faces (two, 0.9.53, "Weather face switching"); the night sub-line is the sky word, not "low 6° by 06:00" (Ember sends no time of the low); the marker sweep uses ease-out cubic, not the canvas bezier (0.2, 0.8, 0.2, 1). While it rains, snows or storms the page opens on the Rain now face instead (next section); a turn switches faces.
- **Tests**: `test_temp.c` (colour scale against the canvas colours, UV bands, AT4/AT5/AT6-style selection and the overcast, rain and snow cases, legacy and missing fields, bake key, count-up values and timing against the AT4 strip, gauge and mercury geometry against the canvas, cloud boxes, ring/rays/thermometer/sun/moon phases/clouds/mercury/marker pixels, the moon rule for `up` true/false/missing/invalid, the burn-in steps and a NaN `dt`, the cloud split and its boxes, the full bake per case), `test_wx_job.c` (the bake job handoff above), `test_legacy.c` (the legacy poller's moon is unknown), `test_fixtures.c` (the four new fixtures, legacy views without detail, bad and missing detail keys, the cJSON hooks, `FEATURES_AHEAD` once per caps body), `test_caps.c` (six features), `run.sh` (hooks set before any other call in `app_main`, the face wiring above).
- **On the knob** (to do, within the live-device rules): open the weather page and compare with AT4/AT5 (snapshot); the bake log line per part (record N here); how a scene re-bake's fade through black and a ring or hero swap look; arrival smoothness and fps in the stats log, and the drift step's refresh time; `psram_free`, `psram_min` and `psram_largest` in the checkin after several now-playing track changes and weather page shows, against 0.9.50; internal heap; Ember's `effective_caps.features` lists `wx_faces` and the view carries the detail keys.

## Rain now face (#34 part 1, 0.9.51)

While it rains, snows or storms now, the weather page shows the Rain now face (canvas AR2, [`face-design.md`](face-design.md)) instead of the Temperature face; it brings back the precipitation animation the Temperature face removed. Same pipeline: `wx_job` bake task and handoff, images only while shown, OTA hold, burn-in shift, the M8 fade through black between the two faces when the weather changes (a turn cuts instead; next section). **Not checked on the knob yet.**

- **Data** (`parse_detail` in `knob_view.c`): `now.rain` (0-3), `today.pop`, `utc_offset` and the `rain` block (`until`, up to 4 `spells` `[start, end, intensity 1-3, kind 0/1]`). A key that is missing, not a number, not an integer (`now.rain` 0.5 is not rounded) or out of range is unknown; a bad spell is skipped, the others kept, and parsing stops at 4 valid spells.
- **Selection** (`components/weather/wx_rain.c`, pure, host-tested): off when the data is stale, older than 30 min or disabled (the Temperature face greys it, as before). Wet now: `now.rain > 0` or a spell covering now (`start` ≤ now < `end` on the knob's clock); with no detail at all (legacy view or poller), a rain or snow condition. A storm condition always counts. Once the knob's clock (observation age + time since receipt) reaches `until`, it is dry, unless a later spell starting at or after that `until` covers now (0.9.54; the sub-line then shows the word, never a past "until"). So `now.rain` 0 inside a spell (Ember's merged dry gap, "a pause, not the end") keeps the Rain now face: in showers the face no longer flips to the Temperature face and back (two fades through black) at every 15-min step. `view_faces_polar_dry.json` (condition rain, dry steps, no spells) shows the Temperature face.
  - Kind: storm condition → storm; snow or rain condition → that; any other condition with a wet step → the spell's kind (1 snow or sleet).
  - Intensity: the peak of the spell covering now, else `now.rain`, else the condition's (WMO code or MET symbol). Ember computes a spell's peak from the steps left in its last fetch (every 10 min), so the peak falls once a heavy part is past and can flip between moderate and heavy when a forecast near 2.5 mm/h is revised. So the face ratchets the intensity it shows up (`wx_rain_keep`, via `wx_temp_from_shown`): while it stays wet with the same kind, a falling peak does not re-bake and the shown intensity holds; a rising one re-bakes at the new peak, so a forecast flipping between moderate and heavy re-bakes once, on the rise, then holds heavy. Going dry and wet again, or a new kind, takes the new peak as it is. The word ("Heavy rain") follows the shown intensity; colours light #4F81B7, moderate #5C9CE0, heavy #3D7BF5 (design system).
  - Value: today's chance of rain (`today.pop`) on a 0-100 % gauge (AR2's ring from #2E3A48 to the intensity colour, end labels "0%" grey and "100%" in the colour); without `pop` (MET outside the Nordic area, old Ember) "--%" in one grey (#5A5A5A), a grey track, no marker, no end labels. The "%" takes the value's colour (grey without `pop`); AR2 still shows it #9A9A9A, changed after the user found it too dim on the knob.
  - Sub-line: "until HH:MM" (`until` + `utc_offset`) while wet, never for a dry storm (so never a past `until`); without either the word ("Light rain", "Heavy snow", "Storm"); offline the observation's age.
  - Re-bake (`wx_temp_bake_kind`): a change of face, kind or intensity (the shown peak: a rise, or a new spell after a dry gap) is a full scene (fade through black, as M8); `pop` appearing or going is a ring bake (no blink); the chance, `until`, temperature and clouds move only the marker and the text.
- **Rendering** (`wx_paint.c` bake, `main/temp_face.c` LVGL):
  - Background (RGB565, the shared 466² buffer): the umbrella (canopy with five scallops, black fill, 4.5 px orange stroke, tip, shaft stub, handle with the hook, passing behind the text as in AR2) and the ring.
  - Three drop sheets, the canvas's clips: left (58,150)-(118,332), right (348,150)-(402,332), the band above the canopy (150,46)-(316,132). Each sheet is one A8 strip of its width × 300 rows (A and B tiles of 150 rows, the canvas's drops, seamless at the wrap), shown twice (at offset − 300 and offset) in a clipping container and recoloured by LVGL (A8 path, no transform). The offset steps by 300/frames px per 15 fps frame: periods 2.667, 3.2 and 2.267 s with the canvas's delays (left 6, right 15, top 0 frames). A static A8 black ramp over the top and bottom 16 % of each box is the canvas's fade mask (over black, alpha × mask = black over the drops); above it a crop of the umbrella (RGB565A8) puts the canopy back over the drops, as the SVG order does.
  - Drips: one A8 sprite per pose (3 baked scales, no live transform), left every 24 frames from frame 5, right every 32 from 13, the canvas's 7 steps (dy 0..34, opacity). Splashes: 3 sprites on the canopy, 3 frames each from the frame the drop's tip reaches the canopy (frames 4, 31 and 17 of the top sheet, the canvas's).
  - LVGL calls: `wx_rain_ops` (pure) says which calls a frame needs against the last frame shown and their values (each strip's y, the drip's pose, y and fade-scaled opacity, the splash's y and opacity, the layer opacity, what is visible); `set_rain` only passes them on, and `run.sh` pins its body line for line. A drip or splash that appears gets its buffer, y and opacity whatever the cached frame holds (a hidden drip's cached dy is 0, so a delta alone left a new drip at the last fall's y for 2 of its 7 frames). During an M8 fade (face opacity < 255) the three sheet containers are hidden: drops, ramps and covers at partial opacity left the drops under the canopy showing through (~22 % at 170) and lightened the cover boxes; the rain comes back on the full-opacity frame, so after a fade the umbrella fades in dry and the drops pop in at full opacity (a trade-off against the show-through; check on the knob).
  - Intensity: light draws the level-1 drops (4/4/5 per 300 rows), moderate the canvas's set (6/6/8), heavy adds 4 per sheet (10/10/12); splashes only for drawn drops.
  - Snow (closest degrade with the design rules; AR2 is rain only): the same sheets with flakes (3 strokes, B2's #D0DCE8) at half speed, no drips or splashes. Storm: rain at its intensity plus B2's yellow bolt (#F0D040, 0.8 scale, 32×44 sprite in the top band), lit for 2 frames at 0.8, 1.2 and 1.6 s of every 6 s (3 flashes in 0.93 s, at WCAG 2.3.1's limit of 3 per second; the test pins the rhythm and at most 3 flash onsets in any 15 frames); no full-face flash.
  - Memory: the rain layers (strips 84,000 B, ramps 11,488, covers 31,884, drips 336, splashes 576, bolt 1,408: 129,692 B) are carved out of the still-cloud buffer (201,312 B), which a rain scene never uses; a face change is always a scene bake, so the canvases are detached while it is rewritten. `run.sh` checks the rain canvases are detached with the others and attached only in `rain_attach` (and the drip pose in `set_rain`), both from `s_buf.still`.
- **Areas/frame** (host, `test_rain.c`, over 2,400 frames per case): a host model applies `wx_rain_ops`'s values to modelled LVGL objects (buffer, y, opacity, hidden), as `set_rain` passes them, records the areas LVGL invalidates (old and new area of a visible object, clipped to its sheet) and joins them as `lv_refr_join_area` does; every frame composited from the replayed objects must equal the frame composited straight from `wx_rain_frame` (strips, drip and splash y, poses and opacities computed in the test, not taken from `wx_rain_ops`). Steady state 3 areas at 15 fps, every frame (each sheet moves each frame), 35,024 px redrawn (left 10,920, right 9,828, top 14,276); drips, splashes and the bolt stay inside their sheet's box, and no pixel changes outside the invalidated areas. Pixels that actually change per frame: light avg 749 (max 1,136), moderate 1,134 (1,396), heavy 1,765 (2,081), snow 967 (1,182), storm 1,811 (2,654). Ink per strip (moderate): 540 / 536 / 714 px. Arrival adds the marker, the counting number with its "%" and then the sub-line fade (≤ 6 areas, 30 fps steps). Rule 1's ~3-4 ms per area gives ~10-12 ms per rain frame (~15-18 % of the LVGL task at 15 fps); the S3 figure needs the stats log.
- **Bake time** (#95): host full rain bake 0.5-0.6 ms per case (`test_rain.c`, `test_umbrella.c`); it was 2.2-2.6 ms, and the S3 log showed 1.68-1.69 s against 0.16-0.54 s for the other faces. `wx_paint_umbrella` used to evaluate the handle, canopy (ellipse plus 5 scallops) and ferrule SDFs over the whole 264×306 box, 4 times per bake (bg and 3 covers). Now each part runs over its own box (handle strip, canopy band down to the rim, ferrule) as separate passes in the old per-pixel order (handle, canopy fill, outline, ferrule). The canopy skips a pixel at normalised outer-ellipse radius² ≥ 1.06² (`ellipse_d` ≥ ~6 px there, from `|∇f| ≤ 2ρ/r_min`), and fills one at ≤ 0.94² in rows ≥ 23 px above the rim (every `canopy_d` term ≤ −6.4, so fill coverage is 1 and outline 0) without the SDF. `wx_paint_drip` bisects once per sub-row instead of per sub-sample. Host parts after: umbrella bg 0.18 ms, covers 0.06, drips 0.01; the rest is the ring, dots and blit every face pays. Output is byte-identical on the host (`test_umbrella.c`); on the S3, FMA contraction can differ by an ulp in the SDF math, so there the claim is "no visual change", not bit identity. S3 after: not measured yet; the `tempface: baked parts ...` log gives it.
- **Cost** (`idf.py size`, #68 head d2c25bf → this branch, same machine and path length, IDF 5.5.5):

  | | #68 head | Rain now | Δ |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Image (`cinder.bin`) | 1,595,248 B | 1,609,872 B | +14,624 (+688 after the reviews: `wx_rain_ops` with its values, the text measure, the intensity ratchet) |
  | Flash `.text` / `.rodata` | 1,066,092 / 425,676 B | 1,079,556 / 426,844 B | +13,464 / +1,168 (`wx_paint.c` +5.2 KB, `temp_face.c` +3.4 KB, `wx_rain.c` 3.3 KB, `lv_obj_align_to` 1.6 KB) |
  | IRAM `.text` | 15,356 B | 15,356 B | 0 |
  | DIRAM static (`.data` / `.bss`) | 101,575 B (23,464 / 14,888) | 101,935 B (23,464 / 15,248) | +360 (the larger `wx_obs_t` copies in `weather_client.c`, 24 object pointers) |
  | PSRAM `.bss` | 49,636 B | 49,964 B | +328 (`shown_t`, layout) |
  | PSRAM heap, weather page shown | 903,330 B | 903,330 B | 0 (the rain layers live in the still-cloud buffer) |
  | Internal heap (LVGL objects, for life) | | 25 more objects (3 containers, 21 canvases, the "%" label) | estimate ~5-7 KB, not measured |
  | `stack_walk.py` | `lvgl` 8,592, `wxbake` 2,496, `ember` 5,152 | same | 0 |

- **Deviations from AR2**: snow and storm variants are mine (AR2 has rain only; built from B2's flake and bolt within the 3 boxes); intensity changes the drop count (the canvas has one density); the value is today's chance of precipitation (Ember has no hourly chance); face dots as on the Temperature face, no view dots (Rain forecast is not built); the strip offset is rounded to whole pixels (steps of 7-8, 6-7 and 8-9 px instead of 7.5, 6.25 and 8.8).
- **Tests**: `test_umbrella.c` (verbatim copies of the old brute-force umbrella and drip painters: byte-identical px on the full face bg, px and alpha on the full face RGBA and on the 3 cover boxes, each cleared and noise-filled, every drip pose, and the whole Rain now bake (bg and the still layout) for light, moderate, heavy, snow, storm and dry; prints the bake and reference times; `CINDER_BENCH=1` repeats one case (dry) 50 times and fails unless the bake is under half the reference), `test_rain.c` (selection: steps, pauses, spells with and without `now.rain`, spells against the knob's clock (not `observed_at`), the `start`/`end`/`until` boundaries, legacy condition and codes, storm without rain and its hidden `until`, stale/old/disabled, `until` passed on the knob's clock, local time with offsets and wrap; bake key across faces, showers inside a spell without a re-bake, a falling peak held while wet, a rising one re-baked (spell, `now.rain` and condition paths), a 2 ↔ 3 oscillation re-baking once then holding heavy, a new peak after a dry spell, a new kind; `wx_rain_ops`'s values after an attach and on a fade step (strips, drip pose, y and opacity, splash y and opacity, bolt); the LVGL-call replay for every kind and intensity from several start frames, with a fade-in and a re-attach; sheets: boxes, timing and drops against the canvas, offsets, loops, snow speed; strips: drops by intensity, the seam; splash hit frames against the canvas, drips, the bolt's pinned rhythm and flash rate; boxes; ramps; the bake: no sprites, ring colour, umbrella pixels, cover = background, ring bake leaves the rest, ring blit = full bake; per-frame areas and changed pixels; `CINDER_RAIN_PPM=dir` writes frames as PPM for a look), `test_wx_job.c` (temperature → rain → temperature through black, a chance change only moves the marker, `pop` going is a ring bake), `test_fixtures.c` (the new keys from the three face fixtures, bad keys, non-integer `now.rain`/`pop`/Unix times, `pop` 101 or a string, a fractional `utc_offset`, 9 spells with 3 bad: the first 4 valid kept). `run.sh`: no rain paint pass in `temp_face.c`, every rain canvas detached, attached only in `rain_attach`/`set_rain` from the still buffer, `apply` attaches on a rain scene, `paint` drives `set_rain` from `wx_rain_frame` at 15 fps with the baked rain while fading out, `set_rain`'s body matches line for line (each call behind its `wx_rain_ops` flag with its value) and it is the only place that shows the sheets, `rain_attach` resets the shown frame (all 0xFF) and opacity (-1), `face_kind` resets the text measure and `set_value` re-aligns the "%" on a width change.
- **On the knob** (to do): a rainy, snowy and stormy view (live, or the fixtures through a scratch server only with the user's OK); the stats log for the areas and ms per rain frame; the bake log; `psram_largest` after page switches; how the strip steps and the fade ramps look against AR2; the drops popping in at full opacity after an M8 fade (if it jars, show the strips on the last fade-in step at its opacity).

## Weather face switching (#72, 0.9.53)

User decision (2026-10-11): on the weather page a plain turn switches between the weather faces. The list holds the two built faces, Temperature and Rain now; the faces still to come (#34: Rain forecast, Wind, Next days, Sun, Air) plug into it. **Not checked on the knob yet.**

- **Input**: the weather page takes detents (`PAGE_TURN`, `page_weather_input` → `temp_face_turn`). Push and turn still changes the page (`knob_cb` sends a turn with the button down to `s_page_steps`, never to the page), swipes are unchanged, and the bot (gaze) and now-playing (volume) turns are routed as before. Pushes, long pushes and taps now reach the weather hook and are ignored there, as they were dropped before. `temp_face_update` runs before `pages_dispatch` in `page_tick`, so a turn starts the switch on the next frame.
- **Face list** (`components/weather/wx_faces.c`, pure, host-tested): `wx_face_id_t` is the list in turn order (`WXF_TEMP`, `WXF_RAIN`; a new face goes before `WXF_N` and needs its case in `wx_temp_face`'s `switch`, which has no default, so `-Wswitch` names a missing one). `wx_face_step` moves by the frame's detents and wraps both ways (several detents in one frame move several faces). `wx_faces_t` holds the pick: `WXF_AUTO` from each page open (`temp_face_show(true)` calls `wx_faces_open`), the face a turn moved to after that. A turn steps from `wx_faces_base`: the pick once the user turned; else the face of the last bake that landed (`wx_job_face`: `s_io.key.face` once baked and seen this visit, so a turn during M8's fade out, and in the black while a weather-driven scene bake runs, steps from the face last shown, not the one coming; a page reopened while a scene bake from the last visit still runs has nothing on screen, so its turn falls to the pick or `s_face`; a key that landed unseen exists only under a cut, which implies a pick, and the pick wins); else the face the page resolved last frame (`s_face`, resolved every visible frame even while PSRAM is short, and forgotten on open); else the first face. So two quick detents move two faces even while the first bake runs or the buffers are missing. A turn that nets to zero in one frame (a two-detent flick with two faces) changes nothing on screen but pins the face shown, so the page stops following the weather until the next open (intended: the user touched the faces).
- **Auto and pick** (`wx_temp_face` in `wx_temp.c`): `WXF_AUTO` is the old rule (Rain now while wet, else Temperature; `wx_temp_from_shown` now also sets `face`). Until the user turns, the face still follows the weather (rain starting while the page shows switches through M8's fade, as before). After a turn the picked face stays until the next page open, whatever the weather: Temperature picked while it rains shows the temperature (sky word "Rain"); Rain now picked while dry shows the dry Rain now face. No timeout.
- **Dry Rain now** (`wx_rain_dry`): the umbrella and the 0-100 % ring as AR2, in the light colour (#4F81B7, the ring from #2E3A48), today's chance (`today.pop`) as the value with its "%", sub-line "Dry now"; no drops, drips, splashes or bolt (`paint` keeps the sheets hidden: `set_rain(..., fo == 255 && !r->dry)`). Old, stale or disabled data or no data: "--%" grey, no marker, no end labels, no sub-line (offline: the observation's age, as on the other faces). A dry storm keeps the storm face (`wx_rain_from` is on for it). Rain starting or stopping on a picked Rain now face is a scene change (`dry` is part of `wx_rain_same_scene`), so the drops come or go through M8's fade; the dry face never takes part in the intensity ratchet (`wx_rain_keep`). The rain layers are still baked (light) into the still buffer and never shown: host bake 0.5-0.6 ms, the same as a wet light face.
- **Transition: a cut, not M8.** A face picked by a turn is a full scene bake like any face change, but `temp_face_turn` calls `wx_job_cut`, and the next idle `wx_job_step` with a scene change skips the fade out (it detaches at once, also in the middle of a fade out) and the fade in, and returns `WX_DO_ARRIVE`: `act` resets the arrival clock, so the new face arrives as on a page open (marker sweep, count-up, hero and clouds fading in, then the sub-line). So the screen is black for the bake only, then the arrival starts. Weather-driven face and scene changes keep M8's fade through black (3 steps out at 15 fps, about 0.2 s, the bake, then 3 steps in) and do not replay the arrival. Reasons: a turn wants an answer at once, and a fade out and in around every detent would make a spin through the faces sluggish; the arrival tells the faces apart and is the motion already budgeted for a page open.
  - A turn while a bake runs waits for it. When that bake lands with a cut pending to another scene, `wx_job_step` applies it without `WX_DO_PAINT`, so the root stays hidden and no outdated face flashes during a spin; the next step cuts. A ring or hero bake landing under a pending cut applies without a paint: the root is still visible, so the new ring, hero and end labels show for that one frame, while the value, mercury and opacity are not repainted; the next step detaches. The cut is dropped when the next idle step has no scene change: either the turns during a running bake ended on the face that lands, or the change is ring or hero only (the fade in keeps stepping). Turns in separate frames always start a bake, because the update runs before the dispatch every frame; a turn and a turn back within one frame net to zero and set no cut. A page open clears a pending cut.
  - Budget: the same as a page open, nothing new. No allocation: the face's 903,330 B of PSRAM are held while the page shows and a switch rewrites them in place while detached (as a scene re-bake does). One full redraw when the bake lands (the background), then the arrival (≤ 6 areas at 30 fps, estimate in "Temperature face") or the rain face's 3 areas at 15 fps plus the arrival.
